it is new, so restoring the 0103 six-argument body in part (2) does not remove it, and it reads `revision_visits.context_key`, which part (4) drops), `launch_context`, `sweep_completion_side_effects`, `record_learning_activity`, `journey_complete_enrollment`, `journey_complete_enrollments`, `journey_complete_enrollments_core`, `journey_credit_state`, `official_attempt_in_context`, `instance_pass_required`, `v_path_progress`, `v_path_step_status`, the two terms views, `v_context_attempt_summary`, `v_context_attempt_scoring`, `completion_notices`, `learning_path_completions`, then its trigger function `learning_path_completions_append_only()` (after the table, because the table's trigger depends on it and dropping the table drops only the trigger), and `context_derive_log`; (4) it drops the two triggers and their two trigger functions, the CHECKs and the context indexes, each with `if exists`, because 0108 may already have dropped the derive trigger, its function and `context_derive_log`; it recreates the 0062 and 0083 indexes, re-creates the two attempt foreign keys `on delete set null` (0034, 0058), and only then drops the generated and added columns; (5) it restores the table-level privileges of `org_api_keys`. Journey completions that the day-identity rule made, and enrolments it left active, are not recomputed by the reverse; staging is test data. In the same parts, for the preview and day-identity objects: part (1) also refuses while any preview row of a new kind, any `preview_runs` row or any soft-removed `journey_days` row exists (the owner purges or resets them first; test data); part (3) also drops `journey_save_days`, `journey_repin_enrollments`, `purge_preview_data`, `preview_purge_log`, then its trigger function `preview_purge_log_append_only()` (after the table, as for `learning_path_completions`); it does not drop `preview_runs`, because `course_attempts.preview_run_id` still references it there, which would stop the script with 2BP01; part (4) drops `preview_runs` as its last statement, after `preview_run_id` is gone; part (4) also drops the paste-window `day_ids` trigger and its function with `if exists`, re-creates `unique (enrollment_id, day_number)` on `journey_day_progress` before dropping `day_id`, restores `journey_days.day_number` to `not null` and drops `removed_at`, and drops `journey_versions.day_ids` and `day_ids_source`, `course_attempts.journey_day_id`, `preview_kind` and `preview_run_id`, and `attempt_requests.journey_day_id`. Part (1) also refuses, and prints the counts so the owner resets those staging rows first, while any (enrolment, day number) holds more than one `journey_day_progress` row (the old key that part (4) re-creates would fail with 23505; two credits of one enrolment can share a number after a move, which is why 0107 dropped it), and while any (learner, course) holds more than one request matching the 0083 predicate (the same failure for the index part (4) re-creates, once a learner has open requests in two contexts); part (2) restores the 0103 body of `consume_grant` and part (3) drops its day-id form and `journey_attempt_satisfies`; part (4) also drops `course_attempts.journey_version_id`. `ops/0109-reverse.sql` restores the 0087, 0099, 0090, 0073, 0031 and 0106 bodies, and restores **0107's** bodies of the two refresh functions, never the 0035 and 0056 ones: those would remove the completion sweep, the only retry of the fail-closed writer. A code rollback needs neither, because readers tolerate the new columns. Statements and points rows written with context keys stay.

### L. Edge cases

| Case | Behaviour | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| Same course on days 3 and 7 of one journey | Two official attempts, two scores, two day credits | `journey:E:<id of day 3>`, `journey:E:<id of day 7>` (D1) |
| Same course in paths P and Q | Two official attempts; P moves nothing in Q; points paid once | `path:P`, `path:Q`; the editor warns |
| Course held individually and in a path | Two statuses on the course page; a Library completion never unlocks a path step | `individual`, `path:P` |
| Retake by grant | Opens only in the grant's product; abandons and replaces only that context's attempt; history kept; in progress while live | Step 10 |
| Failed official attempt | Stands as that product's result. A path step never passed keeps later strict steps locked until a path grant. A failed required journey day blocks the journey until a journey grant | ADR 0017 per row; steps 44, 45 |
| Failed granted retake after an earlier pass (path) | Later steps stay unlocked; path progress and a stored completion are unaffected; the step shows its latest result and "passed earlier" | `passed_ever` (D14) |
| Course updated to a new version | Open attempts restart in every context (ADR 0011); grants re-issued in their own context; finished attempts untouched | Step 38 |
| Future release date | An Individual release gates only the Individual launch; a step release gates only that path step; a journey day opens by its own unlock mode | Step 22 (D2) |
| Missed journey days | Calendar catch-up as today; each caught-up day is its own attempt, marked late | Step 13 |
| Journey deadline edited | Every enrolled learner's deadline and overdue follow at once; snapshots keep earlier days' figures | Live read (D17) |
| Learner in all three products | Three open attempts at once; three bookmarks, results and report rows; one unique learner | The open-row index per context |
| Unenrol, then re-enrol (course or path) | The same history returns; "take again" is a grant | No run instances (D5) |
| Journey reset and re-enrol | A new enrolment and fresh contexts; old attempts stay with the old enrolment and leave every count | New enrolment id |
| Finished journey, learner reviews a day | A revision visit tagged to that day | Step 22 |
| Path edited after enrolment | An added step lowers steps done; a completed path stays completed | The stored completion |
| Journey republished to all enrolled learners (the owner's A27 answer) | A completed day is never asked for again; a started day stays resumable and its completion still credits it; a learner who has not completed a changed day gets the new version of it; no attempt or credit row is changed | Credit and context by day identity (steps 13, 93 to 95) |
| Republish: a day removed (its module cleared, the day removed, or `days_total` cut) | Completed: the credit is kept as history and counts towards nothing; nothing is retaken. Started: the attempt stays resumable from the journey page and can be finished; its result and a credit row are kept as history and count towards nothing (R13). Not started: the day is skipped. A shorter version whose required days are all credited completes at the publish; a learner who then still holds an open attempt on a removed day can resume and finish it in the completed enrolment, and its credit is written as history (steps 13, 22) | Step 95 |
| Republish: a day's course swapped | Completed: the credit stands; the day keeps showing the course the learner completed and its review opens that course; the new course is not offered to them. Started on the old course: the learner finishes the course they started, and that credits the day; the new course is not opened beside it (R13). Not started: the new course | Step 95 |
| Republish: a day inserted before the learner's current day | Completed days keep their credit under their new numbers. The started day, now one number later, stays resumable and credits on completion. The inserted day has no credit, so it becomes the open day and the learner goes back to it; in calendar mode it counts as behind at once (reading R15) | Step 95; `sent_back` |
| Republish: days reordered | Credits and open attempts follow their days. An uncredited day moved before the learner's furthest day becomes the open day | Step 95 |
| Republish: a required flag changed | A rest day that gains a module is a new required day with no credit. A mission day that becomes a rest day is a removed day. A per-day pass rule changed: a credit stands; a learner who started the day before the publish is credited when the result satisfies the rule of the version the attempt was launched under or the new rule, so a rule made stricter does not fail started work (R14; step 13); a rule relaxed on a day whose official attempt already satisfies it is credited at the publish. A course's own `pass_required` is not versioned: a change reaches every started and future journey day at once, with no publish (`0089:46-51`; disclosed) | Step 95; `credited_by_rule_change` |
| Republish: a completed enrolment and a new day | Never re-pinned; it stays completed, with its badge and certificate | The re-pin selects active enrolments only (step 47) |
| An attempt started before a republish and finished after it | Credited to its own day whatever the day's number or course is now; kept as history if the day was removed | The day-identity check of step 13 |
| Republish reaching an enrolment pinned to a version published before the new editor | Re-pinned only when the first changed day comes after the learner's furthest credited or started day; otherwise the enrolment stays on its version and is counted. Expected 0 on production | Step 94's gate; `kept_on_version` |
| Two last days of one enrolment are credited at the same moment | One completion, one badge, one claim; neither credit is missed | The enrolment row lock before the credit insert (step 13) |
| Republish moves a failed required day to another number | The day keeps its identity, its context and its failed result; the learner still needs a journey grant. A course swap on a failed day is the one different case. The new course has its first official attempt in that day's context; it is a different course, not a retake | The context key names the day id (step 5) |
| Preview or test run of a path or journey | Nothing official is written: no enrolment, credit, completion, points, badge, email, webhook, CRM row or tenant LRS statement. The run's progress is shown in Preview Reports from its preview attempts and stays until an admin deletes it by hand | Sections S and T |
| "Everyone in this org" product | Any active member finds it and self-enrols by pressing Start; then exactly as an assigned learner | Recorded enrolment, `source = 'self'` (D15) |
| Visibility switched back to Assigned only | Self-enrolled learners keep their enrolment and records; nobody new can self-enrol | The row exists |
| Staff member who also learns | Start is a real attempt; Preview is its own context; the two never collide or resume each other | `preview` in the open-row key (D7) |
| Path or journey program deleted with attempts | Refused, 409; archive instead | Foreign keys `no action`; step 40 |
| Forged `?lp=` or `?journey=` | Refused before any write | Step 22 |
| Completion side effects fail | No points, credit, webhook or email; the save succeeds; the sweep retries and the webhook then fires; no email | Steps 12, 16, 31, 32 |
| Result arrives in two requests (completed, then passed) | The completion branch runs again for the new state; nothing pays twice | `side_effects_sig` |
| Late save on an old completed attempt | No second payment, credit, webhook or email | Markers of step 7; the claim of step 15 |
| Gamification switched off | No points rows at all; emails, path completion and day credit still work | Steps 15, 35 |

The case asserts that behind a schema probe in the form of `has0098()` (`p0-helpers.ts`), so it passes on both schemas and is merged before the paste; spec 50 is in the nightly's default list (`scripts/lifecycle-nightly.sh:43`) and would turn red on the night after the paste otherwise. Second, in phase 2: the seed changes with it. Today course C is assigned to nobody and the admin's launch at `:85-86` is an unassigned launch. Phase 2 refuses it when course C is "Assigned only" ("You're not enrolled in this course", step 23), and then no attempt exists and the case's own precondition at `:114` fails before the delete; when the course is "Everyone in this org" the launch self-enrols the admin instead (step 24). The seed therefore assigns course C to the admin before the launch, so the case does not depend on the course's visibility. That a course holding only previews of the new kinds still deletes, after the route's purge and with its audit row, is spec 75's case (step 64) | 1, then 2 |

63. **PGlite rehearsal** (`scripts/migration-rehearsal/run.mjs`): scenarios `0105-0106-then-0107`, `0106-then-0105-then-0107` (the order production takes if an interim sitting pastes 0106 first), `0107-then-0108`, `0107-0108-then-0109`: four scenarios, entered in `expectations.json`. Files, in the house form of that folder: `grants-0107.sql`, `seed-0107.sql`, `assert-0107.sql`, `assert-0108.sql`, `assert-0109.sql`. **Grants first.** The rehearsal's shim gives the Supabase roles no grants and no default privileges (`scripts/migration-rehearsal/shim.sql:5`), so on it a write as `authenticated` raises 42501 before 0107 as well, and a view read as `authenticated` raises 42501 from the base tables instead of returning rows: both privilege checks below would pass or fail for the wrong reason. `grants-0107.sql` therefore runs before 0107 in all four scenarios, in the form of `asrole-grants.sql`: `grant usage on schema public to anon, authenticated`; `grant select on all tables in schema public to anon, authenticated`; and `grant insert, update, delete on public.org_api_keys to authenticated`, the table-level privileges the project defaults gave that table (step 8). Each privilege assertion then has a control that shows it can fail. **`assert-0100.sql` is not run after 0107.** The folder's house form runs earlier assert files again after a later migration (the 0106 scenarios run `assert-0100.sql` again), and that form is not copied here: in the four new scenarios `assert-0100.sql` runs only where the parent scenario runs it, before 0107. Two of its checks describe the 0100 guards, which step 7 replaces: its body check (0) reads the hint text "is_preview since 0100" (`assert-0100.sql:137-145`), and its case (8) expects a version whose only attempt is an `is_preview` row to delete (`:536-615`), while after 0107 the derive trigger labels such an insert `legacy_role` and the recreated guard refuses the delete. The guard cases of `assert-0107.sql` (below) stand in their place after 0107. The other earlier assert files (`assert-0103.sql` to `assert-0106.sql`) were not read for this. Before any of them is run again after 0107, the executor checks it against what 0107 recreates (the guards, and the course enrolment view of 0105, step 62) and runs again only the files that still describe the database. Seed: learners I (Individual only), P (path only), B (both), J (a journey with one course on two days), O (an "Everyone" path, self-enrolled), S (a staff member who also learns), a completed row of every kind from before the paste, a completed SCORM row, a tagged preview row, an orphaned journey-day row, a deleted-path row. Assert: the second paste changes nothing, proved on rows the new code could have written: between the first and the second paste the rehearsal inserts a completed row with `side_effects_sig` null (a failed writer call) and a completed SCORM row with `completion_webhook_at` null and `webhook_skip_reason` null (a held webhook), and after the second paste the first is still a sweep candidate and the second carries no marker; the derive trigger's firing is recorded in `context_derive_log`, and 0108 refuses within 24 hours of a firing and passes after it; Q2 refuses the orphan and passes without it; old completed rows carry `side_effects_sig = settle_sig` and SCORM rows the `pre_0107` marker; the CHECK refuses a both-tag insert and a preview without `is_preview`, and admits the old tagged preview; the derive trigger labels a tagless insert, and after 0108 that insert fails; three open rows coexist in three contexts, a preview and a real attempt coexist, and a second open row in one context raises 23505; the immutability trigger raises for the service role; `consume_grant` in `path:P` leaves the Library row open, judges `stands` against P only, and refuses a grant whose context differs from the launch; the six-name `record_revision_visit` call in SQL named notation resolves with both signatures present (PostgREST's own overload choice is not testable here: the rehearsal runs no PostgREST, so the six-name and seven-name `.rpc()` calls are a case of spec 68 on staging); the writer runs the activity branch on an incomplete row, runs the completion branch again when `completed` becomes `passed`, pays nothing twice, skips previews and revision rows and never touches a stamped row; the sweep finds no row from before the paste and survives one raising row; `journey_record_completion` credits only journey rows, credits a started attempt to its own day after a re-pin moved the day or swapped its course, writes a history credit for a removed day, sets `late`; `journey_complete_enrollment` answers true on the first call and false on a second, sequential call (PGlite is one connection, so two concurrent callers are a case of spec 69 on staging); `journey_complete_enrollments` returns each completed enrolment once and takes one claim each; the day-identity rule after a re-pin (A27), on enrolments re-pinned by `journey_repin_enrollments` after the draft was edited through `journey_save_days`, each with a learner who completed days 1 to 3 and holds an open attempt on day 4: (a) a day inserted at number 2: the three credits and the open attempt keep their day ids, `next_day` is the inserted day, the open attempt's day is in `resumable_day_ids`, its completion credits its own day, and no attempt or progress row changed between before and after (compared column by column); (b) two days swapped: credits follow their days and no credited day is `next_day`; (c) the course on day 4 swapped: the open attempt completes and credits day 4, and no second attempt can be opened in that context while it is open; (d) day 4 removed: the open attempt completes, a credit row is written, `credited_days` does not count it, and the enrolment completes when the remaining required days are credited; (e) the new version is shorter and every required day is credited: the function completes it at once with one badge and one claim, `completed_at` set; (f) a completed enrolment is not re-pinned; (g) an enrolment pinned to a `slot_backfill` version with a change at or below its furthest day is kept on its version and counted, and one with the change after it is re-pinned; (h) a per-day pass rule relaxed on a failed day credits it at the publish; `journey_save_days` moves two rows in one call without a unique violation and never leaves an active row without a number; the backfilled `day_ids` of a seeded version equal the `journey_days` ids by (program, number), and Q8 refuses a version day with no `journey_days` row; in all of these, `journey_reminder_counts.done` and `journey_credit_state.credited_days` agree (`assert-0107.sql`), 0109's `journey_enrollment_state` agrees with both (`assert-0109.sql`, since the function does not exist before 0109), and the parity check runs the same fixtures through `computeJourneyState`; the path views and the terms views, read as `authenticated`, return the member's own organisation's rows (the control: more than 0, and no 42501) and 0 rows for another organisation; on `org_api_keys`, as a super owner's `authenticated` session, an update of `name` succeeds before 0107 (the control: the seeded table privilege and the policy are in force) and, after 0107, an update naming `contract_version` or `name` raises 42501, while an insert through the granted columns and an update of `is_active` still succeed; the key inserted that way reads `contract_version = 2` and the seeded key from before the paste reads 1; the file is created in the order at the head of section B (the first paste, on a database at 0106 where none of 0107's objects exist yet, is the test); a claim taken by `journey_complete_enrollments` reads `origin = 'publish'` and a claim taken through the writer reads `origin = 'learner'`; `launch_context` returns, for learners P, O and J, the same facts as direct selects on the seed: `sequence_mode`, each step's `step_number`, `release_at` and `done`, the self-enrolment row, and for J the `start_date`, `unlock_mode`, `count_sundays`, the timezone, `credited_days` and `next_day`, and it returns no enrolment facts for a learner of another organisation; `v_context_attempt_summary` has one row per (course, learner, `context_key`) and keeps a learner whose only rows are reset attempts, with `live_attempt_count` 0; the writer's path completion agrees with `v_path_step_status` on the D14 fixture; one claim per (learner, product, instance) in `completion_notices`, with gamification off as well; points under D8(c): learner B completes C at 0.95 individually, then at 1.0 in P, then at 1.0 in Q → `complete:B:C` and `high:B:C` paid from the Individual row, `perfect:B:C` paid once from the P row, the 0-point event rows for each context, nothing paid from Q; `learning_path_completions` unique per (path, learner), and a failed retake after a pass neither removes nor blocks it; a lone path delete raises 23503 while the organisation and user cascades succeed; both refresh functions answer with every `error` null; the privilege matrix returns 42501 for `anon`; `v_journey_progress` is unreadable by `authenticated`; `v_course_attempt_summary` keeps one row per (course, learner); the parity checks.

    **The purge rehearsal (the proof that no official row is touched).** The seed gains: a course preview, a path test run and a journey test run of learner S with statements, State documents, launch tokens and learning sessions, some dated two years ago and some new; `legacy_role` rows (a completed one flagged by the 0100 backfill, one that is the target of a used grant, one with an `xp_events` row and a `journey_day_progress` row pointing at it, and one with no result that nothing points at); an `lrs_forward_outbox` row naming a new-kind preview attempt; and every official row kind of the seed above. The seed keeps its old and new rows, and age changes nothing. Every real call of the rehearsal first makes the dry run of the same scope and passes that dry run's `selection_hash`; this holds for every case below that deletes. The lettered cases: (R6-a) after both refresh functions and the completion sweep have run, every preview row, every test run and `preview_purge_log` are unchanged, however old the rows are, and no function of 0107 to 0109 other than `purge_preview_data` holds a `delete` on `course_attempts` or `preview_runs`; (R6-b) a manual call with the organisation scope deletes every new-kind preview attempt and run of that organisation, old and new alike, with their statements, State, tokens, sessions and outbox rows, and no by-role row; (R6-c) a null `p_org`, a null `p_actor` and a reason other than `manual` or `product_delete` each raise and delete nothing; (R6-d) the audit row holds the actor's id, the time, the count per table and the total, and the deleted ids, and equals the dry run's summary (nothing writes between the two calls in the rehearsal; the hash fixes the selected attempts and runs, not the counts of their child rows, step 81); (R6-e) a real call with a `p_expect_hash` that differs from its own selection raises and deletes nothing, and so does a real call with a null `p_expect_hash` (22023); a dry run with a null hash answers; a preview inserted between a dry run and its real call makes that real call raise; (R6-f) withdrawn on 2026-11-13 (round 7): the id-list scope; see Review; (R6-g) withdrawn on 2026-11-13 (round 7): a by-role scope; see Review; (R6-h) withdrawn on 2026-11-13 (round 7): an id list that names by-role rows; see Review. No scope and no reason selects a `legacy_role` row: after every call of this rehearsal every seeded by-role row is still in place, the one that nothing points at included. Assert: (1) a dry run returns the counts and changes nothing (row counts of every table equal before and after; no log row). (2) Withdrawn on 2026-11-13 (round 6): an automatic deletion case; see Review. R6-a and R6-b stand in its place. (3) A manual run by scope (one run; one tester; one course; the organisation) deletes exactly that scope. (4) After every run, for every table of the database, the count and a checksum over the primary keys of rows that are not new-kind preview data equal the values taken before the run; this is the case that proves no official row and no `legacy_role` row is touched. (5) When a points row, a day credit, a notice, a path completion, a used grant or a revision visit points at a selected preview attempt (seeded on purpose), that attempt is never deleted: with one such attempt and one clean attempt in one organisation, the clean one is deleted, the other stays with every row that points at it, the log row names it under `excluded` with the table, and the answer reports it (step 81). (6) One `preview_purge_log` row per run that deleted or excluded something, holding ids and counts only. (7) As `authenticated` and `anon`, calling the function raises 42501. (8) The organisation and user cascades still delete a tester's test runs. (9) A second paste of 0107 changes no `preview_kind`. (10) Withdrawn on 2026-11-13 (round 6): the settings table; see Review. (11) Withdrawn on 2026-11-13 (round 6): the restore run; see Review. (12) A run with reason `product_delete` and a course, path or program scope deletes that product's preview data of the new kinds and its outbox rows and writes its log row; a course whose only attempts were previews of the new kinds can then be deleted and leaves no `preview_runs` row without a parent; with one seeded breach on a course, on a path and on a program, the dry run with the product's scope answers `excluded` 1 for each, and the real run leaves the breaching attempt, its run and every row that points at it in place. (13) An outbox row is never written for a preview by the abandon path: not in SQL's reach, so it is a case of `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` and spec 75.

    **Further re-pin and day cases** (`assert-0107.sql`): the dry-run counts for a draft, passed as the would-be version, equal the counts the real run returns after the publish, on the fixtures (a) to (h); a first paste, then a day removed through `journey_save_days`, then a second paste, passes (Q8 only counts, A19's equality is skipped); the new version is shorter, every remaining required day is credited and the learner holds an open attempt on the removed day: the enrolment completes at the re-pin, the open attempt then completes, and `journey_record_completion` writes a history credit on the completed enrolment with no second badge or claim; a day's pass rule made stricter by a publish does not fail an attempt launched under the earlier version (`journey_version_id`), and an attempt with a null `journey_version_id` is judged by the pinned version; `consume_grant` called with a day id that differs from the grant's answers `unavailable` and writes nothing, and its old form still resolves a number; every function of the file has a `search_path` entry in `pg_proc.proconfig`; the reverse script refuses while two credits of one enrolment share a day number.

    **Further purge, index and guard cases.** Purge scope: each key of step 81 alone deletes exactly its scope (one attempt; one run; one tester; a tester with one course, path or program); an unknown key, an empty or null scope, a key with a null value, two product keys, a null organisation, and reason `product_delete` without exactly one product key each raise 22023 and delete nothing. A null actor, an unknown reason, and a real call with a wrong or a null `p_expect_hash` each raise and delete nothing (R6-c, R6-e); a scope that names a list of ids or a by-role key is an unknown key and raises the same way. By-role rows (reading R26): no `legacy_role` row is selected by any scope, the scopes for one preview, one run, one tester, one product and the organisation included, nor by reason `product_delete` on a course, a path or a program; case (4)'s checksum covers every row that is not new-kind preview data. The test-run row: `done_activity_day_ids` is empty on every row after the first paste, a path run with a non-empty list is refused by the CHECK, and a journey run's list is gone with the run after its purge. Append-only: an update or delete of a `preview_purge_log` row raises for the service role. Indexes: the seventeen of step 7 exist. The delete guard: with a session claim that is not the service role, a delete of a `course_packages` row over a version that holds a learner attempt raises `learner_history_exists` through the cascade, and with the service-role claim it does not (the control, and the reason the route must not use that client; step 85). The guards and by-role rows (step 7), on a course, a version and a language seeded for this case alone, each holding one `legacy_role` row and no other attempt: with a session claim that is not the service role, a delete of the `courses` row, of the `course_versions` row and of the `course_packages` row each raises `learner_history_exists` (the language through the cascade), and the row and the product stay; the same three deletes over one learner attempt still raise (the control that the new clause did not stop counting official rows, whose `preview_kind` is null); over only a preview of the kind `course` the three deletes succeed by cascade, and a course whose new-kind previews the product-delete purge of case (12) removed first deletes with nothing left to cascade; with the service-role claim the delete of a course that holds only a by-role row succeeds (the control: the path the test purges take is unchanged). A second paste of 0107 leaves both guard bodies as the first paste wrote them. `consume_grant`: the eight-argument form and the nine-argument form, both give the same answers on one journey grant; the lock order itself cannot be shown on one connection, so it is asserted on the wrapper's stored body (its first locking statement names `attempt_requests`), and two parallel launches of one granted journey day are a case of spec 68 on staging. The reverse script runs to its end on a database that holds `preview_runs` and `preview_run_id` (part (4) drops the table last). The re-pin's relaxed-rule credits are equal between a dry run from a draft and the real run, with the draft's rule passed to `journey_attempt_satisfies`.

64. **Lifecycle specs** (the lifecycle namespace; by hand on staging, never while another harness runs; then the nightly list after one green run).

    |---|---|

    | 69 `journey-contexts` | A course on two days; re-run after reset; review of a credited day and of a completed journey; several active journeys; a failed required day → blocked → grant → credited; **a deadline edit moves enrolled learners' overdue status**; publish with `apply_to=all` completes through the one function, sends no email and takes one claim per enrolment; two concurrent completions of one enrolment make one completion, one badge and one claim; **two different last days credited at the same moment leave the enrolment completed, with both rows, one badge and one claim (the launch never opens two uncredited days at once, so the case is built from two fixture attempts on the two days, completed by two parallel commit requests)**; **a republish to all enrolled learners (A27), built with the new editor on a learner who completed three days and has one day open: a day inserted before the current day, two days swapped, the open day's course swapped, a day removed. In each: no completed day is offered again; the open attempt is resumed from the journey page with its bookmark and its completion credits its day; the inserted day is the open day; the attempt and credit rows read before the publish are unchanged after it; the learner completes the remaining days and the enrolment completes with its badge, claim and email; the page, journey admin and the CRM `days[]` show the same credited count. A stale link with the old day number does not open another day's attempt. A publish-made completion shows on the dashboard card, the journey page, journey admin and the CRM feed with its date, and sends no email (A20)**; a republish that moves a failed day keeps its failed result and opens no new official attempt without a grant; a started day that the publish removed, swapped or moved past the calendar ceiling is launched from the journey page and is not refused by the access check (`?denied=1`); a shorter version completes the enrolment while a removed day's attempt is open, and that attempt is then resumed and finished, with a history credit and no second email; after a course swap a credited day shows the completed course, its MOD code and score on the journey page, in journey admin and in the CRM `days[]`, its review opens that course, and the new course is not offered; a day's pass rule made stricter by the publish does not fail an attempt started before it; the publish confirmation's dry-run counts equal the counts the publish then reports |
    | 70 `rewards-per-context` | `xp_events` re-reads: one paid completion per course, one event row per context, nothing for journeys or a second open path; a fresh learner per run (the ledger is append-only) |
    | 71 `overview-dashboard` | A fresh organisation, a forced refresh, tiles equal the product reports' totals; unique learners; a snapshot row per product and `all`; an analyst opens the Overview and the three drill-downs and not the write forms or feedback; no row of any product table changes |
    | 72 `crm-v2` | Version 1 strict and version 2 shapes on both endpoints; the `path_id` filter in version 1 and in version 2; a `context` on every record (path and journey objects, each day, course rows in version 2; steps through their path); `steps[]` and `days[]` with MOD codes and `passed_ever`; an unreleased step is listed with `released: false` and its `release_at` (A29); a key created after 0107 answers version 2 and an older key version 1 (A28); `enrollment_id` on `learner-summary`; context targets; the webhook's context and scope |
    | 73 `product-edges` | Release dates per product; a missed day; a course update in three contexts; self-enrolment in an "Everyone" journey, and the course and path cases of spec 68 again through the learner pages; no reminder email for a self-enrolled learner (decision A22); CSVs per product; feedback per product |
    | 75 `preview-isolation` (74 is phase G's) | An admin, a super owner and an analyst each press Preview on a course and complete it: the score shows in Preview Reports; no points row, streak change, day credit, path completion, notice, email, webhook, CRM row, leaderboard or report row appears for them, and with tenant LRS forwarding on in the test organisation no outbox row is written. The same three press Start: a real attempt with a score (R6). A path test run through a strict path: step 2 is locked until step 1 is done in the run, a failed step blocks, a release date opens when the simulated date passes it, the run page offers the next step and the launch bar has no Continue (A21). A journey test run: day 2 opens when the simulated date moves on, with no enrolment row, no `journey_day_progress` row and no badge. Manual delete: the dry-run counts, then the delete, then Preview Reports is empty for that scope and one log row exists. No automatic deletion: a preview row and a test run aged two years by fixture are still there after the nightly function and the completion sweep have run, and no audit row was written. An official attempt of the same learner on the same course is unchanged by id and value after every delete. A learner (`user`) cannot open Preview Reports, a test run or the purge route. With forwarding on, a per-version restart over an open preview row, a course-update forced restart and a Preview launch that closes an open `legacy_role` row each write no outbox row; the admin's and the analyst's own rows in Learner Analytics and the Manager Report Card (attempt count, last activity, last active) are the same before and after their previews; a path, a journey program and a course that hold only test runs or previews of the new kinds are deleted through their routes, each after its dialog shows the dry-run summary, with one `product_delete` log row each, and one that also holds a learner attempt answers 409 with its preview data untouched; a course, a path and a program that each hold one seeded breaching preview are refused by their routes with the "kept for review" 409, and the breaching attempt and the product stay; a course delete by a non-admin purges nothing; a language that holds only a course preview is refused with the preview message and deletes after the manual purge, and one that holds a `legacy_role` row is refused with the "kept" message (step 85); the refresh function's answer on staging carries no `preview_purge` entry, and with one seeded breach the manual delete's summary and its audit row report the excluded attempt. The manual delete cases of step 99 are part of this spec. An analyst cannot delete: the delete option is absent and the route answers 403 with nothing deleted, for their own previews as for anyone's (reading R25). No by-role row is ever removed, by the nightly function or by an admin: each is listed read only under "Kept earlier staff attempts", has no delete option, and is still there after a delete of all the organisation's preview data (reading R26). A path and a program held only by a `legacy_role` row with an old tag answer 409 and keep everything; a non-admin calling the path delete and the program delete is refused and no purge runs; a language with a learner attempt answers today's 409; (a purge call that errors cannot be staged through the routes, which build the scope themselves, so the 503 rule is proven by `product-delete-order.test.ts` and not here); a course that holds a by-role row is refused by the course delete with the "kept" message, and the row and the course stay; a delete made straight through the Data API on an admin's own session, of a course and of a version that hold only a by-role row, is refused with `learner_history_exists` (step 7); two parallel launches of one granted journey day leave one retake and no deadlock error (spec 68 carries this one) |
    | changed | 50 (the case "staff-only attempts do not block", in phase 1 and again in phase 2; step 62), 46 (fixture), 53, 55, 60, 61, 63 (D14; no Continue on a preview), 64 (rewritten for the Individual-only view), 65 (the per-product feedback case); 32 (its fixture, and the language switch inside one context); 34 (its fixtures, and `by_role`); 45 and 58 (fixtures); `tests/e2e/attempts/consume-grant.spec.ts` (its fixture, and the grant's context); the teardowns of step 40 |

    **Fixtures that insert attempts.** These insert `course_attempts` rows with the service role and name no `context_kind` today: specs 32 (`:96`), 34 (`:69-70`, `:298-299`), 45 (`:112-113`), 46 (`:132`, `:217`), 58 (`:611`), 60 (`:226`, `:231`), 65 (`:213-214`) and `tests/e2e/attempts/consume-grant.spec.ts:88-89`. (Spec 55's two inserts at `:374-376` and `:400-402` are a learner's session and are meant to be refused; they are not fixtures and do not change.) Specs 32 and 34 are in the nightly's default list (`scripts/lifecycle-nightly.sh:43`), and the e2e job of `pr-checks.yml` runs every pull request against the staging database. After 0108 each of these inserts fails `not null`. They change to name `context_kind`, through one helper per harness (`tests/bot/lifecycle/p0-helpers.ts`, `tests/e2e/helpers/supabase.ts`), in the first pull request after the 0107 staging paste, never before it: an insert that names the column fails on a database without it. The same pull request widens `attempt-insert-sites.test.ts` to `tests/` and `scripts/`. It is a precondition of the 0108 paste (step 20).

    **Fixtures that build journeys without `journey_days` rows.** Two specs insert a `journey_versions` row and no `journey_days` row: spec 55's journey setup (`:264-283`, a version with one day, then a real `?journey=...&day=1` launch) and spec 46 (`:128` a version with `days: []`, `:132` an attempt with `journey_day: 3` and a path tag as well, `:208` a progress row). Spec 55 is in the nightly's default list (`scripts/lifecycle-nightly.sh:43`) and lifecycle data lives 48 hours (`:38-40`), so as they stand Q8 would refuse the staging 0107 paste on any day, and from the paste on the derive trigger would raise on spec 55's launch: the nightly would turn red at 0107, not at 0108. They change in two pull requests. Before the staging 0107 paste, because it works on both schemas: each inserts a `journey_days` row per day it uses, before its `journey_versions` row, so the paste-window trigger can fill `day_ids`, and spec 46's attempt carries one tag only (Q2 refuses a row with both); the same pull request carries spec 50's staff-only delete case behind its schema probe (step 62), for the same reason: it is in the nightly's list and would turn red at the paste. After the paste, with the fixtures above: spec 46 names `context_kind`, `journey_day_id` and `day_id`; it need not name `journey_version_id`, which the scan requires only of `app/` and `lib/` (step 23). Spec 55's two refused inserts (`:374-376`, `:400-402`) stay as they are.

65. **Acceptance criteria.**
    1. Learner R (an example learner) holds course C individually, in path P and on day 12 of journey E. He completes C from the Library: Individual status passed; P's step not started; day 12 not credited, with no `journey_day_progress` row.
    2. He completes C from P: a second attempt with `context_key = 'path:P'`; P's step done; the Individual result unchanged by id and value; day 12 still not credited.
    3. He completes C from day 12: a third attempt with `context_key = 'journey:E:<the id of day 12>'`; the day credited from that attempt; the other two results unchanged.
    4. Points: exactly one paid `complete:R:C`; 0-point event rows for `individual` and `path:P`; nothing for the journey.
    5. Each of these shows R once with its own product's status: course Reports, course learners and CSV, path Reports, path Learners and CSV, the path index, journey admin, the journey CSV, Organisation Reports, Learner Analytics, the Manager Report Card, the course page and the dashboard.
    6. The Overview shows 1 unique learner, 3 enrolments, 3 completions, 0 failed, 0 overdue, 0 not started; refreshing changes no product row; an analyst opens all three drill-downs.
    7. A `?lp=Q` launch for a path R is not enrolled in is refused; a plain launch of a course he holds only through P sends him into P.
    8. A path grant opens a retake that abandons only P's open attempt; the Individual result stands; the Overview shows P in progress while the retake is live. If that retake fails after an earlier pass, later steps stay unlocked, the path stays completed if it was, and reports and the CRM show the latest result with `passed_ever` true.
    9. An admin enrolled in E previews C: no day credited, no points, no attempt that counts. Her Start launch then opens a real record. An enrolled analyst's Start launch is a real attempt with a score.
    10. A failed required day 12 shows E as failed on the Overview and in journey admin; a journey grant and a pass credit the day.
    11. Emails: one course email when the Individual course is successfully completed, on SCORM and on cmi5, once per learner and course; one path email when the whole path completes; one journey email when the learner completes the whole journey (the one exception, decision A20: a journey completed by an admin's publish to all enrolled learners sends none); none for steps, days, revision visits, previews or granted retakes that complete again.
    12. CRM version 1 keeps today's fields and reads the Individual product strictly; version 2 shows three product objects; every record carries a context, its own or its parent's (step 55); the webhook carries the context and respects the scope; `path_id` returns only those paths' learners and records, in version 1 and in version 2.
    13. A future-dated Individual assignment hides the Library card while `?lp=P` still works; a future-dated step refuses `?lp=P` while the Library launch still works.
    14. A course update restarts open attempts in every context and re-issues grants in their own context; finished attempts are unchanged.
    15. A learner who presses Start on an "Everyone in this org" course, path or journey has a recorded enrolment before the first attempt and appears in that product's reports, the Overview and the CRM feed. A member who never starts is not counted.
    16. Editing a journey's deadline changes every enrolled learner's overdue status at once in journey admin and after the next refresh on the Overview.
    17. Feedback R gives for C in P does not change his Individual feedback for C; each product's report shows its own.
    18. Every before- and after-check of 0107, 0108 and 0109 is green on staging; the grants check is green; the nightly list is green two nights running.
    19. After a publish to all enrolled learners that removes a day, swaps a day's course, inserts a day before the learner's current day or reorders days (A27, in the owner's four sentences): no day the learner completed is required again; a day the learner started is resumed with its progress and its completion credits it, also when the publish removed it, swapped its course, moved it past the calendar ceiling or completed the enrolment over it, and a pass rule the publish made stricter does not fail it; a learner who has not completed a changed day gets the new version of it; every attempt and credit row that existed before the publish is unchanged after it. Every journey surface shows the same credited count and the same open day, and completing the remaining required days completes the enrolment once, with one badge, one claim and one email.
    20. A journey that an admin's publish completes shows as completed, with its date, on the learner's dashboard card, the journey page, the certificate, journey admin, Learner Analytics, the Report Card, the Overview and the CRM feeds, with day figures that agree, and sends no email (A20).
    21. A Preview or a test run by an admin, a super owner or an analyst writes nothing official anywhere: no points, badge, streak, day credit, path completion, enrolment, email, webhook, CRM row, report row or tenant LRS statement. Its scores and its path or journey progress show only in Preview Reports. An authorised admin can delete it at any time, after a dry-run summary and a confirmation, with an audit row (who, when, how many); nothing deletes it automatically, however old it is; and no official row changes by id or value when it is deleted (R7).
    22. A tester can walk a whole strict path and a whole calendar journey in a test run, meeting the same locks, pass rules and unlock dates a learner meets, without waiting for a date and without a Continue button in the launch bar (A21). Until phase G this covers course days, which are the only kind that exists; from phase G a run also passes an activity day by marking it done inside the run (decision A41), and the criterion then covers a journey with activity days.
    23. A language switch made in one product closes only that product's open attempt; the closed row, its data and its statements are still in the database; no completed result changes; an audit row names what was closed (A24).

### N. Security, scale and performance

66. **RLS and grants.** Every new relation has RLS on, a policy and literal grants in its file; service-only tables carry an explicit no-access policy, because `tests/rls-audit/audit.sql:247-248` fails any table with `organization_id`, RLS on and no policy, and that halts production deploys; a table with RLS off raises the cron audit's critical alert. Matviews stay revoked from `authenticated`. Journey progress is service role only. `anon` gets nothing. The context trigger binds every role.
67. **Forgery.** The context comes only from the launch link and is checked against recorded enrolment, activity, release and lock before anything is written; a failed read refuses. Without this, per-context official attempts could be minted by URL.
68. **Self-enrolment and the service role.** The enrolment write of step 24 and the route of step 48 run with the service role only after the session, active membership and visibility checks, and write only the caller's own row in the caller's organisation.
69. **Privacy.** New columns hold ids, day numbers, flags and scores. `completion_notices` holds ids and times, no address and no name. No name or email is added anywhere. Logs carry attempt ids only.
70. **Workers free plan (50 subrequests, 10 ms CPU, both environments).** Estimates, counted as ADR 0019 counts them (middleware 1, learner layout about 12, `requireOrgAccess` about 5); the launch page was not measured for this record.
    - Launch: the page has 47 `.from(` or `.rpc(` call sites today, of which one request runs a subset. This record did not count the worst branch. The independent check read today's call sites on a learner's first strict `?lp=` launch and came to about 31 page reads on top of the 18 fixed ones, about 49; that is an upper reading, because it does not remove branches that cannot run together, and it was not recounted here. Either way, nothing shows today's base is under 35, so a target of 40 cannot be met by adding reads to it. Planned work, not a contingency: `launch_context` (step 18) is built in phase 1 and wired in phase 2, and replaces the access read, the entitlement reads, the release read and the strict lock's reads (eight call sites today, `launch:597-725`) with one call. A self-enrolling learner adds the insert and one more call (2). Target: the worst path (a self-enrolling learner's first strict-path launch that consumes a grant) at or under 40 of 50. **A measured count on a local build, branch by branch as ADR 0019 does it, is a gate of phase 2** and is recorded under this record; if it is still over 40, the phase does not ship until further reads have moved into the function.
    - Course page chooser: one read of the learner's path and journey holdings of the course (2 at most), from rows the page mostly reads already. The course page's whole request is measured with the chooser in phase 6 and recorded the same way.
    - Publish with `apply_to=all`: one set-based call replaces today's loop of a count read, an update and an insert per enrolment (`app/api/journey/program/route.ts:152-171`), and the route sends no email (step 47), so its count no longer grows with the number of enrolments.
    - Completion routes: one writer call replaces two or three calls. One email costs 5 to 6 subrequests (`lib/notifications/send.ts:155-163`, `:203`, `:245`, `:270`, `:291`); a single attempt has one context, so at most one email per request. The statements route gains that cost on the completing request only. The commit route awaits its send inline today; that is unchanged and disclosed.
    - Overview: three reads per page view. `learner-summary`: the per-enrolment count becomes one batched read before per-day rows are added.
71. **Refresh cost and growth.** The 15-minute set and the nightly set are timed separately; a timed run on a seeded dataset of about 6 million attempts gates the 0109 paste. The repository has no script, seed or place for that run today (the rehearsal is PGlite on a small seed). It is built in phase 7 as `scripts/refresh-timing/` (a seed generator and a timer), and it runs on a throwaway Postgres on the executor's machine or a CI service container, never on the shared staging database, where it would slow every harness, and never on production. The hardware is recorded beside the figure, because local timing is not the hosted database's timing; the first refresh on staging after the 0109 paste is timed as well and recorded with it. Attempts grow by one row per product a learner actually launches. `product_metrics_daily` grows with organisations, days and instances; a journey counts once per program.

### O. Phases, effort and gates

72. The core has no feature flag: two decision rules on one table for the life of a flag would recreate the drift this record removes, and the schema change is one transaction. Safety comes from production being empty, the paste-first order on staging and the rehearsal. Flagged: the Overview (`PRODUCT_OVERVIEW`), the CRM version (per key), journey activities (`JOURNEY_ACTIVITIES`), Preview Reports and test runs (`PREVIEW_REPORTS`, step 84).

    **A departure from `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 6, which only the owner can allow (decision A19).** Principle 6 says code is deploy-safe in both orders and tolerates a missing column. This record keeps it for every reader, cron and feed: they tolerate the absent objects of 0107 and 0109 (the fallbacks of steps 31, 49 and 53, and the hold sweep's select, which falls back to today's select when `webhook_skip_reason` is missing). It does not keep it for these write paths from phase 2 on: (1) the launch insert, which names `context_kind`; (2) the two completion routes, which call `record_learning_activity` and read an error as "not official"; (3) the journey publish route with `apply_to=all`, which calls `journey_repin_enrollments` (steps 47, 94), and the curriculum save, which calls `journey_save_days` (step 92); (4) every other write from phase 2 on that names a 0107 column or calls a 0107 function, among them the self-enrolment insert of step 24 (`source`), the journey self-start (D15), the revision visit in its context (step 11), grant requests, approvals and `consume_grant` per context (phase 4) and the completion-notice stamp (step 15). On a database without 0107 a launch would fail, a completion would silently earn no points, credit, webhook or email, and a publish to all enrolled learners would re-pin and complete nobody. ADR 0014, the precedent, kept the principle with a narrower-select fallback. The departure is allowed for these write paths, with this rule in its place: **on every environment 0107 is pasted before a commit that contains phase 2 serves a launch there.** On staging that is the order of step 73. On production it is the sitting of step 74; a tag cut from `main` between the phase 2 merge and that sitting is allowed only while the first-learner gate of step 74a is closed, because then nothing can be launched or completed there and no enrolment exists for a publish to complete, and the owner is told so before the tag. Two admin actions need no learner and fail on a database without 0107: saving a journey curriculum (`journey_save_days`) and pressing Preview (the insert names `preview_kind`). In that window nobody edits a journey curriculum or presses Preview on production (production has no course to preview), and the owner is told before the tag that these two actions fail until the sitting. The alternative keeps the principle: the launch insert retries without the context columns on 42703, the routes fall back to today's classify-then-call path when the function is missing (PGRST202), and on PGRST202 the publish route keeps today's count loop. It costs about 2 more days for (1) to (3), the publish route's fallback included; fallbacks for the writes under (4) would add more and are not priced here. It keeps alive, behind a schema test, the fail-open path this record removes. The owner allowed the departure on 2026-11-13 (A19: "**Allow** the required database migration (0107) to be applied before the new application code starts serving launches that depend on it"), with four preconditions "before the new code serves real learners": staging validation, a production backup, migration verification and safe deployment checks. They are gates in steps 73, 74 and 74a. The alternative's fallbacks are not built.

What still needs the owner's word is the acceptance of this record, with the one open question on by-role rows in section "Decisions requested" (the answer built here is "no": they are never deleted). A reading marked "object if wrong" holds nothing: after acceptance its phase starts unless an objection is on record, and an objection must come before the phase named for it below, because after that the reading is built.

By the owner's decision of 2026-11-13: a successful restore rehearsal in an isolated environment, made with the release candidate's migration files and recorded (step 98). Without it the sitting does not start.

The owner's sitting (step 74): a push freeze on every branch, no workflow in progress other than production's short scheduled crons and the tag's own deploy, no staging harness running, each paste in the minutes step 74 names; then 0105 and 0106 if not yet pasted, 0107, the tag, 0108, 0109, with every before- and after-check recorded (the third precondition, migration verification) and the deploy checks of step 74 (the fourth).

What a first production Preview needs is in items 2 to 7: the manual delete screen (step 99) is in the release, so an admin can delete from the first Preview on. The content backup condition of step 74a still comes before the first course, as `docs/release/v1.4.0.md` requires.

    **What blocks production's first release and first learner: phases 1 to 7, 9a and P, 86 days by the table, so plan for about 129; then 9b, 2.5 more (plan for about 4), before the first learner. The owner chose this release on 2026-11-13 (R8, not A4).** The release and the first learner are bound by different things:
    - The release is bound by D3 together with a choice of this record. D3 puts 0109 in the same sitting as 0107. This record writes 0109 in phase 7, with the report code that reads it, and proves it on staging there. So the sitting cannot happen before phase 7 is proven, and the tag cut then carries phases 1 to 7. The owner's words fix the sitting; placing 0109 in phase 7 is the architect's plan, and it is what turns D3 into a later release.
    - The first learner is bound by D11: Yoddha reads version 2 before production's first learner. Version 2 is built in 9a, so 9a must be on production before the gate of step 74a opens. It is put in the same release.
    - The first learner is also bound by the owner's sentence of 2026-11-11, that "the Continue button, chapter lock/unlock logic, Learning Path progress, dashboards and reports must all follow the same path-specific completion rule". The dashboards are phase 6 and the reports are phase 7, so both must be on production before the first learner. This binds the first learner, not the release.

    Phases 1 to 4 are still built and proven first, on staging. Recorded enrolment on Start for courses and paths sits inside them (phases 1 and 2), because the views read only enrolment rows: without it an "Everyone" learner would be invisible or refused. The journey half of D15 sits in phase 6. The Overview (phase 8, behind its flag) and journey activities (phase G) do not block the release. The conversation with Yoddha opens at acceptance and runs alongside phases 1 to 4, because Yoddha's own move to version 2 is the part this team does not control.

    An earlier release was offered and is not built: phases 1 to 4 and 9a in a first release, with 0109 pasted in a second sitting when phase 7 is ready. It departs from D3 as accepted, and until phases 6 and 7 followed, the product chooser would be missing and path reports and the leaderboards would not follow the path rule (step 59). The owner declined it on 2026-11-13: "I will go with your recommendation, **R8**, rather than the faster A4 option."

    Every pull request is independently reviewed and pushed under the owner's standing approval for non-production branches. The owner merges, deploys staging and pastes.

### P. Deploy and paste order

73. **Staging.** The owner runs the Q2 and Q8 counts and resets any orphaned journey rows. Before the paste, the fixtures pull request that gives specs 46 and 55 their `journey_days` rows is merged (step 64), and the lifecycle-namespace organisations made by earlier runs are deleted, or the nightly is paused for 48 hours so its cleanup removes them: a journey fixture built without `journey_days` rows would make Q8 refuse the paste. Pull request 1 (0107, 0108, the rehearsal, the pure modules, fallback-safe readers) merges and deploys to both staging deployments. The owner pastes 0107 in a window clear of everything else that uses the staging database: the pg_cron refresh at 03:30 UTC, the lifecycle nightly from 22:41 UTC (about 45 to 80 minutes), the nightly e2e at 04:00 UTC, any pull request's e2e job and any hand-run harness; the before- and after-checks are recorded under this record. (The :03 and :07 offsets are production's crons, `.github/workflows/cron.yml`, and belong to step 74.) Staging has no database backup and needs none: it holds test data (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 5), so a failed paste is met by the re-paste rule of step 4 and the reverse script of step 61, not by a restore. Phases 2 to 4 follow. The owner pastes 0108 once the preconditions of step 20 hold (phase 2 on both staging deployments, the insert-sites guard green over the test folders, the nightly and the e2e job green on fixtures that name `context_kind`, and no firing in `context_derive_log` for 24 hours), and 0109 with phase 7. A re-paste of 0107 on staging stays possible until 0109 and changes no value the new code wrote (step 4).

    **Staging validation (the first of the owner's A19 preconditions; reading R19).** Before the production sitting: every phase the tag carries has passed its gate on staging; 0107, 0108 and 0109 are pasted there with every before- and after-check green and recorded; the rehearsal is green for every scenario; specs 68, 69, 70, 72 and 75, the cases of spec 73 that phases 6 and 7 build (its journey self-enrolment case, which is phase 6's gate, and its CSV and feedback cases, which phase 7 builds), and the changed specs have passed by hand and joined the nightly list; and the lifecycle nightly is green on the release candidate. Spec 71 needs the Overview page and stays phase 8's gate; the whole of spec 73 and the two green nights stay 9b's gate; neither phase is in the release, so "specs 68 to 73" as a whole could not be met by the release's own phases. Specs are run "by hand on staging, never while another harness runs": that rule is this record's (step 64) and ADR 0014's and ADR 0019's, not `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 9, which states only the `qa-*` purge rule. The nightly job has a 75-minute timeout against about 40 to 50 minutes today (`.github/workflows/lifecycle-nightly.yml:60-61`); before the first new spec joins the list (in phase 2, where the change is priced) the timeout is raised or the list is split into two jobs that run one after the other, never side by side on one database, because a timeout is a red night and restarts the two-night count of step 74a.
74. **Production** (owner-run, explained beforehand, never the Supabase CLI). Production holds 0100, 0103 and 0104 on top of 0099 and neither 0105 nor 0106. D0 keeps 0105 out of any sitting before this one. An earlier sitting may paste 0106 and tag the code already on `main`; that code was planned to run before 0105.

    **Gates before the first paste (the owner's A19 preconditions, 2026-11-13).** The sitting does not start until each is recorded in the release runbook of phase 9a.
    - *Staging validation*: step 73's list, complete, on the release candidate. The tag is cut from that commit, or from one that differs from it only in documents.
    - *Production backup*: an on-demand run of the database backup workflow on `main`, green, started within the hour before the first paste, with its steps Dump database, Encrypt with GPG (required) and Upload to Backblaze B2 all successful and no artifact published. The scheduled nightly alone does not count: its 02:00 UTC schedule in fact starts between about 08:00 and 09:30 UTC. This is the practice of the v1.4.0 sitting (`docs/release/v1.4.0.md`). What the facts show today (run history read on 2026-11-12, ids and conclusions only): the production database backup is working; all 31 runs since 2026-10-18 10:55 UTC are green, the latest scheduled one on 2026-11-12; the five red runs of 2026-10-14 to 2026-10-18 came before the workflow was hardened. So the precondition can be met as practised and nothing needs repair first. "Verified" means what is proven: a green run, and the owner's restore drill (download, decrypt, completeness by counts), which last printed `RESULT OK` on 2026-10-18 and is repeated before this sitting. Its limit, stated: a restore of a dump into a database project has never been rehearsed; production is on the free tier with no point-in-time recovery and no vendor-side backups; the disaster path is a restore of the pre-sitting dump into a fresh project, which loses every write made after the dump. Production has no learners and no courses at the sitting, which is what makes that limit acceptable there.
    - *Restore rehearsal (the owner, 2026-11-13: "mandatory before the production sitting ... in an isolated environment, with the restore process and verification documented")*: the rehearsal of step 98 has passed, with the release candidate's migration files, and its record is in the release runbook. This closes the limit stated in the bullet above ("a restore of a dump into a database project has never been rehearsed") before the sitting, not after it. A rehearsal that failed, or one made before the candidate's files existed, does not open the gate.
    - *A freeze*: no push to any branch and no harness running on staging, so every parallel stream pauses for it. Workflows, as practised at the v1.4.0 sitting ("no workflow running" cannot hold): production's scheduled crons keep firing, every 5 and every 15 minutes (`.github/workflows/cron.yml:22-31`), and the tag's own deploy is the one workflow the sitting starts; before each paste the owner checks that no other run is in progress.
    - *Paste minutes on production* (`docs/release/v1.4.0.md:92`): clear of 02:00, 03:00, 03:30 and 06:00 UTC, of the hour's :00, and of the quarter-hour offsets :03, :07, :18, :22, :33, :37, :48 and :52. Scheduled runs start late, so this lowers the chance of a lock wait and does not remove it: each file sets `lock_timeout = '5s'`, and on a timeout nothing changed, so the owner waits a minute and pastes the same file again.
    - *Counts*: Q8 to Q10 read 0 on production; a count above 0 goes to the owner before 0107 is pasted (step 4).

    **Migration verification (the third precondition).** Each file's before-checks are read before its paste and its after-checks after it, in the SQL editor, and each result is written into the runbook before the next file. A failed check stops the sitting at that file. Nothing is repaired by hand in the sitting.

    **Safe deployment checks (the fourth precondition).** Before the tag: the RLS audit and `check-migration-grants` are green on the tag's commit; no `cron.yml` line names a route this release adds (it adds none; principle 6); the notice addendum and Yoddha's agreement are in hand. After the tag: the production deploy workflow is green; the health check answers 200, and the only entries it reports as degraded are the stale-cron ones that were degraded before the sitting (production health is expected to read `degraded` while GitHub starves the short crons, `docs/release/v1.4.0.md:442`, `:498`; a new degraded entry stops the sitting's sign-off); the `refresh-report-views` heartbeat reads `ok` after its next 03:30 UTC run, with the refresh answer's `completion_sweep` entry present and its `error` null, and no `preview_purge` entry in the answer (nothing deletes Preview data on a schedule); a new CRM key reads version 2 and an old one version 1 (A17); launch-side checks are recorded as not performed while production has no courses. A code rollback is a re-deploy of the previous tag and needs no reverse migration, because readers tolerate the new columns (step 61). The first-learner gate of step 74a stays closed through all of this: the new code serves no real learner until that gate opens.

    Then, in one sitting, in the order the owner accepted under D3:
    1. 0105, if not yet pasted, with Q0 to Q8 before and A1 to A8 after (A8 recorded as not performed; ADR 0020 step 9).
    2. 0106, if not yet pasted, with Q0 to Q3 before and A1 and A3 to A5 after (ADR 0021 I.2).
    3. 0107, with Q1 to Q10 before and A1 to A24 after. Every count reads 0.
    4. The release tag. It carries phases 1 to 7, 9a and P (step 72 says why; P by reading R12), with the notice addendum and Yoddha's agreement in hand. The deploy must be green.
    5. 0108. Its `context_derive_log` check passes because nothing was launched.
    6. 0109, in the same sitting, with its before- and after-checks.

    The one order, stated the same way in the notes on ADR 0020 and ADR 0021: 0105; then 0106 if not yet pasted; then 0107. 0106 may instead have been pasted in an earlier sitting; it is never pasted before 0105 inside this one.

    0105 is pasted, not skipped: it carries the Custom Group helpers, the release gates and `v_path_enrollments_expanded`, which 0107 needs. 0107 follows it in the same sitting and removes its path arm, so the state "path learners in course reports" never serves a request on production.

    **74a. The first-learner gate.** Production has no learners and no courses today, so the release and the first learner are two separate events. After the sitting, production still gets no learner: no course, path or journey is assigned to anyone, and none is set to "Everyone in this org", until all of these are recorded as a dated note under this record: (1) the sitting of step 74 is complete and the deploy is green; (2) phase 9b's staging proof is done, with the nightly green two nights; (3) Yoddha reads contract version 2: their key is a version 2 key and their written confirmation is on record; (4) withdrawn on 2026-11-13 (round 6): a Preview retention gate; see Review. Nothing about Preview data is a condition; the number is kept so that references to condition (5) stay right; (5) the content backup is green and its restore drill has passed from `main` (`docs/release/v1.4.0.md` already requires this before the first course; it has been green since 2026-11-11, and a drill passed on 2026-11-10 from a fix branch). A version 2 key is a key created in the settings page after the sitting; it reads 2 by the column default (step 8, decision A28), so Yoddha is issued a new key and nobody runs SQL on production for it. Their old key keeps reading version 1 until it is revoked. The owner opens the gate. If (3) cannot be met, R4 applies: option (c) goes back to the owner, and the gate stays closed until the owner decides. The gate is a procedure in the production runbook, not code. The runbook is written in phase 9a, because the sitting it describes comes before 9b; the dated note that records the four remaining conditions and opens the gate is written in 9b.
75. **Standing never-paste rules, kept and extended.** Never paste 0098 after 0100. Never paste 0073, 0081, 0090, 0092, 0095 or 0099 after 0103. After 0107: the list of step 4. After 0109: the list of step 21. Never push a `v*` tag as a bookmark.

Principle 3 ("Learner history is never rewritten or deleted") is departed from for preview data only: rows written by a Preview launch or a test run are deleted when an authorised admin deletes them by hand, never automatically (section S, decision A36, the owner's third message of 2026-11-13); rows flagged as previews by role before this record (`legacy_role`) are preserved and the delete function never selects them; no other learner row is ever deleted or rewritten. Principle 5 ("No test or seed data on production") is departed from for a Preview or a test run on production: they are the owner's R7 testing data, kept out of every official record by their context. This is the owner's decision ("I agree to allow Preview and test runs on production, as an exception to the no-test-data-on-production rule", 2026-11-13), with the owner's conditions: separate from official data and without any official effect, identifiable, open to authorised users only. The fourth condition of that answer, "deleted within the 90-day maximum", was withdrawn by the owner's third message. Stated plainly: Preview data now stays on production with no time limit, until an admin deletes it, which is a wider exception to principle 5 than the one the owner first allowed; the owner is asked to confirm it at acceptance (section "Decisions requested"). It is an exception for Preview and test runs only, and no test fixture, seed row or test-learner account is allowed on production by it. The principle 3 departure is likewise the owner's decision (A36), for preview and test data only. Phase 9b writes all three into the file. No other principle is touched. Section 4 is not overridden.

### S. Preview data and Preview Reports (the owner's R7)

There is no section R, so that "R7" always names the owner's item. The owner's R7 answer and third message of 2026-11-13 settle the open question O-1 of ADR 0006 note (r): Preview results are kept, apart, until an authorised admin deletes them by hand, and count nowhere. Nothing deletes them automatically.

78. **What a preview is, from phase 2.** A row is a preview only when its launch was started with Preview or from a test run; never by role (R6). `preview_kind` says which: `course` (Preview of one course), `path_run` or `journey_run` (a launch inside a test run, section T), or `legacy_role` (flagged by role before this record; step 82). Every preview row has `context_kind = 'preview'`, is excluded by the one official rule in SQL and TypeScript, and is skipped by the writer (step 12). **Launch mode (decision A32; the owner, 2026-11-13: "I agree that Preview should run in normal course mode and store scores and results. However, all Preview data must remain completely isolated from official learner data, reports, analytics, progress, rewards and completion records.").** The owner asks Preview Reports for "Preview scores and results" and for path and journey test progress. Today a preview launches in Browse mode (ADR 0006 step 17), and a KIVO 2.0 package in Browse sends only `initialized` and `terminated` (ADR 0006 note (r)): it stores no score and no completion, so its previews would show nothing and a strict test run could never unlock its second step. So a preview launches in `Normal` mode: the module behaves exactly as it does for a learner and the result lands on the preview row; what keeps it from being official is the row's context, enforced by the database, not the module's behaviour. This changes ADR 0006 step 17 for previews and closes O-1 without a hold. The Browse alternative is not built. What a KIVO 2.0 package sends in Normal mode on a preview row was not run for this record; it is the same launch a learner gets, and spec 75 proves it on staging.

    **How a preview launch behaves (the `canManage` short-circuit of `launch:318` is gone, step 23, so this is stated).** The one-attempt rule does not apply to previews: a preview has no official attempt, no revision visit, no grant and no window. A Preview launch resumes the open preview row of its own key and kind, if there is one, and otherwise inserts a new row; after a preview is completed, the next Preview of that course is a new row, never a revision row. `evaluate()` is not run for a preview launch, and `contextResult` is never asked whether a preview is "official". When a step or day is taken more than once in a test run, the run's state reads the latest completed preview row of that step or day in the run, and Preview Reports list the earlier ones under it. An open `legacy_role` row is never resumed by the new code, although it shares the plain key `preview`: the launch looks its row up by key and `preview_kind`, closes an open `legacy_role` row of that version it meets (status `abandoned`, conditional on in progress, as the launch's other closes do; a close is not a change of context; unlike those closes it sends no "abandoned" statement to a tenant LRS, step 80) and inserts the new row, so new preview data never lands on a row the purge cannot select.

79. **0107: the preview objects** (creation order: the head of section B).
    - `preview_runs` (new table, preview only; decision A31): `id uuid primary key`, `organization_id` (cascade), `user_id` (the tester; cascade), `kind text not null check (kind in ('path','journey'))`, `learning_path_id uuid`, `journey_program_id uuid`, `journey_version_id uuid` (each `on delete no action`), `simulated_date date` (the date the run's unlock rules read; null means today), `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`, and a CHECK that a path run names only the path and a journey run only the program and the version, and that a path run's `done_activity_day_ids` is empty; `done_activity_day_ids uuid[] not null default '{}'` (decision A41: the activity days a tester marked done inside a journey run; empty and unused until phase G; it is the run's own state, lives and is purged with the run row, and is the one piece of test-run progress that is not derived from an attempt, because an activity day has no attempt). It holds ids, a kind and dates: no result and, apart from that list, no progress. It is the one row a test run writes outside `course_attempts`; it is not an enrolment, and no official reader may read it (step 86). RLS on with an explicit no-access policy; `revoke all on public.preview_runs from public, anon, authenticated`; `grant select, insert, update on public.preview_runs to service_role`. No delete is granted: rows leave only through the purge function and the organisation and user cascades.
    - `course_attempts.preview_kind text check (preview_kind in ('legacy_role','course','path_run','journey_run'))`, with `(preview_kind is not null) = (context_kind = 'preview')`. Backfill (once, step 4): `legacy_role` on every row with `is_preview`. `course_attempts.preview_run_id uuid references preview_runs(id) on delete no action`. Both are in the shape CHECK, the key and the immutability trigger (steps 5 and 7).
    - No settings table: nothing is deleted by age, so 0107 stores no retention figure and no switch, and no retention page is built.
    - `preview_purge_log` (append-only, in the `xp_events` trigger form; its trigger function is `preview_purge_log_append_only()`, plpgsql, `set search_path = pg_catalog, public`, modelled on `learning_path_completions_append_only()`, in the creation order's item 3, the reverse script's drop list and the `search_path` check of step 19). This is the owner's audit record: who, when, how many.
