Columns: `id`, `organization_id` (cascade), `ran_at timestamptz not null default now()` (when), `reason text not null check (reason in ('manual','product_delete'))`, `actor_user_id uuid not null` (who: the id of the signed-in admin who confirmed the deletion; no foreign key, so the row outlives the account), `scope jsonb` (ids only), `counts jsonb not null` (how many: one number per table, the cascaded tables included, and their total), `attempt_ids uuid[]`, `run_ids uuid[]` (the deleted ids, kept so that the record says exactly what a deletion removed and an admin can repeat it by hand after a restore, step 97), `excluded jsonb` (the attempts the call refused to delete, each with the table that points at it; step 81). Ids and counts only: no score, no name, no email. RLS on, no-access policy, `grant select` to `service_role`; inserted only by the delete function. There is no scheduled run, so no reason for one and no row without a named person.
    - `purge_preview_data(p_org uuid, p_scope jsonb, p_reason text, p_actor uuid, p_dry_run boolean, p_expect_hash text default null) returns jsonb` (the last argument is the `selection_hash` of the dry run the admin confirmed, step 81): plpgsql, `security definer`, `revoke execute ... from public, anon, authenticated`, `grant execute ... to service_role` (step 81). It raises on a null `p_org` or a null `p_actor`: every call is for one organisation and names the person who started it. A dry run needs no hash, which is why the argument has a default; a real call (`p_dry_run = false`) with a null `p_expect_hash` raises 22023 and deletes nothing, so the function itself accepts no deletion that does not match a dry run.

80. **Isolation by construction: every store, how a preview is kept out of it, and how it is purged.** "Today" is the code at `main` 1aeb339, where nothing but two delete guards and one staging-only view reads the preview flag, so a completed preview can earn points and a streak, credit a journey day, send the SCORM course and path emails, fire the CRM webhook and be forwarded to a tenant LRS.

    | Store | Today | From this record | Purge |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `course_attempts` (with `cmi_data`, score, status) | An ordinary attempt with a flag | Its own context; excluded by the one rule; the only place a preview's result lives | Deleted by the purge function |
    | `xapi_statements`, `xapi_state`, `cmi5_launch_tokens`, `learning_sessions` | Written per attempt | Unchanged: they are the preview's own data, keyed by its attempt | Cascade from the attempt (`0004:11`, `:30`, `:46`; `0100:386`) |
    | `preview_runs` | Does not exist | Ids and dates of a test run | Deleted by the delete function when an admin deletes the run, when its last attempt goes in the same call, or by a wider scope (step 81); never by age |
    | `xp_events`, `user_gamification` (points, streak), badges, monthly recognitions | Written for a preview, even one that never completes (daily activity) | Never written: the writer skips previews (step 12) and the wrapper returns early (step 34) | Nothing to purge for new previews. The table is append-only for every role (`0053:129-146`); rows written for `legacy_role` previews cannot be deleted (step 82) |
    | `journey_day_progress`, `journey_enrollments`, `learning_path_completions`, `course_assignments`, `learning_path_assignments` | A staff preview can credit a day and complete an enrolment | Never written: `journey_record_completion` requires a journey context (step 13); a test run has no enrolment and writes no credit or completion; Preview never self-enrols (step 24 runs only without the mode) | Nothing to purge |
    | `completion_notices`, `notification_log`, sent emails | SCORM previews send the course email and can send the path email | No claim, no send, no log row (steps 12, 32) | Nothing to purge. Emails already sent are outside the system |
    | CRM webhook and the CRM feeds | The webhook fires; the feeds count the row | The routes take "official" from the writer, which skips previews; the feeds read non-preview contexts only | Nothing to purge. A message already sent is outside the system |
    | `attempt_requests`, `revision_visits` | An analyst's preview can consume a grant | A request in a preview context is refused (step 37); a preview records no visit and is never a revision row | Nothing to purge |
    | `lrs_forward_outbox` and the tenant LRS | Every statement and the sweeper's derived statements are forwarded, with no preview marker; the LMS cannot delete from a tenant LRS | Never forwarded (decision A35): `mirrorToExternalLrs` (`lib/lrs/enqueue.ts:69-102`) and every sweeper source that reads attempts (`lib/lrs/sweep.ts`) exclude `context_kind = 'preview'`, "Resend all history" included. So does the third path, `enqueueAbandonedStatements` (`lib/lrs/abandoned.ts:34-60`), which reads attempts by id with no context filter today and is called by every abandon site: the per-version restart (`.../restart/route.ts:233`), the course-update forced restart (`lib/attempts/force-restart.ts:731`), the launch's closes (`launch:789`, `:809`, `:942`, `:992`) and the language switch (`language-preference/route.ts:259`). Its select drops preview rows, `legacy_role` ones included, so a restart over an open preview and the close of an open `legacy_role` row (step 78) send no "abandoned" statement, which would carry the tester's identity to a store the LMS cannot delete from | The purge deletes outbox rows by `attempt_id` explicitly (the column has no foreign key, `0044:30`); expected 0 |
    | Report matviews, leaderboards, question analytics, `report_card_cache`, the Overview | Count the row | Built on the official rule or on enrolments; a preview is in neither | Nothing to purge |
    | Raw TypeScript attempt readers on official surfaces: Learner Analytics' tally and last activity, the Report Card's "last active" | Count every row that is not a revision visit, previews included (`analytics/page.tsx:1008-1022`; `lib/manager/insights.ts:507-515`) | They drop `context_kind = 'preview'` (step 49's row), and the source scan of step 62 fails on a new reader that does not | Nothing to purge |
    | Audit rows written through `auditLog` that name attempt ids (the per-version restart's row, the language switch's row of step 27) | The restart's row lists the attempts it closed, previews among them | Ids only, no result. The audit trail is not preview data (reading R18, as for the purge log); a preview's id in it outlives the preview | Not purged; stated |
    | Course file storage and its locked backup | Not written by a preview | Preview Reports export nothing into file storage; no preview data may be written to the `course-content` bucket, whose backup copy is under an object lock (ADR 0003) | None needed |
    | Logs (the hosting platform's request logs, browser error telemetry, the database platform's logs, CI logs) | Lines and URLs can carry attempt, user and course ids; no score, no name | Unchanged; ids only | Not deletable by the application. This record sets no limit on them |
    | Database backups | Every nightly dump holds every row | Unchanged | Not purgeable row by row: a dump is one encrypted file. A manual delete does not reach a dump, and a restore brings the deleted rows back (step 97; reading R27) |

81. **The delete: one SQL function, started only by a person.** Nothing deletes Preview data automatically (the owner's third message of 2026-11-13). In the application the function is called from two places only: the manual delete of Preview Reports (step 99) and the three product-delete routes (step 85). No schedule, no cron route, no refresh function and no restore step makes a deleting call. Outside the application it runs in three checks: after-check A22 and step 98 (8) call it as a dry run, and the rehearsal of step 63 calls it on its own seeded database. Deleting a learner-data row is new to this system, so the function is written to be unable to touch anything else.
    - *Selection.* Preview attempts of the three new kinds: `context_kind = 'preview' and preview_kind in ('course','path_run','journey_run')`, in organisation `p_org`, which is never null, narrowed by `p_scope`: one preview, one run, one tester, one course, one path or one journey program, or the whole organisation. The same predicate is repeated in the `delete` statement itself, so no scope can widen it. A non-preview row can never be selected, and neither can a `legacy_role` row: no scope and no reason names that kind (step 82). No scope names an age: there is no retention figure and the function reads no setting.
    - *The scope.* `p_scope` is a JSON object that holds exactly one of these shapes, and the function raises 22023 and deletes nothing on anything else (an unknown key, an empty object, a null, a key with a null value, two product keys):
      - `{"attempt_id": <uuid>}`: one preview;
      - `{"run_id": <uuid>}`: one test run with its attempts;
      - `{"user_id": <uuid>}`: one tester's preview data;
      - one product key, `{"course_id": <uuid>}`, `{"learning_path_id": <uuid>}` or `{"journey_program_id": <uuid>}`: that product's preview data (for a path or program, its test runs and their attempts);
      - `{"organization": true}`: all of `p_org`'s preview data of the new kinds;
      - `user_id` together with one of `attempt_id`, `run_id` or one product key: one tester's data inside that scope. It is a filter an admin uses ("this tester's previews of this course"); it gives a tester who is not an admin no delete of their own (reading R25).
      No scope takes a list of ids, and none names by-role rows: both are unknown keys and raise. An id that belongs to another organisation or names a row that is not a preview selects nothing. A null `p_org` or a null `p_actor` raises. The reason is `manual` or `product_delete`; any other raises. Reason `product_delete` raises unless the scope is exactly one product key. Every key is applied with `and`, inside the fixed predicate, so no scope can widen the selection beyond preview rows.
    - *A test run goes with its attempts.* A `preview_runs` row is preview data too: it names the tester, the product and dates. A run is deleted when the scope names it, when a wider scope covers it, or when this call deleted its last attempt. A run with no attempt is deleted only when the scope covers it. A run has no clock of its own: nothing is deleted by age.
    - *Isolation check before deleting.* It finds rows that point at a selected attempt in `xp_events`, `journey_day_progress`, `completion_notices`, `learning_path_completions.steps`, `attempt_requests.used_attempt_id` and `revision_visits`. There must be none by step 80. An attempt that has one is never deleted: an official side effect on a preview is a defect to be looked at, not something to delete around. The function does not raise for the whole call: it sets the attempt (and its run) aside, deletes the rest, writes the excluded ids and the tables that point at them into the audit row, and answers `excluded` above 0. A dry run reports the same, so the admin sees "n kept for review" in the summary before confirming, and the page shows it again after the delete; a product delete stops on it (step 85). Preview Reports list the set-aside attempts under "Kept for review". This record plans no way to delete around one: the defect that wrote the official row is fixed first, and what happens to the row is the owner's decision. Nothing runs overnight, so a set-aside attempt is seen when an admin deletes, and in the "Kept for review" list.
    - *Dry run (the owner's "dry-run summary").* With `p_dry_run = true` it returns what a real call would delete and writes nothing, no audit row either: the count per table (attempts, test runs, statements, State rows, launch tokens, sessions, outbox rows) and their total, and the number excluded. It also returns a `selection_hash`: a hash over two sorted id lists, each under its own label: the attempts and test runs the call would delete, and the attempts it would set aside. The hash fixes which attempts and test runs go and which attempts are set aside; it does not cover the counts of their child rows (statements, State rows, launch tokens, sessions, outbox rows). An empty selection has a hash too. **The real call must carry that hash.** A real call with a null hash raises 22023. A real call whose own selection gives another hash raises and deletes nothing, so the attempts and test runs deleted, and the attempts set aside, are exactly those a dry run reported (step 99): a preview launched, a row deleted by someone else or an attempt newly set aside in between each change the selection, and the call raises. The child-row counts can be higher than the summary's, and this is stated: the selection has no status filter, so a preview that is still being written to, for example one still open, can be selected, and it may write statements, State rows or session rows between the summary and the confirmation. Those rows belong to a selected preview and go with it. The audit row and the result the page shows hold the real call's counts, not the dry run's. What the function proves is that a deletion matches a dry run; that a person saw that dry run's summary rests on the two callers, the manual route and the product-delete routes, and on their tests (steps 85 and 99).
    - *Delete.* Outbox rows by `attempt_id`; the attempts (statements, State, tokens and sessions go by cascade; the function owns the tables, so the missing delete grants of `learning_sessions` and the absent trigger on `course_attempts` are not in its way); then the runs whose last attempt this call deleted and the runs named or covered by the scope, never any other run. It asserts that the number of attempts deleted equals the number selected less the excluded ones, and raises otherwise, which rolls the whole call back.
    - *Audit (the owner's "audit record").* One `preview_purge_log` row for every call that deleted or excluded anything, written in the same transaction as the delete, so there is no deletion by this function without its row: who (`actor_user_id`, never null), when (`ran_at`), how many (`counts`, per table and in total), why, the scope, and the deleted ids. Ids and counts only. The ids are kept so that the record says exactly what was removed; "Deletion history" lets an admin download them, and after a restore they show what to delete again by hand (steps 97, 99). Re-applying a logged deletion automatically is a possible later feature and is not planned.
    - *Manual, at any time.* `POST app/api/preview/purge/route.ts` (phase P): the caller's session and organisation; the role check of reading R25 (`super_owner` or `admin` of that organisation; a `data_analyst` is refused with 403); a first call with the dry run returns the summary, which the page shows in a confirmation; the second call deletes (step 99). It calls the function with the service role and passes the caller as `p_actor`. No TypeScript code deletes an attempt row: rule (iii) of `tests/unit/revision-listing.test.ts` (no `.delete()` on `course_attempts` and three other tables in app code) stays as it is.
    - *Nothing automatic.* `refresh_report_views()` and `refresh_gamification_views()` do not call this function, and their answers carry no `preview_purge` entry. No `/api/cron/*` route, `cron.yml` line or pg_cron job names it. Two guards keep it so: a source scan in `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` (step 62) asserts that `purge_preview_data` is named in application code only by `lib/preview/purge.ts`, which only the manual route and the three product-delete routes import, and never under `app/api/cron/`, in `components/`, in `.github/workflows/` or in `scripts/` outside `scripts/migration-rehearsal/`, whose files call it on the rehearsal's seeded database (step 63); and a rehearsal case (step 63) seeds previews dated two years ago, runs both refresh functions and the completion sweep, and asserts that every preview row, every test run and the audit table are unchanged, and that no function of 0107 to 0109 other than `purge_preview_data` holds a `delete` on `course_attempts` or `preview_runs` (read from `pg_proc`).
    - *The refresh route's failure reporting, for the completion sweep.* `app/api/cron/refresh-report-views/route.ts` answers 500 and records an errored heartbeat when the `completion_sweep` entry carries an error, in phase 1's pull request with its unit test: today the route answers `ok: true` whenever the function returns (`:39-55`), the heartbeat is recorded with that (`:61-64`), and a failed sweep of the fail-closed writer would pass unseen. Other entries keep today's behaviour. The refresh answer has no entry for Preview data.
    - *After a restore.* Nothing is deleted. A restored database holds again the Preview data that was deleted after the dump; admins re-check Preview Reports and delete again by hand what is no longer needed (step 97).
    - *Rehearsal.* Step 63's purge rehearsal is the proof that no non-preview row is touched; spec 75 repeats it on staging.

82. **Rows flagged as previews by role before this record (`legacy_role`; decision A33; reading R26).** The owner, 2026-11-13: "Please preserve the old staff records that have been classified as Preview based on the user's role." They are preserved: nothing deletes them automatically, the delete function never selects them, and no admin can delete them. The flag on them does not prove a test: an analyst's first launch used the official slot and could consume a grant (Context row 9), and the 0100 backfill flagged every earlier attempt of anyone who held a staff role when it was pasted, real learner work before a promotion included (`0100:456-467`). No role-change history exists to tell them apart. So: 0107 labels them `preview` and `legacy_role` (they leave every official count), Q10 counts them, and they are kept. On production every count is expected to read 0, because production has no courses; a count above 0 goes to the owner before the paste. On staging they are test data, and the owner may reset them under the standing direction of 2026-10-18. Three things about them are beyond any delete and are stated: points rows they earned (`xp_events` is append-only for every role), statements already forwarded to a tenant LRS, and emails and webhook messages already sent. Such a row can also still hold a used grant; the grant stays "used". Two effects of these rows reach the same people now that they learn for real under R6, and both are kept as they are and stated: (1) a journey day credit that a by-role preview earned is labelled `legacy_cross_context` (step 8) and still counts as credited, so that person is not asked for the day again; (2) points a by-role preview earned stay, and their per-course key means the person's later real completion of that course pays nothing more (it still gets its 0-point event row and its result). Both were that person's real standing under the rule of the time, when the launch itself treated those rows as their attempts. Two more effects reach real learner work and are stated: (3) a person whose earlier work the 0100 backfill flagged holds, after 0107, no official attempt in the Individual or path context of that course; the earlier result is kept but hidden from every official surface, and their next Start is a first official attempt, with no grant; (4) an open by-role row is never resumed by Start, whose key is `individual`, a path or a day and never `preview`, and it is closed by that person's next Preview of the version (step 78), so work in progress on it is not continued. Q10 counts all four. If the owner wants them undone instead, the ledger's own means is a compensating `adjustment` row and a label on the credit; this record does not plan it, and on production the counts are expected to be 0.

    **Never deletable (reading R26, object if wrong).** Some of these rows are tests, and some are real learner work: the rows of anyone who held a staff role when 0100 was pasted (`0100:456-467` flagged every earlier attempt of theirs), among them rows that hold a result, a used grant, points or a day credit, and rows of real work that has no result yet. The launch mode does not tell them apart either: with ALDS_P4 on, every launch by an admin, a super owner or an analyst is written as Browse (`lib/alds/launch.ts:58-60`), an assigned analyst's attempt in the official slot included, and a SCORM or A.1 package stores a score and a completion in Browse (`launch:1298-1305`), so a by-role row can hold a real result in either mode. The owner's A36 answer allows deletion "only" for "Preview/test data" and says "Official learner records and completion history must remain protected."
    - *No scope reaches them.* No scope and no reason of the delete function selects a `legacy_role` row (step 81): "all preview data of this organisation" does not touch one, and neither does a product delete's call. The function holds no test of which by-role rows could go, because a rule that never deletes them needs none.
    - *The one open question.* Whether admins may delete by-role rows that nothing official depends on is asked of the owner as one yes or no (section "Decisions requested"). What is built is "no". A "yes" would add a scope and a test of what protects a row to 0107's function, and the delete guards of step 7 would then count only the by-role rows that test protects, reading the same test; it is a separate, priced step (step 72), so it must come before phase 1.
    - *Preview Reports list them read only (steps 84, 99).* "Kept earlier staff attempts", apart from the new-kind previews: per row the course, the person and the date; no score beyond what Preview Reports show for any preview; no delete option, and its heading says "These are kept and cannot be deleted". On production the list is expected to be empty.
    - *Product deletes.* A course, a path, a journey program or a language that holds any by-role row is not deleted, and the database enforces it. For a course, a version or a language, the delete guards that 0107 recreates count by-role rows and raise `learner_history_exists` (step 7). For a path or a program, a by-role row with an old tag refuses through its `no action` key (step 6). The 0100 bodies of the guards let every preview through (`0100:528-531`, `:561-565`), so without the change a course's cascade would remove by-role rows unseen, and a delete made straight through the Data API would meet no check at all. The routes still count first, so that the admin reads the reason: 409 with nothing deleted (step 85; for a course, "This course holds earlier staff attempts that are kept. Archive it instead"). The count gives the message; the guard and the key are the protection.
    - *What this means for deleting content.* A course, a version or a language that a staff member opened while launches were flagged by role holds a by-role row, and no admin can delete it again; it is archived or deactivated. Before 0107 a course whose only attempts were staff attempts could be deleted (ADR 0003 decision 3; row 15 of "Conflicts with existing records"). On production this is expected to affect nothing: there are no courses, so no by-role rows, and 0107, the tag and 0108 are one sitting, so none can be written afterwards. On staging it affects test courses, every one a staff member opens until phase 2 serves there (step 60). From phase 2 a staff member's Start is a real attempt and blocks a delete like any learner's (R6); a Preview or a test run does not block, and the product delete purges it with an audit row (step 85).
    - *What still removes them.* An organisation's delete and a user's delete, by cascade, as today: the whole tenant or person goes (reading R22). A delete made with the service role passes the guards as before (step 7); in the application that client deletes only the rows of an upload that failed or was abandoned, and the test purges use it to delete whole test organisations.
    - *After a restore.* Nothing to do for these rows: they are never deleted, so a dump holds the same ones.
    - *What stays.* Every by-role row, for good, on the owner's sentences "Please preserve the old staff records" and A36. The three things beyond any delete, named above, are unchanged.
    - *What waits.* Nothing. An objection to reading R26, and a "yes" to the open question, must come before phase 1, because 0107 cannot be re-pasted on staging once 0109 is there.

83. **No retention limit: how long Preview data lives, and where a manual delete does not reach (decision A34 is closed for Preview data).** The owner, 2026-11-13: "Preview data should not be automatically deleted after 30, 60 or 90 days", and Preview data stays "until an Admin decides that it is no longer required and manually deletes it". No retention figure is stored, no rule on any backup bucket is asked, and nothing waits on one.
    - *Live rows.* They stay until an authorised admin deletes them (step 99). A manual delete removes the rows, their statements, State, tokens, sessions and outbox rows at once.
    - *Growth, stated.* With no automatic deletion, Preview data grows with testing and is counted in the database's size. Preview Reports show each organisation's totals (attempts, test runs, the oldest row's date) on the delete screen, so an admin sees what has built up. This record sets no reminder and no limit; a later record may.
    - *Database backups.* Every nightly dump is a full copy, previews included, encrypted as one file, so one row cannot be removed from it. A Preview row deleted by hand stays in the dumps taken before the delete, for as long as those dumps exist, and a restore of such a dump brings it back (step 97). The owner confirmed the bucket on 2026-11-13: the database backup bucket holds the nightly encrypted dumps, has no default object-lock retention, and keeps every version. So today no dump is ever deleted. That breaks no rule of this record. Whether old dumps should expire is a general backup retention question, optional and independent of Preview: a rule that deletes whole dumps also shortens how far back the official records can be restored. It is the owner's to decide at any time and is no gate here (reading R27).
    - *Logs.* Lines that can carry a preview's attempt id (the hosting platform's request logs, browser error telemetry, the database platform's logs) live as long as each store keeps them. Ids only, no score, no name. No limit applies and no read of their retention is required.
    - *Outside the system*, and not reachable by any delete: a tenant's LRS (hence decision A35, never forward), mail already delivered, a CRM message already sent. New previews send none of these.
    - *Gate.* None: nothing about Preview data holds back production's first course (step 74a).

84. **Preview Reports** (`app/[org]/(admin)/preview-reports/`, flag `PREVIEW_REPORTS`; phase P). A separate section of the admin area with its own nav item, "Preview Reports", with the line "Not official records" under its title (not "test data", which on production would read as a breach of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` principle 5; R21). Nothing in it links into an official report and no official report links into it.
    - *Courses.* Per course and tester: each Preview's start, last activity, status, score, pass or fail under the course's rule, time spent, the count of statements and interactions stored, and a drill-down to the question-level answers of that preview attempt, so a tester can see that "data is being captured as expected".
    - *Path test runs.* Per run: the path, the tester, the steps in order with locked, open, done or failed, score, `passed_ever` and the latest result, the run's simulated date, and whether the run reached the end; the same columns the path report shows a learner, computed by `pathProgress()` over the run's preview rows.
    - *Journey test runs.* Per run: the program and pinned version, the simulated date, each day with locked, open, credited-in-this-run, failed or rest, score and late, and whether the run finished; computed by `computeJourneyState()` over the run's rows.
    - *What an official completion would have done.* For each completed item, labels only: the email template that a learner's completion would send, whether a webhook would be sent under the organisation's scope, and that points would be considered. Nothing is sent or written.
    - *Housekeeping: "Delete preview data" (step 99).* The organisation's totals; the delete options with their dry-run summary and confirmation; "Deletion history", the audit rows (who, when, how many) with "Download ids"; the read-only list "Kept earlier staff attempts" and "Kept for review" (steps 81, 82). No retention figure and no delete-by date are shown: there is none. A `data_analyst` sees this part read only, without the delete options (reading R25). Every page of the section carries the "Preview" mark on each row, which with the separate section is how preview data is "identifiable", and the permission check of reading R9 is how it is "accessible only to authorised users" (the owner's R21 conditions).
    - *Reads.* The `data_analyst` model: every read uses the service role after the permission check of reading R9, and names `organization_id = org.id`, because RLS does not back it up. The reads are plain selects of `course_attempts` with `context_kind = 'preview'` and of `preview_runs`, through `lib/preview/runs.ts`. There is no SQL view and no matview for previews, so nothing preview-shaped can be joined into an official object by mistake.
    - *Scoring.* `contextResult` accepts a preview key and returns that key's rows only; it is called with one only from `lib/preview/` (step 86). The official kinds are unchanged.

85. **Manual delete and product deletes.** The manual delete, its options, its dry-run summary and its confirmation are step 99. **Product deletes show the summary too.** A product delete is started by a person and can delete Preview data, so the owner's "dry-run summary ... and ask for confirmation" applies to it. Each of the three routes below answers a first request, sent without `confirm`, with the dry run's summary and deletes nothing; the delete dialog shows "This also deletes preview data: n previews, n test runs, n records in all"; the admin confirms, and the second request carries `confirm` and the summary's `selection_hash`. Each route passes the session user's id as `p_actor` in both calls, never a value from the request, so the audit row of a product delete names the admin who confirmed it. A product with no Preview data keeps today's single confirmation on the screen, and still two requests: the dialog sends the first request after the admin has confirmed the delete as today, and when the summary is empty (every count 0, nothing kept for review) it sends the second at once, with `confirm` and the hash of the empty selection, and shows no further dialog. `product-delete-order.test.ts` covers that no route calls the real delete without `confirm`, that every route passes the request's `selection_hash` to the real call as `p_expect_hash`, never a hash it computed itself in the same request, that every route's `p_actor` is the session's user and can never be supplied by the client, and that a route answers 400 with nothing deleted when `confirm` comes without a hash. **A change between the dry run and the real call** (a Preview launched, an attempt newly set aside) makes the real call raise on its hash (step 81): nothing is deleted, the 503 rule below applies, and the dialog shows the new summary, on which the route's next request answers as the rules below say. The routes still read `excluded` from the real call's answer and refuse on it, as a second guard that costs nothing. The delete routes of step 40 call the purge with reason `product_delete` for the path or program before their own delete, so test runs never block removing a path or program that has no learner attempts; without it, `preview_runs`' `no action` reference would answer the misleading 409 "has learner attempts".

    **The three routes (built in phase 4; their spec 75 cases in phase P).** The path delete, the journey program delete and the course delete (`app/api/courses/[courseId]/route.ts:167`). The course delete is one of them because the delete guard lets a course whose only attempts are previews of the new kinds be deleted (step 7), and that cascade would remove preview attempts with no log row and leave their test runs' steps gone without a trace. Each route, in this order: (1) counts the attempts that would block the delete and that the purge cannot remove: for a course, its attempts that are not previews and its `legacy_role` rows; for a path or a journey program, every attempt that carries the product's tag (`learning_path_id`, or an enrolment of the program), which includes a `legacy_role` row with an old tag, because its `no action` key refuses the delete (step 6) and the purge never selects it. Above 0 the route answers 409 and deletes nothing, preview data included; when only by-role rows block, the 409 is the "kept" message of step 82 (for a path or a program: "This item holds earlier staff attempts that are kept. Archive it instead"). By-role rows are counted through the helper in `lib/preview/runs.ts` that the language delete uses (`lib/preview/` is the only place that may name `preview_kind`, step 62); the delete function is not asked about them, because it never selects them. The count is what gives the admin the right message; it is not the protection. A by-role row that the count missed, or one written after it (possible only until 0108 is pasted, by old launch code through the paste-window trigger of step 7), is still not deleted: the delete guard refuses a course's delete with `learner_history_exists`, and the row's `no action` key refuses a path's or a program's (steps 6, 7); (2) calls `purge_preview_data` with reason `product_delete` and the product's scope, which writes the log row and removes the outbox rows, which have no foreign key (`0044:30`); (3) deletes the product. If a learner attempt or a by-role row appears between (1) and (3), the delete is refused and the route answers 409: for a path or a program by the attempt's `no action` key (23503); for a course by the delete guard, which raises `learner_history_exists` (below). The preview data is then already gone, with its log row. That is accepted: preview data may be deleted at any time.

    **Three rules of the routes.** *A preview set aside is never deleted by a product delete.* Step 81 keeps a preview attempt that an official row points at, for the owner to decide. So in all three routes, between (1) and (2), the route calls the purge as a dry run with the same scope (it is the first request's summary), and when it answers `excluded` above 0 the route answers 409 "This item holds preview data that is kept for review and cannot be deleted yet" and deletes nothing, preview data included. The real call in (2) is read the same way: `excluded` above 0 there answers the same 409 and step (3) is not run. Without this, a course delete would cascade the set-aside attempt away with no log row and no decision, and a path or program delete would stop on the kept run's `no action` key with the wrong message. *The course delete stays a session delete.* The delete guard returns at once for the service role (`0100:558-560`, kept by step 7) and otherwise raises `learner_history_exists` when the course holds an attempt that is not a preview of a new kind (step 7; the 0100 body, `:561-573`, counts attempts that are not previews); the route deletes with the caller's session client (`app/api/courses/[courseId]/route.ts:222-225`) and maps that error to 409 (`:227`; `lib/courses/learner-history.ts:64-66`). Step (3) keeps exactly that client. Only the count and the two purge calls use the service role, and they sit inside the route's admin branch (`:200-218`): a caller who is not an admin reaches no purge and, as today, an RLS delete that removes nothing. A course delete made with the service client would skip the guard and cascade real learner attempts and by-role rows away, which cannot be undone; `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` (step 62) gains the case that the course route's `courses` delete is never called on a service client and that its purge calls are inside the admin branch. In the path and program routes the purge calls come after the admin check that step 40 adds. *One window stays, and is named.* A Preview launched between (2) and (3): on a course it is cascaded with no log row (the guard does not count previews of the new kinds); on a path or a program its new run row refuses the delete with 23503; step 40's 409 would then say "has learner attempts", so on a 23503 the route re-counts as in (1) and, when no blocking attempt exists, answers 409 "Preview data was added while deleting. Try again", and the admin presses delete again. Both need a tester to start a preview in the same moment an admin deletes the product; neither touches an official row.

    **Two more rules.** *A purge call that fails stops the delete.* If the dry run or the real purge call returns an error of any kind, a raise, a timeout or a network failure, the route answers 503 "Preview data could not be checked. Nothing was deleted. Try again", and step (3) is not run. Only a clean answer with `excluded` 0 lets the product delete go on. Without this rule a route that read an error as "nothing to purge" would cascade a course's previews away with no log row, a set-aside one included. `tests/unit/product-delete-order.test.ts` (new) covers, for each of the three routes, an error from each call, `excluded` above 0 from each call, and the order count, dry run, purge, delete, with the case that a course holding a by-role row is refused before any purge call. *One re-count, never a loop.* On a 23503 from step (3) of a path or program delete the route re-counts once and answers, with no second delete in the same request: 409 "has learner attempts" when a blocking attempt exists; else 409 "This item has learner records (requests or completions). Archive it instead" when an `attempt_requests` or `learning_path_completions` row names it, since both hold `no action` keys to it and the attempt count cannot see them; else 409 "Preview data was added while deleting. Try again".

    **The language delete (not a product delete).** `DELETE app/api/courses/[courseId]/packages/[packageId]/route.ts`, read at the branch's current `main`, which holds pull request 245: it counts every attempt on the language's versions, previews included, with the service client and answers 409 "learner attempts exist on this language" (`:292-312`), which for preview data is a misleading refusal. It then deletes the `course_packages` row on the caller's session (`:337-341`), so 0100's version guard runs inside the cascade and refuses with `learner_history_exists` when any version holds an attempt that is not a preview (`0100:528-539`) and, once 0107 has recreated the guard, also when one holds a by-role row (step 7); the route maps that error to 409 (`:343-351`). A delete on the service client would skip the guard (`0100:525-527`) and cascade a learner attempt inserted after the count. The route keeps refusing on preview data, because the purge has no language scope and this record adds none to 0107, but it says why. It counts through the helper in `lib/preview/runs.ts` (`lib/preview/` is the only place that may name `preview_kind`, step 62; `runs.ts` imports nothing from `purge.ts`, so the import rule of step 81 holds): attempts that are not previews answer today's 409; otherwise any `legacy_role` row answers 409 "Earlier staff attempts are kept on this language. Deactivate it instead" (reading R26: such a row is never deleted, so the message promises no delete); otherwise new-kind previews answer 409 "Only preview data exists on this language. Delete this course's preview data in Preview Reports, then delete the language". The route purges nothing, and the helper calls no delete function. The route does delete attempts in one case, by cascade: the version guard does not count previews of the new kinds (step 7), so a Preview started between the route's count and its delete goes with the language and leaves no log row. The route's own count is what stops that outside the window; `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` has the case that this route's `course_packages` delete is never called on a service client, as for the course route. A spec 75 case covers the three answers and the delete that succeeds after the manual purge; the race with a learner attempt cannot be staged in a spec, so it is a rehearsal case (step 63: the guard refuses through the cascade for a session claim and lets the service role through). It is inside phase 4's days, as an estimate.

    **The cascades that delete attempt rows without the delete function** are named, not hidden: an organisation's delete; a user's delete; a course's delete, for a Preview started in the window between the purge and the delete; and a language's delete, for a Preview started in the window between its count and its delete. The first two remove the whole tenant or person, their by-role rows and their purge log included. None can be reached by a learner; the course's and the language's cascades remove no attempt that is not a preview and no by-role row, because the delete guards refuse while one exists (step 7), whatever a route counted. **These four are exceptions to the owner's "an audit record for every deletion" and to the dry-run summary, and are put to the owner as such (reading R22).** After an organisation's or a user's delete no audit row of their preview data survives, because the log goes with the tenant or names a person who no longer exists. In the two windows, a Preview started in the same moment an admin deletes the course or the language is removed with no log row. The windows are not closed by a log row written from the route: the log is inserted only by the purge function (step 79), and a route that wrote it would be a second writer of the audit. Nothing is stored about a row removed in a window: a dry run writes nothing, and the routes' counts are not kept.

86. **Guards.** `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` and `preview-run-state.test.ts` (step 62); the purge rehearsal (step 63); spec 75 (step 64); after-checks A21 to A23 (step 19). `attempt-insert-sites.test.ts` asserts that a preview insert names `preview_kind` and no official tag. The tier guard's one amendment is stated in step 62: check (ii) is unchanged, a new check (ii-b) limits `preview_kind` and `preview_run_id` to the launch page, `lib/alds/launch.ts`, `lib/preview/`, the two preview routes and the Preview Reports pages, and `context_kind` is not guarded by name. `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` asserts positively that `lib/lrs/` (with `lib/lrs/abandoned.ts`), `lib/integrations/` and the raw attempt readers of step 49 leave the preview context out.

    **The owner allowed the departure below on 2026-11-13 (A36):** "I agree to allow deletion of Preview data, with a dry run before deletion and an audit record for every deletion. This permission must apply only to Preview/test data. Official learner records and completion history must remain protected." The dry run is step 81's and every button's first call; the audit record is the `preview_purge_log` row; the limit to preview and test data is the selection predicate, which no by-role row can meet (step 82). **Where each condition holds, and where it does not (reading R22).** Every deletion is started by a person. *Dry run before every deletion:* in two layers. The function refuses a real call that carries no hash, or a hash that is not its own selection's, so no deletion goes through it that does not match a dry run (step 81). That a person saw the summary of that dry run and confirmed it rests on the function's only two callers in the application and their tests: `preview-purge-route.test.ts` and `product-delete-order.test.ts` (steps 85 and 99). The manual delete shows the summary and asks for confirmation (step 99), and so do the product deletes (step 85). No deletion runs without a person, so there is none without a dry run before it. The real call checks the `selection_hash` of the confirmed summary, so no preview and no test run is deleted that the admin did not see; a selected preview that is still in use can add rows of its own before the confirmation, and they go with it (step 81). *An audit row for every deletion:* it holds for every deletion the function makes, in the same transaction, with a named person in every row (`actor_user_id` is never null). *Protection of official records:* the selection predicate, the isolation check, the count assertion, and for by-role rows the rule that nothing selects them. **The exceptions (reading R22).** The four cascades of step 85 (an organisation's delete, a user's delete, a Preview started in the window of a course delete, and one in the window of a language delete) remove Preview rows without the delete function. They leave no audit row and show no dry-run summary. All four are started by a person; none is a schedule. They are stated to the owner as exceptions to both conditions.

    **The principle this section departs from.** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 3: "Learner history is never rewritten or deleted: attempts, statements, stored package bytes." The purge deletes attempt rows and their statements. It is the first operation in this system that cannot be undone. The departure is the owner's own requirement (R7, A36 and the third message of 2026-11-13: by hand only), it is limited to preview data of the three new kinds (rows flagged by role before this record are preserved and never selected), and it is fenced by the selection predicate, the isolation check, the dry run, the count assertion, the audit row and the rehearsal. Accepting this record with decision A36 allows it; nothing else in principle 3 changes.

99. **The manual delete screen and its confirmation (numbered 99 because 87 to 98 are taken; phase P).** The owner: "The LMS should provide a manual deletion option in the Preview Reports section. Before deletion, the system must show a dry-run summary of the records selected for deletion and ask for confirmation. Every deletion must generate an audit record containing who deleted the data, when it was deleted and how many records were deleted."
    - *Where.* `app/[org]/(admin)/preview-reports/delete/page.tsx`, the "Delete preview data" part of Preview Reports, linked from every Preview Reports page; each row and each test run in the reports also carries its own "Delete". Client parts in the same folder; the reads through `lib/preview/runs.ts`, the calls through `lib/preview/purge.ts` and `POST app/api/preview/purge/route.ts` (step 81).
    - *Who (reading R25, object if wrong).* `super_owner` and `admin` of the organisation, checked in the page and again in the route; the organisation is the session's, asserted on every read and passed as `p_org`. A `data_analyst` may open Preview Reports and this part read only, sees no delete option, and is refused by the route with 403. A tester who is not an admin has no delete of their own: the owner names "Authorised Admin users", and an analyst is not one. An admin's "one tester" option covers the need.
    - *What can be selected.* One preview; one test run; one tester's preview data (the admin's own name is offered first); all preview data of one course, path or journey; all preview data of this organisation. Earlier staff attempts cannot be selected: they are listed read only under "Kept earlier staff attempts" (step 82).
    - *The flow.* (1) The admin chooses a selection. (2) The page calls the route with `dry_run: true` and shows the summary: what is selected in words, the count per kind of record and the total, the number kept for review, and the sentence "This cannot be undone. Official learner records are not affected." (3) The admin confirms with the dialog's "Delete" button. (4) The page calls the route with `dry_run: false` and the summary's `selection_hash`. If the selection changed in between (a tester launched a preview, another admin deleted), the function raises, nothing is deleted, and the page shows the new summary and asks again. (5) The page shows the result from the audit row: who, when, how many, and "n kept for review" if any.
    - *Deletion history.* The audit rows of the organisation, newest first: the admin's name (looked up from `actor_user_id` for display; "former member" when the account is gone), the time, the selection in words, the counts and the total. The list is read only. Each row offers "Download ids" (a file of the deleted attempt and run ids, nothing else), so that a deletion can be repeated by hand after a restore if that is ever needed (step 97). Nothing on the page re-applies a logged deletion; that is a possible later feature and is not planned. History rows cannot be edited or deleted (the table is append-only).
    - *No automatic path.* The page and the route are the only callers in the application besides step 85's routes (the scan of step 81). The route is not under `app/api/cron/`, accepts no cron secret and no API key, and requires a signed-in session.
    - *Tests.* `tests/unit/preview-purge-route.test.ts` (new): 403 for `data_analyst` and `user`, with no call of the function; another organisation's ids delete nothing; a real call without a hash from a dry run is refused by the route before the function is called; `p_actor` is the session's user and can never be supplied by the client; the scope shapes the route may send. Spec 75 (step 64): an admin deletes one preview, one run and a course's previews, each through the summary and the confirmation, and the summary's counts equal the rows that disappear (in these cases nothing writes to the selected previews between the summary and the delete); cancelling at the confirmation deletes nothing and writes no audit row; a changed selection asks again; an analyst sees no delete option and the route answers 403; the history row shows the admin, the time and the counts, and its "Download ids" file holds the deleted ids; an earlier staff attempt is listed read only, has no delete option, and no selection the route accepts removes it; an official attempt of the same learner and course is unchanged throughout.

    **Placement.** Phase 1 (0107): the columns, `preview_runs`, the audit log, the delete function (called by no refresh function), the wrapper's early return, Q10, the purge rehearsal. Phase 2: Preview by mode with `preview_kind = 'course'` and the launch mode of A32; the tenant LRS and CRM filters, the abandon path and the raw attempt readers; the guard amendments. Phase 1's pull request also carries the refresh route's failure reporting for the completion sweep (step 81). Phase 4: the three product-delete routes with their summary dialogs, and the language delete's helper (step 85). Phase P: Preview Reports, test runs, the manual delete screen and route (step 99), spec 75. Phase 9a: the restore lines of the backup runbook (step 97). Under R12's alternative only the path and journey report pages and the test runs leave the release; the manual delete with its screen and spec 75's course, isolation and delete cases stay in it (step 72). No new migration file: every database object of this section is in 0107, so D3's order for the sitting is unchanged. 0109 recreates `refresh_report_views()` again; it holds no call of the delete function.

### T. Testing a whole Learning Path or Journey without official records (the owner's A21 question)

87. **The question.** The owner agreed that Preview has no Continue button and asked how an admin or authorised tester then validates a complete path or journey "without creating official learner progress or results". Today there is no such way. A path can be previewed one step at a time, and on staging a preview from a path shows a Continue that skips the wait. A journey cannot be previewed at all: the only way to walk one is a real self-enrolment, which writes real day credits, a real completion and a badge, and shows in every journey report and the CRM feed. A later calendar day can be reached only by back-dating the start date.

88. **The options.**

    | | 1. A guided test run in Preview Reports (recommended) | 2. A test-learner account | 3. A scripted harness |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | What it is | A staff member starts a "Test run" of a path or journey. Each launch is a preview in the run's own context; the run page shows every step or day as a learner would see it | A real account with the learner role, enrolled like any learner, in an organisation kept for testing | The lifecycle specs (63, 68, 69, 73) drive a browser as a learner on staging |
    | Official records written | None | Yes, real ones for that account: attempts, credits, completions, points, emails, CRM rows | Yes, in throwaway organisations on staging |
    | Locks, pass rules, step and day order | Yes: the same pure rules, read over the run's rows | Yes | Yes |
    | Calendar unlock of a later day, release dates, without waiting | Yes: the run has a simulated date the tester moves forward | Only by back-dating the journey's start date; a step's release date cannot be skipped | Yes, by fixture dates |
    | The Continue button itself and its wait | No. The run page offers the next step; the launch bar has no Continue | Yes | Yes |
    | Real emails | No. It names the email a learner would get | Yes, to the test mailbox | Captured, not delivered |
    | CRM and the webhook | No. It says whether one would be sent | Yes, and Yoddha sees the test account unless the organisation is separate | Yes, against a test receiver |
    | Points, badges, leaderboards | No | Yes | Yes |
    | Grants, requests, revision visits, self-enrolment, reminders, reports, the Overview | No: these are official flows a preview is kept out of | Yes | Yes |
    | Where it can be used | Staging and production | Staging only. On production it is test data (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 5), and keeping it out of reports would need a test flag that every official reader must honour: a second exclusion rule beside the preview one, which this record exists to avoid | Staging only |

    A fourth option was considered and is not offered: a preview that carries the path and shows a no-wait Continue (the alternative that decision A21 offered). The owner decided on 2026-11-13 that Preview has no Continue button, and this record does not bring one back under another name. If the owner later wants a "next step" control inside the launch bar of a test run, that is a new decision for the owner.

89. **The recommendation: option 1, the guided test run, with the staging practice of options 2 and 3 for what it cannot reach.**
    - *Start.* "Test run" on the path editor, the journey program editor and Preview Reports, for the roles of reading R9. `POST app/api/preview/runs/route.ts` inserts a `preview_runs` row with the service role after the session, organisation and role checks; a journey run pins the program's current version.
    - *The run page* (`app/[org]/(admin)/preview-reports/runs/[runId]/`) lists the steps or days with the state a learner would see, from `pathProgress()` or `computeJourneyState()` fed with the run's preview rows, the run's pinned version and its simulated date. The real rules are what is tested, because the same functions decide. A journey run's days are "credited in this run" when the run's preview attempt of that day satisfies the day's pass rule; no credit row exists.
    - *Launch.* Each open step or day has "Open (test run)": `launch?mode=preview&run=<id>`, with `&day=<day id>` for a journey. The resolver's test-run row (step 22) checks the run and that the step or day is open in it; a locked step is refused as it is for a learner. The launch bar says "Preview: not an official record" and has no Continue. The exit returns to the run page, which now shows the next step open and offers it.
    - *Simulated date.* "Next day" and "Jump to date" on the run page move `preview_runs.simulated_date`. Calendar days, release dates and the deadline are read against it, so a 30-day journey can be walked in one sitting and "behind" and "overdue" can be seen.
    - *Result.* The run, its scores and its progress show in Preview Reports and nowhere else, and go with the purge.
    - *What it does not test, and where that is tested.* The Continue button and its wait, real email delivery, the CRM feed and webhook, points and badges, grants and requests: on staging, with learner accounts by hand and by specs 63, 68, 69, 70, 72 and 73 every night. The run page says so in one line, so a tester does not take a green test run as proof of those.
    - *Activity days (decision A41).* D13's activity days, built in phase G, are credited with no attempt (`credit_kind` `self` or `admin`), so a run that reads only its preview attempts could never pass one and would stop at the first such day. From phase G the run page shows "Mark done (test run)" on an open activity day; it adds the day's id to `preview_runs.done_activity_day_ids` (step 79), and the run's state counts that day as credited in the run. No `journey_day_progress` row, approval or email is written; the mark is purged with the run. It is an exception to "a test run's progress is derived from preview attempts only", limited to activity days, and it is stored on the run's own row and in no official table, so the rule of section A, principle 7, and step 80, that nothing of a preview enters a progress table, holds. What it does not test: the approval flow of an admin-credited activity, which stays a staging check with a learner account. Until phase G no activity day exists and the list stays empty.
    - *Cost and placement.* 3 days in phase P; half a day in phase G for the activity mark; the schema is in 0107 (step 79).

### U. Journey republish and day identity (the owner's A27 answer)

90. **What identifies a day today: nothing but its number.** A version's frozen days hold `{day, course_id, mission_title}` and no identity (`app/api/journey/program/route.ts:82-86`). Credits and attempt tags hold an integer day and no version (`0058:169`, `:173`, `:192`). An enrolment's earlier pins are overwritten with no history (`route.ts:135-140`). The draft table has a row id, `journey_days.id`, but it is a slot id: the editor never loads it, "move up" swaps the content of two numbered rows and leaves the rows where they are (`journey-client.tsx:472-485`), and save upserts on the number (`route.ts:663-665`). Used as it is, that id would be the per-number rule again. Today an in-progress attempt survives a publish to all only because the launch finds it by course version and re-tags it to the new day number (`launch:834-884`); 0107's immutability trigger and the per-context lookup remove exactly that. A rule that credits by day number would therefore repeat completed days and orphan started ones, which is what the owner's answer forbids.

91. **The design: a day keeps one identity for life, and credits and contexts name it.** In 0107:
    - `journey_days.id` becomes the day's identity: it travels with the day when the admin moves it (step 92). `journey_days.removed_at timestamptz` (new): a removed day is kept, without a number, so attempts and credits that name it keep a parent. `day_number` becomes nullable, with `check (removed_at is null or day_number is null)`; `unique (program_id, day_number)` (`0058:73`) is unchanged and ignores the nulls.
    - `journey_versions.day_ids jsonb` (new): `{"<day number>": "<day id>"}` for every day of the version, and `day_ids_source text check (day_ids_source in ('slot_backfill','editor'))`. Backfill (once): from `journey_days` by (program, day number), source `slot_backfill`. It is exact as a reading of history by slot, because until the new editor ships a row's id and its number are the same thing (step 90). `days` itself is not rewritten (principle 5). A paste-window `before insert` trigger fills `day_ids` the same way, marked `slot_backfill`, for a version published by the old code; 0108 drops it and sets the column `not null`.
    - `course_attempts.journey_day_id` in the key, the CHECK and the immutability trigger (steps 5, 7); `attempt_requests.journey_day_id` (step 8).
    - `journey_day_progress.day_id uuid references journey_days(id) on delete no action`, backfilled once from (the enrolment's program, `day_number`), then `not null`; `unique (enrollment_id, day_id)` is created and `unique (enrollment_id, day_number)` (`0058:173`) is dropped, because after a move two credits of one enrolment can carry the same number at credit time. `day_number` stays as the number when credited.
    - No existing column value changes: every identity is written into a new column, derived from the row's own program and number.

92. **The editor and the save (phase 2; an API change, recorded here).** The journey admin page loads each row's id; `DayRow` carries it. "Move up" and "Move down" swap the two rows' numbers, not their contents. "Insert a day here" creates a row and shifts later numbers; "Remove day" marks the row removed and shifts later numbers up. Clearing a day's module keeps the day (it becomes a rest day), as today. When one save clears module C on one day and sets C on another, the editor asks "Move instead?", because otherwise the day's identity stays behind and learners who completed C would meet it again as a new day (reading R17). The curriculum PUT saves through `journey_save_days(p_program_id uuid, p_days jsonb) returns jsonb` (plpgsql, `security invoker`, so the table's admin-write RLS still decides; `revoke execute ... from public, anon`, `grant execute ... to authenticated, service_role`): in one transaction it clears the numbers of the rows that move, writes the new numbers, inserts new rows, marks removed rows, and raises if an active row is left without a number. The publish writes `day_ids` from the rows with source `editor`, in the same pull request as the editor, so that a version is marked `editor` only when every move in it was a move of identity. The two never ship apart.

93. **Credit, launch and resume by identity.**
    - The context of a journey attempt is `journey:<enrolment id>:<day id>`. It never changes, and nothing needs to change it: when the day moves to another number or gets another course, the same context still names the same day.
    - `journey_record_completion` credits the attempt's own day (step 13). `journey_credit_state` and the twin decide completion and the open day by day id (steps 13, 45).
    - The launch (step 22) admits a day that is open, or credited, or **resumable**: its context holds an open official attempt. Without the third, a started day that moved behind the open day, or beyond the calendar, could not be reopened. While that attempt is open the launch resumes it; it does not open the day's current course beside it. The journey page lists resumable days under "Started earlier", a removed one included (the list is built in phase 6; from phase 2 a started day that is still in the pinned version is resumed from its day on the journey page, and a removed day, which has no place on the page until the list exists, by its launch link, which is how phase 2's gate exercises it). The access check that runs before the resolver admits these launches too (step 23): without that change it would refuse a started day that was removed, swapped or moved past the ceiling before the resolver was ever asked. A completed enrolment admits the resume of an attempt that was open when a publish completed it, and nothing else new (step 22).
    - Links carry the day id. A link with a number (an old bookmark, a CRM target issued before the change) is resolved through the pinned version's current numbering and then checked like any other; the check that the day holds the launched course stops it from opening a different day's module.
    - Readers that show days by number are fed through `creditedDaysFor` (step 45). The day funnel groups by day id and labels each with its current number. The CRM `days[]` looks credits up by day id and gains an additive `day_id`. The tenant LRS day activity keeps the number at credit time, so no statement already sent is renumbered and the profile needs no further change.

94. **The re-pin: one function, one transaction.** `journey_repin_enrollments(p_program_id uuid, p_version_id uuid, p_draft jsonb, p_dry_run boolean) returns jsonb` (plpgsql, `security definer`, service role only; no argument has a default) replaces the route's update and loop (step 47). It names its target, because before a publish the program's current version is still the old one, and a dry run with no target would compare old with old and show 0 everywhere. A real run names the version to pin (`p_version_id`, with `p_draft` null and `p_dry_run` false); the function raises unless that version belongs to the program and is its current version. A dry run passes `p_version_id` null and the would-be version as `p_draft` (`days`, `day_ids`, `days_total`, as the publish will freeze them; step 47) and changes nothing. Both compare each enrolment's pinned version with that target by the same code. It locks the program's active enrolments in id order; applies the gate below; sets `version_id` to the target on those it re-pins; writes the credits of a relaxed pass rule (step 95); calls the completion core with the origin `publish` (step 13); and returns the counts of step 47. With `p_dry_run` it returns the counts and changes nothing. Completed and reset enrolments are not selected. Today these are separate requests with no transaction.

    **The gate for versions published before the new editor.** The backfill reads history by slot. It cannot recover a move an admin made with the old editor: such a move swapped content between numbered rows, and reads now as two course swaps in place. For an enrolment whose pinned version is `slot_backfill`, identity alone would therefore still repeat or skip a module across such a move. So for those enrolments, and only those, the function re-pins only when the first day at which the old and new versions differ (course, required or not, the per-day pass rule, or a cut of `days_total`) comes after the learner's furthest day that holds a credit or any attempt. Otherwise the enrolment stays on its version; it is counted (`kept_on_version`), named in the publish confirmation, and offered the admin's existing reset-and-re-enrol. For those learners the owner's third sentence is not met by that publish: they keep everything they did and do not get the update. This is the one limit of the design. It concerns only enrolments that exist before the new editor ships. Production has none and is expected never to have one, because its first learner comes after the release; on staging they are test data. Q9 sizes it before the paste.

95. **The outcome in every edge the owner's four sentences cover.** Edits are made with the new editor and published to all enrolled learners. "Done" means the learner holds a credit for the day; "started" means an open official attempt; "ahead" means neither.

    | Edge | Done | Started | Ahead |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | A day removed (the day removed, its module cleared, or `days_total` cut below it) | The credit is kept and counts towards nothing. Nothing is retaken | The attempt stays resumable and can be finished. Its result is kept on the attempt and a credit row is written as history; neither counts towards the journey (R13). This holds also when the same publish completes the enrolment (every remaining required day is credited): the enrolment completes at the publish, the open attempt stays resumable in the completed enrolment (step 22), and its history credit is still written (step 13) | The day is skipped |
    | A day's course swapped | The credit stands. The update does not reach this learner (the owner's third sentence): the day keeps showing the course they completed, with its score, on every surface, its review opens that course, and the new course is not offered inside the journey | The learner finishes the course they started and that credits the day; the new course is not opened beside it (R13) | The new course |
    | A day inserted before the learner's current day | Completed days keep their credits under their new numbers | The started day, one number later, stays resumable; its completion credits it even though an earlier day is open | The inserted day has no credit and is the open day: the learner goes back to it. In calendar mode its date has passed, so it counts as behind at once and reminders follow (reading R15; the publish confirmation shows `sent_back`) |
    | Days reordered | Credits follow their days; a done day moved later stays done | Resumable wherever it moved, beyond the calendar ceiling too; credit has no ceiling (A30) | An uncredited day moved before the learner's furthest day becomes the open day |
    | A required flag changed | A rest day that gains a module is a new required day; nobody holds a credit for it. A mission day that becomes a rest day is a removed day (row 1). A per-day pass rule (step 46) made stricter or looser: an existing credit stands | A publish never makes a started day harder (R14): the attempt is credited when it satisfies the rule of the version it was launched under or the pinned version's (step 13). A rule made stricter does not fail work started before it; a rule relaxed helps at once. A result that satisfies neither blocks the day until a grant, as any fail does | The new rule. A rule relaxed on a day the learner already finished without passing: the re-pin writes the credit, because the day's official attempt now satisfies the rule (`credited_by_rule_change`); it never removes one |
    | A completed enrolment and a new day | Not re-pinned (step 47): it stays completed with its badge and certificate | Not applicable | Not applicable |
    | An attempt started before the republish and finished after it | Not applicable | Credited to its own day, whatever number or course the day has now; history if the day was removed. No check of the day's current course refuses it | Not applicable |

    One thing no publish controls: a course's own `pass_required` is not versioned. Changing it reaches every started and future journey day that inherits it at once, with no publish (`0089:46-51`, kept as the fallback of step 9). The course form says so.

    Against the owner's four sentences: (1) a completed day is never required again, in every row; (2) a started day loses nothing and is not restarted, in every row; (3) the update reaches every learner who has not completed the changed day, with the stated limit of step 94 and the two places where (2) and (3) pull against each other, for a learner who is mid-way through a swapped or removed day (R13) or a day whose pass rule the publish made stricter (R14), each decided for the started work by the owner's answer; (4) no attempt row and no credit row is updated or deleted by a publish, proven by the before-and-after comparison of the rehearsal and spec 69.

96. **What the day identity makes unnecessary.** No rule is keyed on the day number, no check compares an attempt's course with the day's current course at credit time, and the publish reports no count of days reopened or failed days moved: a moved day is not retaken, and a moved failed day keeps its context and its failed result, so it gives no new official attempt without a grant. What holds beside it: one rule in SQL and its TypeScript twin, the enrolment lock, the one-function completion, A30. Alternatives weighed for the owner's answer are in "Alternatives considered"; what the first design held is in "Review".

### V. Restore: Preview data after a restore, and the restore rehearsal (the owner's second and third messages of 2026-11-13)

97. **After a restore, nothing is deleted automatically; admins re-check Preview Reports (reading R27).** There is no expiry, so there is no cleanup to apply to a restored database, and no step of a restore makes a deleting call. The restore rehearsal calls the delete function once, as a dry run (step 98 (8)).
    - *What a restore does to Preview data.* A dump is a copy of a past day. A restored database holds every Preview row of that day again, so Preview data that an admin deleted after the dump was taken comes back. It also loses every `preview_purge_log` row written after the dump, because the audit rows live in the same database. Both are stated to the owner (reading R27).
    - *The runbook lines (executor, phase 9a, with the restore procedure).* `docs/ops/backup-restore.md`, Scenario 1, gains these lines before "Point the prod Worker at the new project": (i) "Restore the privileges. The dump carries no grant and no revoke. Run the grant and revoke statements collected from the migrations (the statements only; never paste again a migration file that the dump already holds), then run the privilege matrix that ends that file. It must pass before anything else is done." (step 98 says why); (ii) "If the database being replaced can still be read, export its `preview_purge_log` rows newer than the dump (ids and counts only) before it is retired, and keep the export with the restore's record, never in the public repository."; (iii) "A restore brings back Preview data that was deleted after this backup. Nothing removes it automatically. After the database serves, tell each organisation's admins to open Preview Reports and delete again what is no longer needed." The single-table Scenario 2 gains the same line (iii) for a restore of `course_attempts`, `xapi_statements`, `xapi_state` or `preview_runs`.
    - *Repeating a deletion, by hand.* Every audit row keeps the ids of the attempts and test runs it deleted (step 79), and the "Deletion history" list of step 99 shows each row the restored database still holds, with "Download ids". An organisation's admin deletes again in Preview Reports, through the same selections, dry-run summary and confirmation as any delete, using the history, a download kept earlier, or that organisation's part of the export of line (ii), which the operator hands to the admin, to see what had been removed. No operator and no script makes a deleting call, and nothing re-applies a logged deletion automatically; that is a possible later feature and is not planned.
    - *The gap this cannot close.* Deletions made after the last dump are known only from the export of line (ii) or from a download an admin kept. When the old database cannot be read and no download exists, those audit rows are lost and the admin decides again, from Preview Reports alone, what to delete. Production takes one dump a night, so the gap is at most the deletions of one day.
    - *A dump older than 0107.* It holds no row of a new kind and no delete function. Restoring it means pasting the later migrations again in their order. The runbook says so, because the first dumps after the release are of this kind.
    - *What it does not do.* Nothing runs against the restored database's Preview rows before it serves, and nothing reaches the dump itself. Whether old dumps are ever deleted is a general backup retention question for the owner, independent of Preview (reading R27).
    - *Proof.* The purge rehearsal of step 63 proves on seeded rows that the function deletes exactly its scope and writes the ids into the audit row. The restore rehearsal of step 98 proves line (i) on a real restored copy, and that the delete function is there and answers a dry run on it.

98. **The restore rehearsal: a gate of the production sitting.** The owner: "Please make a successful backup restore rehearsal mandatory before the production sitting. It should be carried out in an isolated environment, with the restore process and verification documented." Until now the proof of the backup was a green run and the restore drill (download, decrypt, completeness by counts of the file); a restore into a database has never been rehearsed (step 74).
    - *Isolated environment.* Not production and not staging. Staging is one shared database that every harness and the pull-request e2e job write to and purge, and production's rows must not be loaded into the test environment. Two environments qualify: (i) recommended, a separate throwaway database project, created empty by the owner in the vendor's dashboard for this rehearsal only and deleted at its end; this is Scenario 1 of the runbook, the same path a real disaster would take, and it has the platform's roles, `auth` schema and API layer, so the application can be run against it; (ii) a local Postgres of the dump's major version on the owner's machine. Option (ii) proves that the file decrypts, loads and counts, and nothing more: it has none of the platform's roles or API, so the application cannot be checked against it, and statements that name those roles may fail. The gate needs (i). If a throwaway project cannot be created, (ii) is recorded with "application health: not performed", and whether that opens the gate is the owner's decision at the sitting, stated beforehand. The Supabase CLI is not used in either (it is linked to production), and no local Supabase stack is planned.
    - *When, and with which files.* After the release candidate is fixed and before the sitting, with a production dump taken for the rehearsal by an on-demand run of the backup workflow, and with the candidate's migration files. An earlier rehearsal, for example now (proposal P12), is useful to find problems early and does not open the gate, because it cannot include the candidate's files.
    - *The process, in order, each step recorded.* (1) On-demand backup run, green. (2) Download, decrypt, decompress, as in the drill. (3) Load into the empty isolated project with `psql`; the number of errors is recorded and each is explained or the rehearsal fails. (4) Verification A, completeness: the row count of every table of `public`, and of `auth.users`, on the restored copy against the same counts read on production within the hour (production has no learners, so they are expected to be equal; any difference is explained); the counts of policies, triggers, functions, indexes and constraints against production's. (5) Verification B, privileges: the dump carries no owner and no grant statements (`--no-owner --no-privileges`, the runbook's operational notes), so the literal grants that migrations have carried since 2026-10-30 are not in it. The rehearsal checks the privilege matrix on the restored copy (a learner's and an admin's read of the tables the application reads, and a real 42501 for `anon`); what is missing is restored by the grant statements of the migrations, and the runbook gains that step with the list the rehearsal found. This is the most likely place for the rehearsal to find a real gap, and it is the reason a count of rows alone is not enough. **Lost revokes, not only missing grants.** `--no-privileges` drops every `revoke` as well as every `grant` (`.github/workflows/backup.yml:120-122`).
