A function that a dump re-creates gets PostgreSQL's own default, execute for `public`, so without its `revoke execute ... from public, anon, authenticated` every `security definer` function that the API can call by name would be open to `anon` and `authenticated` on the restored copy: `purge_preview_data`, both forms of `consume_grant`, `record_learning_activity`, `launch_context`, `journey_repin_enrollments` and the others of the matrix (0100 shows the dependence at `0100:578-581`). The same holds for table revokes and for the column-level revoke on `org_api_keys` (step 8, after-check A17). A check of table reads alone would pass while that exposure exists. So verification B is not a sample of reads. The dump of the rehearsal is taken before the sitting, so at this point the copy holds the objects up to production's last migration and none of 0107's: the functions a bare restore exposes here are the older ones, 0103's `consume_grant` and `record_revision_visit` and the others that carry a revoke today, and step 19's matrix, which lists 0107's objects, cannot run yet. Verification B is therefore in two parts. Part one, here: the privileges of `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` on every function, relation and column of `public` are read on the copy as loaded (`has_function_privilege`, `has_table_privilege`, `has_column_privilege`) and compared with the same read on production, as verification A compares counts; the differences are recorded (what the vendor's defaults give a new project is read here, not assumed); the fix below is applied; the comparison is read again and must show no difference, and one real call as `anon` and one as `authenticated` to `consume_grant` must each be refused with 42501. Part two, after (7) and before (8): step 19's privilege matrix for the objects of 0107 to 0109, with a call as `anon` and as `authenticated` refused with 42501 on every service-role-only function, `purge_preview_data` and `consume_grant` named first, and the `org_api_keys` column case. The fix is the grant and revoke statements of the migrations, collected in their order into one file by the executor in phase 9a and kept beside the runbook; statements only, never a migration file that the dump already holds, because step 4's never-paste-again list forbids re-pasting those files (a dump older than 0107 still gets the later migrations pasted once, in their order, as step 97 says). The file ends with its own check: for every revoke it holds, the privilege reads false for that role, and for every grant, true; that check is what the runbook means by "the privilege matrix" in a real restore, where production is gone and there is nothing to compare with. The proof that the collection is complete is the comparison of part one showing no difference on the restored copy, not the collection's own text. It is priced inside phase 9a's half day for the rehearsal, as an estimate: the grants and the revokes come from the same statements; if the collection proves larger, the executor reports it before the rehearsal. Dumping with privileges instead would change the backup workflow and its portability note, and is not planned here. The runbook's line (i) of step 97 is this fix followed by the matrix. (7a), after (7) and before (8): part two of verification B. (6) Verification C, the application's health: a production build of the code that runs on production, started on the owner's machine with the restored project's address and keys in its environment, never deployed anywhere: the health route answers 200, one owner account signs in, one admin page and one learner page render. It settles whether the dump carries the `auth` schema, which the runbook leaves open. (7) Verification D, the sitting's pastes on a copy of production's own data: 0105 and 0106 if production does not hold them, then 0107, then 0108 and 0109, each with its before- and after-checks, as step 74 orders them. This is added value the owner did not ask for and costs minutes: the sitting's files are proven against production's real rows before the sitting. (8) Verification E: nothing is purged after a restore (step 97). The check is that the delete function exists on the copy after 0107, that a dry run of it for one organisation, called with the service role and the fixed check actor of after-check A22 (the function raises on a null actor), answers with every count 0 (production has no preview) and writes nothing, and that the row counts of every table are the same before and after that dry run. That the function deletes what it should is proven on seeded rows by step 63, not here: no test row is seeded into a copy of production. (9) The copy is destroyed: the throwaway project is deleted, the plaintext dump and the decrypted file are removed from the machine, and both are recorded.
    - *What passes.* Every step done, every difference explained, the application healthy, the migrations' checks green, the dry run's answer clean, the copy destroyed. A failed step fails the rehearsal; it is repeated after the cause is fixed.
    - *Where the record lives.* The procedure is a new scenario in `docs/ops/backup-restore.md` ("Restore rehearsal"). The result is a dated section of the release runbook under `docs/release/`, where the v1.4.0 runbook already records the restore drill: dates, counts, pass or fail per step, the count of grant and revoke statements replayed and the matrix result before and after them, and the time the restore took. The repository is public, so the record holds counts and outcomes only: no project reference, no address, no key, no name, no row content.
    - *Price.* Half a day of executor work in phase 9a (the procedure, the count queries, the checklist, the record's form), inside phase 9a's days in step 72's table. The owner's time is not executor work: an estimate of two to three hours for the rehearsal itself, and a throwaway project for its length. Whether the vendor's plan allows another project at no cost was not read; the owner checks it when creating the project.
    - *After the release.* The gate is for this sitting. The rehearsal becomes the runbook's standing practice before any sitting that pastes a migration on a production with learners; a later record may fix its frequency.

### Risks and protections

| Risk | Protection |
|---|---|
| Learners redo the same module in several products | The owner's requirement; the course page shows one line per product; the editors warn on repeats |
| Two scored results for one module inside one journey | D1; per-day scope is the only alternative to a stuck day |
| A second product earns no points for a module already paid | D8(c); every product still shows its completion and score |
| A forged link yields a free official attempt | Step 22 before any write; a failed read refuses; unit matrix and spec 68 |
| Grading: an official attempt in one product changes another | The official rule filters by context first; `consume_grant` checks the grant's context; the rehearsal and spec 68 |
| Grading: a preview becomes a result | `preview` is its own context, excluded by the one rule in SQL and TypeScript; the CHECK ties it to `is_preview` |
| Grading: a staff member's or analyst's real work is lost as a preview | The owner's answers D7 and R6 (all three roles): Start is a real attempt; `preview-mode-sites.test.ts` |
| Grading: an admin's test clicks become official results on KIVO 2.0 courses | The owner chose this on 2026-11-13 as "an intentional change to my earlier KIVO 2.0 rule dated 6 November 2026". Admins who only test press Preview or start a test run; the launch bar says which mode is running |
| Grading: an admin's republish gives a learner a fresh official attempt on a failed day | Removed by the day identity: a moved day keeps its context and its failed result (section U). Only a course swap on a failed day opens a first attempt, of a different course |
| A journey enrolment that can never complete after a re-pin, with no badge, claim or email | One rule by day identity in the SQL completion, the SQL state and `computeJourneyState`: the open day is always one without a credit (steps 13, 45; section U); the rehearsal's re-pin fixtures, the parity check and spec 69 |
| Learners: an admin's republish repeats a completed day or loses a started one | The owner's A27 answer. Credits and contexts name the day's identity, which moves with the day; a started day stays resumable and credits on completion; the re-pin is one transaction with a dry run and counts in the confirmation; the rehearsal and spec 69 compare every attempt and credit row before and after (steps 93 to 95) |
| Learners enrolled before the new editor ships are kept on their version by a later publish | Step 94's gate, counted and named in the confirmation; expected 0 on production; Q9 sizes it on staging |
| A learner is sent back to an inserted day and shows as behind | Reading R15 for the owner; `sent_back` in the confirmation before the publish |
| Existing records: the purge deletes an official row | The selection predicate is repeated in the delete; a non-preview row cannot be selected, and neither can a by-role row (step 82); the isolation check sets aside and reports any attempt with an official side effect; the count assertion; the dry run; the audit row; the purge rehearsal compares every other table before and after; spec 75 (step 81) |
| Existing records: real learner work flagged as a preview by role is deleted | No by-role row is ever deleted, automatically or by an admin: no scope or reason of the delete function selects one; the database refuses the delete of a course, a version or a language that holds one (the recreated delete guards, step 7) and of a path or a program (the row's `no action` key), also for a delete made straight through the Data API; the routes answer 409; and the rows are listed read only. The rehearsal seeds by-role rows of each kind, finds every one in place after every purge call, and has the guard cases (steps 63, 82, 85; reading R26). An organisation's or a user's delete still removes them with everything else, as today, and the guards let the service role through, as today. Production expected 0 |
| A course that a staff member opened while launches were flagged by role can no longer be deleted (ADR 0003 decision 3 allowed it) | Stated to the owner in row L2 and in row 15 of "Conflicts with existing records". It is archived or deactivated instead. Production: no by-role row is expected. Staging: test courses; the test purges delete on the service client and are not affected; spec 50's case changes (step 62) |
| Existing records: a lifecycle rule on the backup bucket would shorten the backup history of official learner data | None is required by this record. The bucket keeps every dump, as the owner confirmed; a general backup retention rule is the owner's separate choice (reading R27). The on-demand backup before every sitting and the restore rehearsal are unchanged |
| A restored database is served with missing grants or with lost revokes (every service-role-only function callable by `anon` and `authenticated`) | The dump carries no grant and no revoke, so the runbook replays the migrations' grant and revoke statements and runs the whole privilege matrix before the database serves, with a 42501 required for `anon` and `authenticated` on every service-role-only function (step 97, line (i)); the restore rehearsal proves it on a restored copy (step 98). Nothing is purged on a restored database; the Preview data a restore brings back is the row below on that risk |
| A product delete goes on after a purge call that failed | Any error from either purge call answers 503 and nothing is deleted (step 85); `product-delete-order.test.ts` |
| An admin deletes Preview data by mistake, or deletes more than was shown | The dry-run summary and the confirmation before every delete, and the `selection_hash` check, so no preview and no test run is deleted that the summary did not show (a selected preview still in use can add rows of its own before the confirmation; the audit row holds the real counts, step 81); only `super_owner` and `admin` of the organisation may delete; official rows cannot be selected (steps 81, 99). A deleted preview cannot be brought back except by a restore |
| Preview data grows without limit, because nothing deletes it automatically | The owner's choice. The delete screen shows each organisation's totals and oldest row; no reminder is built (step 83) |
| A restore brings back Preview data that was deleted, and loses the audit rows written after the dump | Stated to the owner (reading R27). The runbook exports the newer audit rows when the old database can be read, admins re-check Preview Reports and delete again by hand, and the logged ids, which an admin can download, show what had been removed (step 97). The gap is at most one day's deletions |
| A purge called with a scope it does not know deletes too much or silently nothing | Exact scope shapes; 22023 on anything else (step 81); the route sends only the scopes of step 81 for the session's organisation, and `p_actor` is the session's user (step 99) |
| Privacy: Preview data, a tester's results included, stays for as long as no admin deletes it, and stays in backups after a manual delete | The owner's decision of 2026-11-13 (third message). It is isolated, shown to authorised users only, never sent outside (A35), and holds staff testers' results, not learners'. Backups are encrypted; whether old dumps expire is the owner's separate choice (step 83, reading R27) |
| Privacy: preview statements reach a tenant's LRS, where the LMS cannot delete them | Never forwarded, "Resend all history" included (decision A35); the source scan and spec 75 with forwarding on |
| Grading: a preview launched in Normal mode is read as a result | The database ties it to the `preview` context (CHECK and trigger); the one rule excludes it; the writer skips it; spec 75 asserts no official trace for all three roles (decision A32) |
| A tester takes a green test run as proof of emails, CRM or the Continue button | The run page says what it does not test; those are proven on staging by learner accounts and the nightly specs (step 89) |
| Privacy: a test run's header table is read by an official report | No-access RLS, service role only, no view over it, and the source scan of step 86 |
| The production sitting starts without a restorable backup | The on-demand green run within the hour, the repeated restore drill and a successful restore rehearsal in an isolated environment are gates (steps 74, 98) |
| Two last-day credits at the same moment both miss the completion | The enrolment row is locked before the credit insert (step 13); spec 69 runs two parallel requests |
| A new CRM key is version 1, so the first-learner gate would need SQL on production | The column default is 2 after the existing keys are labelled 1 (step 8, A28); after-check A17; spec 72 |
| A re-paste of 0107 marks failed writer rows as handled, or held SCORM rows as never sending | Backfills run only when their column is first added (step 4); the rehearsal re-pastes over rows the new code could have written |
| 0108 turns the nightly and every pull request's e2e red | The fixtures name `context_kind` first; the insert-sites guard scans the test folders; 0108 refuses within 24 hours of a derive firing (steps 20, 64) |
| A deploy on a database without 0107 fails launches, loses completion side effects and completes no journey at a publish to all | Decision A19: the paste-first rule of step 72; readers, crons and feeds keep their fallbacks |
| Privacy: a new view exposes every organisation's assignment dates | The terms and path views are `security_invoker` with literal grants; a cross-organisation read as `authenticated` returns 0 rows (steps 9, 18, 19) |
| Privacy: a super owner's session sets a CRM key's contract version | Table-level insert and update revoked from `authenticated`, the written columns granted back (step 8) |
| Grading: a pass that arrives after `completed` loses its rewards or credit | The settled-state marker re-runs the branch (step 12) |
| Points, credit or a webhook lost on an error | Fail-closed writer, the sweep scheduled from 0107, the Attention item. Emails are not retried (disclosed) |
| Duplicate emails | One claim per learner, product and instance, taken before the send (step 15) |
| Privacy: an analyst reads another organisation, or learner feedback | The service role only after `canViewReports`, with the organisation on every read; feedback stays `canManage` only (step 51) |
| Privacy: self-enrolment writes with the service role | Step 68 |
| Existing records | No row moved, no existing value changed; new columns only; before-counts equal after-counts; deletes refused |
| Yoddha reads "not started" for path-only learners in version 1 | The versioned feed, built in 9a and in the first release; the first-learner gate of step 74a keeps production without learners until Yoddha reads version 2; the agreement gates the tag; R4 if Yoddha cannot move |
| Reports on staging between 0107 and 0109 | The old-name views stay one row per learner (step 9); the remaining differences are listed in step 59 |
| A journey learner resumes an old attempt in the staging window | Staging only, days; the next journey launch repairs it (step 60) |
| The 0107 paste is refused | Q2 runs first; the owner's reset on staging; production reads 0 |
| A re-paste of an old file strips the new bodies | The never-paste lists (steps 4, 21, 75) |
| Workers limits on the heavier launch | `launch_context` is planned work, and the measured count gates phase 2 (step 70) |
| Workers limits on a publish to all enrolled learners | One set-based call and no email in the request (steps 47, 70) |
| Stale reads within one render | Step 25 and its test |
| Refresh time at scale | Separate budgets; the timed gate before 0109 |
| A preview's "abandoned" statement, or a staff member's preview in an official report, through a reader that never met the official rule (`lib/lrs/abandoned.ts`, Learner Analytics, the Report Card's engagement) | Each named in steps 49 and 80 with its filter; the widened source scan of step 62; spec 75's cases |
| A purge that fails silently, or is blocked for good by one official row on a preview | Every call is started by a person, so a call that fails is an error the route reports to that person and nothing is deleted: the manual route shows it, and a product delete answers 503 and does not go on (step 85). A breaching attempt is set aside and reported while the rest is deleted; the admin sees "kept for review" in the summary and after the delete, the audit row names it, and Preview Reports list it (step 81) |
| A preview that a course delete removed unseen | The three product-delete routes show the summary, then delete through the function with an audit row (step 85). The four cascades that leave no audit row are stated (reading R22) |
| Something deletes Preview data automatically after all (a later cron, a refresh function) | The source scan and the rehearsal case of step 81; after-check A23 reads both refresh functions' bodies |
| A started journey day refused before the resolver is asked, ended by a publish that completes the enrolment, or failed by a rule the publish made stricter | The access check admits it (step 23); a completed enrolment admits the resume and the history credit (steps 13, 22); the lenient pass rule (step 13, R14) |
| About 143 planned days (95.5 by the table), of which about 129 (86 by the table) come before production's first release (step 72) | The owner sees the figures, how they grew ("Superseded figures" in section "Review") and the dependencies before accepting (step 72); the owner chose R8 over A4; ways to shorten the elapsed time are proposals with their risks (section "Opportunities to finish sooner"); phases 1 to 4 are proven on staging first; the Overview and journey activities trail behind flags; the earlier-release alternative is named with what it gives up |

## Consequences

- A learner's result on a course is a result in a product. The Library, each Learning Path and each journey day keep their own official attempt, revision visits, grants, bookmark, score and status. Finishing a module in one place completes or unlocks nothing elsewhere.
- Every binding policy keeps its content and is scoped to the context. ADR 0017's guard is untouched: it is per attempt row.
- Course reports show the Individual product. ADR 0020's path arm and hold-back, on staging since 2026-11-11, never reach production as a served state.
- Admins, super owners and analysts learn for real when they press Start and test on purpose when they press Preview (D7; the owner's R6 answer of 2026-11-13, which replaces the owner's rule of 2026-11-06). A launch is never a preview by role. Preview has no Continue (the owner's A21 answer); a whole path or journey is tested in a test run (section T).
- Preview data is kept apart, shown only in Preview Reports, never counted, never sent anywhere, and kept until an authorised admin deletes it by hand, after a dry-run summary and a confirmation, with an audit row; nothing deletes it automatically, so it grows until someone deletes it. This is the first deletion of learner-data rows in the system and a named departure from `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 3, limited to preview data written by a Preview launch or a test run (section S). Rows flagged as previews by role before this record are preserved and can be deleted by nobody; a course, path, journey program or language that holds one cannot be deleted and is archived or deactivated instead, which the database enforces (steps 7, 82, 85). That covers every course a staff member opened while launches were flagged by role. ADR 0003 decision 3 let a course with only staff attempts be deleted; that ends for these rows. On production none is expected; on staging they are test courses (row L2 of "Decisions requested").
- Completion side effects have one writer that fails closed. A passing error no longer pays points or fires a webhook; it delays them until the sweep.
- Each product sends one completion email. cmi5 learners gain the course email; a granted retake no longer re-sends anything. One exception to D16, which the owner accepted on 2026-11-13 (A20): a journey that an admin's publish completes sends no email; it shows as completed everywhere, the dashboard included.
- Paths and journey programs with learner attempts cannot be deleted; they are archived.
- "Everyone in this org" learners have recorded enrolments, so reports, the Overview and the CRM can see them. Members who never start are not counted.
- The CRM contract has a version per key. Version 1's meaning changes on release; the agreement is a release gate, and Yoddha reading version 2 is a gate on the first production learner.
- The effort is 95.5 executor-days by the table for phases 0 to 9b with phase P, and about 143 to plan for; with journey activities it is 104 to 106, and about 156 to 159 to plan for (step 72); the plan said 50. Production's first release carries phases 1 to 7, 9a and P: 86 days by the table and about 129 to plan for (the plan: 25 for phases 1 to 4); the first learner follows the gate of step 74a. How the figures grew is in section "Review", under "Superseded figures".
- A journey day has one identity for life. A journey is complete when every required day of its pinned version holds a credit for that identity, checked in SQL and TypeScript alike. After an admin's publish to all enrolled learners, nobody repeats a completed day or loses a started one, and learners who have not completed a changed day get its new version (the owner's A27 answer). The journey editor moves, inserts and removes days as operations of their own, and the curriculum save and the publish go through two database functions.
- A language switch closes one product's open attempt and deletes nothing; an audit row records it (A24).
- Preview and test runs exist on production, as the owner's named exception to "No test or seed data on production" (R21, 2026-11-13): identifiable, open to authorised users only, without any official effect. Stated plainly: Preview data stays on production with no time limit, until an admin deletes it. That is a wider exception to `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 5, than the one the owner first allowed, whose fourth condition was "deleted within the 90-day maximum retention period"; the owner's third message of 2026-11-13 withdrew that condition, and the owner is asked to confirm the wider exception at acceptance (section "Decisions requested").
- The database backup bucket is not changed by this record: it keeps every nightly dump, as today, so a deleted Preview row stays in older dumps. A general backup retention rule is optional, independent of Preview, and the owner's to decide (step 83, reading R27). There is no preview retention figure and no settings table.
- A restore brings back Preview data that was deleted after the backup, and loses the audit rows written after it; nothing is purged, admins re-check Preview Reports and delete again by hand, and the logged ids, which can be downloaded, show what had been removed. A successful restore rehearsal in an isolated environment is a gate of the production sitting (steps 97, 98).
- A CRM key created after 0107 reads contract version 2; older keys stay on version 1 (A28).
- Between the phase 2 merge and the production sitting, a production tag of `main` is allowed only while production has no learner (decision A19).
- Binding on later work: a new reader of "done", "official", "in progress" or "revision" calls `contextResult`, `readContextResult` or a product's progress rule, never `computeScoring` or a raw completed-or-passed test; a new write of an attempt, request or visit names its context; a new column that carries a context references its parent `on delete no action`; a new completion side effect is added inside `record_learning_activity`; a new server-render re-read carries its own abort signal; a journey day is named by its id, never matched by its number; a new store that a launch can write is added to the table of step 80 with how a preview is kept out of it and how it is purged; no code deletes an attempt row directly except `purge_preview_data` (the cascades of an organisation and a user remain, and so do the two windows of a course's and a language's delete, for previews only; a route that deletes a course or a language deletes on the caller's session, never the service client, so the delete guards refuse through the cascade (step 7); a route that deletes a product refuses while it holds a by-role row, purges its preview data first and refuses while the purge sets a preview aside; and the language delete refuses while any preview exists, step 85); no scope of the delete function may select a by-role row; a new feed, export or forwarder excludes the `preview` context, and so does a new reader of raw `course_attempts` rows on an official surface; a new function carries a pinned `search_path`.

## Alternatives considered

- **The ADR 0023 draft as written (paths only).** Individual plus each path as contexts, keyed on `learning_path_id`, journeys reading the Individual result. Not chosen: a Library completion would still credit a journey day and rewrite the row; a course on two journey days stays stuck; previews share the Individual slot and stay previews by role; journey membership keeps cancelling points; classification still fails open; the SCORM webhook stays unlatched; 0105's path arm stays in course reports. Kept from it: the diagnosis, the per-context scope of ADR 0014, `contextResult` with the guard test, the index split, `no action` foreign keys, the paste-first order with an either-state check, its reader sweep as the build checklist. Changed from it: journeys become contexts (D1); its decision (h), "the standing result relocks", is replaced by D14; its decision (d), course reports in the context of enrolment, by D2; its decision (c), webhook for the Individual context only, by the per-organisation scope; its decision (g) by D7.
- **Three attempt tables, one per product.** Triples the runtime, the LRS feed and the CRM logic. Rejected.
- **Filtering only in reports.** One product would still complete another. Rejected.
- **A stored `learning_records` table with a cached official pointer.** A writer on the launch hot path, a circular foreign key and a cache that can drift, for nothing a view over enrolment and attempts does not give. Rejected.
- **A generated key without an explicit kind.** A careless insert would silently become an Individual attempt. Rejected in favour of `context_kind` with the CHECK.
- **Nulling the tags on old preview rows.** An update of existing attempt columns. Rejected (step 6).
- **A one-time `side_effects_at` stamp.** Loses a pass that arrives after `completed`. Rejected (step 12).
- **Changing the grain of `v_course_attempt_summary` in 0107.** Six LIVE matviews would count a learner in two statuses until 0109. Rejected for new-name context views (step 9).
- **`notification_log` or `xp_events` as the email latch.** Neither can serve (step 15).
- **Points per product instance (D8 a).** Farmable by repeating a module in open or recreated paths; needs a ledger rule change. Declined by the owner's choice of (c).
- **Freezing the journey deadline per enrolment (D17 a).** Declined by the owner.
- **A feature flag on the core.** Two rules on one table. Rejected (step 72).
- **A webhook scope per API key.** The webhook has no key when it fires. Rejected for a per-organisation setting.
- **Run instances for yearly re-assignment now.** Deferred (D5); `path_run:<id>` stays an additive key later.
- **Journey republish, weighed against the owner's four A27 sentences (2026-11-13).** (C0) Credit per day number, round 3's rule: repeats a completed module after an insert or reorder and orphans a started day. Rejected by the owner. (C2) Keep number-keyed rows and record a day map at each re-pin: to be exact the map needs the same day identity as the chosen design; every one of about 13 readers must then translate through the chain of maps; one day has different context keys before and after a publish; a moved failed day still opens a fresh official attempt without a grant. Rejected. (C3) No identity; re-pin only the enrolments whose first changed day lies after everything they did: protects completed and started work with almost no schema, but a learner past an earlier change never gets that publish or any later one, which fails the third sentence for most learners of a running journey. Kept only as the gate for versions published before the new editor (step 94). (C4) Match a credit to any day that holds the same course: fails when a day's course is swapped (the completed day would be asked for again) and is ambiguous when a course repeats. Rejected. (C5) No re-pin of enrolled learners at all: fails the third sentence for everyone. Rejected. Chosen: the day identity of section U, the only candidate that meets all four sentences in every edge.
- **A stable day id read from today's `journey_days.id` with no editor change.** That id is a slot id tied to the number (step 90); it would be the per-number rule again. Rejected.
- **A deferrable unique constraint on (program, day number) for the save.** It makes one-statement swaps possible, but `ON CONFLICT` cannot use a deferrable constraint, so the save would need a function anyway; the function with nullable numbers needs no constraint change. Not chosen.
- **Previews with no stored result (Browse everywhere, nothing kept).** The round-3 position. Replaced by the owner's R7: results are wanted, apart, for testing.
- **A preview-only copy of the progress tables (preview credits, preview completions).** A second set of writers and tables to keep in step, and rows a wrong join could count. Rejected for progress derived from preview attempts by the same pure functions (section S).
- **A test-learner flag on real accounts.** Every official reader would have to honour a second exclusion rule. Rejected (section T).
- **The purge as TypeScript deletes, or as a new cron route.** The first breaks the rule that app code never deletes attempts and cannot reach two tables without new grants; the second adds a schedule line that fires on production before its route is deployed. Rejected for one SQL function called only from the manual delete route of step 99 and the product-delete routes of step 85 (step 81). Rounds 4 and 5 called it from the nightly refresh as well; round 6 removed that call.
- **A separate migration for the preview objects.** It would add a file to the sitting whose order the owner fixed under D3. Not chosen: they are in 0107.
- **Sweeping today's by-role preview rows with the rest.** Some are real results (step 82). Rejected in round 4. Round 5 listed it as option (c) of a clarification on A33; that clarification is closed since round 6, because nothing is swept. Reading R26 holds instead: by-role rows are preserved and nothing selects them.
- **Automatic deletion of Preview data at a retention limit.** Rounds 4 and 5 designed it on the owner's first answers of 2026-11-13 ("automatically delete Preview data after a maximum retention period of 90 days"): a nightly run of the delete function from the report refresh, a settings table with a live retention and a backup lifetime tied so that no copy outlived the limit, a lifecycle rule on the database backup bucket, a purge on every restored database, a platform owner's retention page, and a gate on production's first course. Rejected by the owner's third message of 2026-11-13: "Please remove the automatic 90-day deletion feature completely." None of it is built; Preview data is deleted by hand only (section S).
- **Re-applying a logged deletion: a scope that takes a list of ids, "Delete these again" on a history row, "Upload ids", and a typed total as the confirmation of a wide delete.** Round 6 added them so that a deletion could be repeated exactly after a restore. Deferred in round 7: the owner asked for a dry-run summary, a confirmation and an audit record, and these go beyond that. The audit row still keeps the deleted ids and "Deletion history" offers "Download ids", so a deletion can be repeated by hand. Re-applying deletions automatically is a possible later feature and is not planned.
- **Letting admins delete by-role rows that nothing official rests on** (a by-role scope, a test of what protects a row, a list of deletable earlier staff attempts). Round 6 designed it as reading R26. Not built in round 7: the owner wrote "preserve", A36 limits deletion to Preview and test data, and some by-role rows are real learner work with no result yet, which such a test would let go. It is the one open question to the owner; on a "yes" it returns as a separate, priced step (section "Decisions requested").
- **A retention setting per organisation, capped by a global figure** (weighed for finding 7 of round 5). Moot since round 6: there is no retention figure, no settings table and no limit to cap. Round 5 had not chosen it, as a second table and a second rule for a need nobody had stated.
- **The restore rehearsal on staging, or on a local database alone.** Staging is shared and purged by harnesses, and must not hold production's rows; a local database cannot run the application. Not chosen for the gate (step 98).

## Conflicts with existing records

The owner asked on 2026-11-13 to be told of any conflict with an existing ADR, a database rule or a previously approved requirement. Only confirmed conflicts are listed, each with an existing ADR, a database rule or an approved requirement. Each was read in the record, migration, test or runbook named (rows 1 to 8 and 10 to 15), or in the code as the fact read cites it (row 9). The table gives each row's current state. "Resolved" means this record states how; what the owner is still asked is in "Decisions requested".

| # | Conflicts with | Why | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 3: "Learner history is never rewritten or deleted: attempts, statements, stored package bytes" | The owner asks that an admin can delete preview data at any time. Preview data is attempt rows and statements | Resolved by the owner (A36, 2026-11-13: "I agree to allow deletion of Preview data, with a dry run before deletion and an audit record for every deletion. This permission must apply only to Preview/test data."): a departure limited to preview data of the three new kinds, by hand only, fenced as step 81 says. Rows flagged by role before this record are never deleted (step 82). This record's own principle 5 and its out-of-scope line say the same. No official row is ever deleted |
| 2 | The owner's rule of 2026-11-06 (ADR 0006 note (r)): "Only Super Owner and Admin accounts should not generate official course results, scores or completions on KIVO 2.0 courses"; and ADR 0006 step 17 (a launch by the three staff roles is a preview) | R6: Start by those roles is recorded "exactly like a normal learner's attempt" | Resolved by the owner, who called it "an intentional change to my earlier KIVO 2.0 rule dated 6 November 2026". The note on ADR 0006 says so; step 17's role rule ends when this record is accepted |
| 3 | ADR 0006 step 17 and note (r): a preview launches in Browse, and a KIVO 2.0 package in Browse stores no score | R7 asks Preview Reports for preview scores and results, and a test run needs a step's result to unlock the next | Resolved by the owner (A32, 2026-11-13: "Preview should run in normal course mode and store scores and results"): previews launch in Normal mode and are kept unofficial by their context. Step 17's Browse rule ends for previews when this record is accepted |
| 4 | ADR 0019 decision 3 (the owner, 2026-11-10): "Continue shows on a preview launched from a path, and the three preview roles skip the wait" | A21: "Preview mode should not have a Continue button" | Resolved by the owner's answer. Decision 3 ends on both halves when this record is accepted; the note on ADR 0019 says so |
| 5 | ADR 0005 D1 and its guard `tests/unit/alds-tier-read-sites.test.ts`: no grading, LRS, CRM or integration code reads `is_preview` or `launch_mode` | Keeping previews out of official results, the tenant LRS and the CRM feeds needs those paths to tell a preview apart | Resolved: they read `context_kind`, which the database ties to `is_preview`; only the preview code names `preview_kind`. The guard as written matches only the names `is_preview` and `launch_mode`, so it would not notice these reads. Its one amendment is stated in step 62: check (ii) is unchanged, a new check limits `preview_kind` and `preview_run_id` to the preview code, `context_kind` is not guarded by name, and the exclusions are asserted positively by `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts`, so D1's intent stays enforced and is not bypassed by a new column name. `launch_mode`, the tiers and the manifest stay unread. The note on ADR 0005 is widened from "this one purpose" to these readers |
| 6 | The database rule that `xp_events` is append-only for every role (`0053:129-146`) | Closed on 2026-11-13. It conflicted only with a time limit on Preview data, which the owner withdrew | No conflict. New previews write no points row; points rows of by-role previews stay (step 82) |
| 7 | The 0100 backfill (`0100:456-467`) and ADR 0006 note (r) ("historic rows are never rewritten") | "Preview" on older rows means "held a staff role when 0100 was pasted", real learner work included; a delete of preview data that reached them would remove it | Resolved: such rows are labelled `legacy_role`, preserved, and never selected by the delete function, in the owner's words "Please preserve the old staff records" (step 82; reading R26); counted by Q10; the owner decides on any count above 0 on production |
| 8 | The backup practice on record (`docs/ops/backup-restore.md`: dumps are "kept until you delete"; no lifecycle rule stated) | Closed on 2026-11-13. It conflicted only with a time limit on Preview data, which the owner withdrew | No conflict, and no lifecycle rule is asked. Stated instead: a deleted Preview row stays in older dumps, and a restore brings it back (steps 83, 97; reading R27) |
| 9 | The tenant LRS feed as built and documented (`lib/lrs/enqueue.ts:69-102`, `lib/lrs/sweep.ts`, `docs/LRS_ANALYTICS_PROFILE.md`: every stored statement and every attempt is forwarded; first enable and "Resend all history" send all history). A shipped behaviour and a tenant-facing document, not an ADR | The LMS cannot delete from a tenant's LRS, so a forwarded preview could never be deleted there | Resolved: previews are never forwarded (decision A35). The feed no longer carries every attempt; the tenant-facing document says so in phase 2. Statements forwarded from by-role previews before this are an exception (step 82) |
| 10 | The owner's D1 (2026-11-12): the journey context is `journey:<enrolment>:<day>`, read until now as the day number | A27's four sentences cannot be met with the number in the key: a moved day would be a new context and the started attempt would be orphaned | Resolved by the owner (R16, 2026-11-13: "a Journey day must have a stable identity independent of its display position or sequence number"): "day" in D1 is the day's identity. D1's substance is unchanged: a context per enrolment and day, two results when a course repeats |

| 13 | ADR 0004 step 9 and ADR 0014 step 18 (the language switch reads every open attempt of the learner on the course) | A24: only the current product's attempt | Resolved by the owner's answer; both notes say so |
| 14 | `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 5: "No test or seed data on production" | The owner asks for Preview Reports "in the LMS for testing and validation", and this record makes Preview and test runs available on production, where what they write is data made for testing | Resolved by the owner as a named exception (R21, 2026-11-13: "I agree to allow Preview and test runs on production, as an exception to the no-test-data-on-production rule"), with three conditions that stand: separate from official data and without official effect, identifiable, open to authorised users only. The fourth, "deleted within the 90-day maximum retention period", was withdrawn by the owner's third message of the same day. So Preview data stays on production with no time limit, until an admin deletes it: a wider exception to principle 5 than the one first allowed. The owner is asked to confirm it at acceptance (row L3 of "Decisions requested"). No test fixture, seed row or test-learner account is allowed on production by it |
| 15 | ADR 0003 (accepted 2026-10-16) decision 3 with its step 10 and its consequence "Versions with only staff attempts remain deletable"; ADR 0005, whose step 10 and 0100 build text replaced the guards' learner clause by `not a.is_preview`, so that a preview attempt "no longer blocks a delete"; the guard bodies of `0100:511-576`, which count only attempts that are not previews; and the test of that rule, spec 50's case "staff-only attempts do not block: course C deletes" (`tests/bot/lifecycle/50-record-guard.spec.ts:107-119`) | By-role rows are never deleted (row L2, built as "no"). They have been `is_preview` rows since 0100 (`0100:456-467`), and the 0100 guards let a course, a version or a language that holds one be deleted, with the row cascading away, as ADR 0003 decision 3 intended; 0107 only labels them `legacy_role`. Under the never-delete rule that cascade must stop. 0107 therefore recreates both guards to count by-role rows (step 7). A course whose only attempts are by-role rows could be deleted and no longer can. By-role rows are those flagged by role until the Start and Preview buttons serve; on staging the old launch code keeps writing them between the 0107 paste and phase 2 (step 60) | Resolved in this record, and it follows the owner's answer to row L2: such a course, version or language is archived or deactivated, not deleted. From phase 2 a staff member's Start is a real attempt and blocks a delete like any learner's (R6); only a Preview or a test run does not block, and the product delete purges it with an audit row (step 85). ADR 0003 decision 4, the service-role exemption, is unchanged, so the test purges work as before. Spec 50's case changes in phase 1 and again in phase 2 (step 62). A dated note on ADR 0003 says so and takes effect on acceptance. Production: expected to affect nothing. Under a "yes" to L2 the guards would count only the by-role rows that are protected |

No row is left for the owner to choose. Row 14 carries one confirmation asked at acceptance. Row 7 rests on reading R26, with the one open yes or no on by-role rows (row L2 of "Decisions requested"); row 15 follows from the same answer. The owner's third message of 2026-11-13 changed parts of the owner's own earlier answers R7, R21, A34 and A33, as the Context says; that is the owner's change and not a conflict with a record.

The restore rehearsal conflicts with nothing: it adds a gate. `launch_mode` stays unread by every reader, the delete function included.

Not conflicts with an accepted record: this record's own first design of the journey republish (never accepted; section U holds the current one); the column list in the note this branch wrote on ADR 0009 (D6's rule is unchanged and wider; the note says so); a test-learner account on production, which would meet principle 5 ("No test or seed data on production") and is therefore offered for staging only (section T).

Checked and found not to conflict: ADR 0009 decision 6 (it declined a service-role write-guard trigger; this record adds none for deletes); ADR 0017 (the fail-to-pass guard is per attempt row and previews are outside the official rule); ADR 0021 (feedback stays `canManage` only; Preview Reports show a tester's own answers, not learner feedback); D3 (no migration file is added to the sitting); the content bucket's object lock (no preview data is written there); A30 (no floor or ceiling at credit time; the day-identity rule relies on it). ADR 0003 was read in full for row 15; its other decisions (the backups, write-once statements and versions, the unique prefix, the service-role exemption) are not changed by this record.

## Opportunities to finish sooner (proposals, not applied)

The owner asked for ways to finish sooner "without compromising any approved requirement, functionality, data accuracy, security, testing, performance, deployment safety or quality of work", shared with their impact and risks "before changing the agreed scope". None of these is applied: the phase table, the gates and the scope stand as written until the owner says otherwise. No proposal removes scope, a test, a review, the rehearsal or a gate.

**First, what the figures are.** The table's days are effort. The planning figure is effort times one and a half. Neither is elapsed time: both add the phases one after another, as if one executor did everything. The dependencies of step 72 allow more: phases 1 and 2 must come one after the other (38.25 days), and after phase 2, phases 3, 4, 6, 7 and P need only phase 2, while phase 5 needs phase 3 (the chain 1, 2, 3, 5 is 49.25 days). The longest chain is 1, 2, 7, 9a: 55.25 days by the table (about 83 to plan for), against 86 (about 129) end to end. The other 30 days or so are work that can run beside the chain if people and the shared resources allow. Those shared resources set the real limit: one staging database on which only one harness may run at a time; the owner's merges, staging deploys and pastes; independent review of every pull request; one nightly run a day for "two green nights"; and Yoddha's own pace. One thing on the serial path no executor can shorten: the owner's restore rehearsal before the sitting (step 98). The figures of this paragraph are step 72's. The figures in the table and the combinations below were worked out on an earlier table, in which the release was 85 days and the longest chain 54.75; they were not recomputed and are about a day low. Each is marked where it stands, with step 72's figure beside it where step 72 gives one.

Savings are in table days unless marked elapsed, and they do not add up item by item.

| # | Proposal | Saves | Needs | Risk | Owner's word needed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Run phases 3, 4, 6, 7 and P side by side once phase 2 is merged; 5 after 3; 9a after 3, 6 and 7 | Up to about 30 days of elapsed effort on the release (85 down to about 55 to 61 on the earlier table; step 72's table has 86 for the release and 55.25 for the longest chain) | Three or four executors; one owner per shared file with a merge order (the launch page, the journey page and program route, `attempt-kind.ts`, the CRM feeds are each touched by several phases); two independent reviewers; a staging-run lock; merges batched daily | Integration mistakes between streams in shared files, which could mis-state a product's result: held down by daily rebases and by re-running the guard and parity tests on every merge. More pushes mean more `qa-*` purges on staging. Review quality under load | No for the method; yes if it changes who works on what |
| P2 | Phase 1 in two streams, SQL and TypeScript, joined by the parity test | 3 to 5 days elapsed | The answer shapes of `contextResult`, `launch_context` and the path views frozen first (the record fixes most of them) | The SQL rule and its twin drift until the parity test runs; the parity test gates the second merge. 0107 stays one file and one transaction | No |
| P3 | Write phase 2 and phase 3 code on stacked branches while 0107 is in review, and merge only after the staging paste | 2 to 4 days elapsed | 0107's interfaces frozen after its review | Rework if the review changes an interface; those branches' checks stay red until the paste. Nothing merges before the paste | Yes: it starts code before 0107 is pasted, though not before acceptance |
| P4 | Build 9a in two parts: the version 2 shape (contract version, strict version 1, `steps[]`, `days[]`, `path_id`, `context`, `released`) right after phase 2; the fields that phases 6 and 7 fill (journey status and score, revisits per product) after them | 2.5 to 3.5 days elapsed on the release, and much more on the first learner: Yoddha can build against version 2 on staging weeks earlier. As written, version 2 exists on staging only when 9a deploys, and 9a is last, so all of Yoddha's build time falls after the release | The version 2 field names frozen in the addendum first; a staging key created after 0107 | Yoddha builds against fields whose values arrive later; the addendum must list them. Spec 72 asserts the shape both times | Yes: it splits a phase of the agreed table |
| P5 | Open the conversation with Yoddha now on the addendum and the version 2 tables, not at acceptance (the plan said "as soon as you answer D11", answered 2026-11-12) | Up to about 6 days elapsed at the end of 9a (the last notice took about six days to agree) | The owner sends the draft; the lines that sections S and U touch are marked pending | A second round with Yoddha if the addendum changes after it is agreed | Yes: the owner acts |
| P6 | Take the 0108 paste out of the path between phases 2 and 3: keep it a phase 2 exit item done alongside, pasted when its preconditions hold, always before 0109 on staging | 1 to 2 days elapsed | The phase 2 gate reworded | The paste-window triggers stay longer on staging and could hide a missed insert site at run time; their firings are logged and the source guard is static. Production is unaffected | Yes: it rewords a gate |
| P7 | Batch the owner's staging sessions: the Q counts, the reset, the first merge, the 0107 paste and the fixtures merge in one; 0108 in the first session after its 24 hours; 0109 with the phase 7 merge | 1 to 3 days elapsed | Two to three hours of the owner's time per session; no harness running | A long session; a failed check mid-way stops it at a defined point (the reverse script; staging is test data) | Yes: the owner's time |
| P8 | Run hand-run specs against a local production build of the branch on the staging database, as the nightly does, without waiting for a staging deploy before each gate; the deployed build stays the target for 0108's precondition and the 9b proof | 1 to 4 days elapsed over about eight gates | The staging-run lock; the staging keys on the executor's machine, outside the checkout | Security: the staging service key on more machines; keep it on the fewest, never in a log. A local build does not exercise the hosting platform's CPU limit. The measured subrequest count of step 70 is already taken on a local build, branch by branch, and that count is the phase 2 gate; a deployed build may be checked as well. The 9b proof stays on deployed builds | No; each gate note records its target |
| P9 | Admit specs 68 to 73 and 75 to the nightly by a dispatched run on the day each passes by hand. (The nightly's longer timeout, or two jobs one after the other, is not part of this proposal: step 73 requires it and phase 2 prices it) | Half a day to a day per spec | The CI change of step 73 already merged | A dispatched run is not a night: "two green nights" stays nights | No |
| P10 | Run 9b's staging proof and the two green nights on the release candidate before the production sitting | 2 to 4 days elapsed to the first learner | `main` frozen from the candidate to the tag, or a tag that differs only in documents | A fix during the freeze restarts the two nights; phase 8 and G wait on their branches meanwhile. The proof is then on the very code the tag carries, which is the stronger check | Yes: it moves 9b's proof before the sitting and freezes `main`; step 74a's conditions are unchanged |
| P11 | Build the timed-refresh harness and draft and rehearse 0109 alongside phases 2 to 6; paste 0109 only with phase 7 | 1.5 to 3 days off the end of phase 7 | A local Postgres for the harness | Local timing is not hosted timing; record the hardware. 0109 may change when phase 7's loaders are reviewed. Pasting it early would end 0107 re-pastes on staging, so it is not pasted early | No |
| P12 | Verify the production backup and repeat the restore drill now, and write the sitting runbook from the v1.4.0 one during 9a | Days, if a broken backup or drill would otherwise surface at the sitting; a quarter day of 9a | The owner runs the workflow and the drill | None to data (the drill is read-only); the restore target stays private. A restore rehearsal is a gate (step 98); one made now finds problems early, the missing grants above all, and does not replace the gate's, which needs the candidate's files | Yes: the owner acts |
| P13 | Smaller pull requests along boundaries the record already draws, reviewed in parallel (the journey twin, its readers and the publish call stay one pull request) | 1 to 3 days of review time | Two reviewers and a merge order | More merges and more e2e purges on staging; every intermediate state of `main` must be consistent | No |
| P14 | Withdrawn on 2026-11-13: reading the backup bucket's retention and setting a lifecycle rule early. No lifecycle rule is needed for Preview data, and the owner has confirmed the bucket (step 83); see Review | | | | |

What the combinations come to, as arithmetic on the table's days under the staffing named, not as measurements: P1 alone, a release chain of about 55 days (plan for about 82) with three executors; P1 with P4, about 51 to 52; P1 with P2, P3, P4 and P11, about 40 to 45 (plan for about 60 to 68) with four. These are the earlier table's figures. On step 72's table the chain under P1 alone is 55.25 days (plan for about 83); the other combinations were not recomputed, because their working is not in this record. The elapsed savings of P5 to P10, P12 and P13 come on top and are calendar days, not table days.

Risks the parallel streams add, whichever proposals are taken: a branch cut before the fixtures commit restarts 0108's 24 hours (now a precondition, step 20); the production sitting needs a push freeze across every stream (step 74); concurrent e2e runs purge each other's `qa-*` data.

Not proposed, because each would compromise something the owner listed: a first release split in two (declined, A4); a flag on the core or 0107 in several files; specs or two nightly jobs at once on one staging database; dispatched runs in place of the two nights; pasting 0109 early on staging; skipping the rehearsal or the independent review; leaving the manual delete or the filters out of the first release.

## Decisions requested from the owner

D0 to D17 are answered (the Context holds the owner's words) and are not asked again. Accepting this record accepts every row below as written unless the owner objects to one; steps may be excluded individually (ADR 0001 decision 2).

**What the owner is asked.** Three things, and nothing else is open: (1) the acceptance of this record; (2) one yes or no, row L2; (3) one confirmation, row L3.

**How to read the table.** It is one table and holds the current state only.
- "Answered" rows carry the owner's words and the date. They are the owner's rule and hold nothing.
- "Reading, object if wrong" rows say how this record applied the owner's words where they leave room. Each is built as written unless the owner objects before the phase that builds it: R15, the scope part of R18, R20 and R26 before phase 1 (they are in 0107); R17 and the wording part of R18 before phase 2; R9, R12 and R25 before phase P; R19 before the production sitting; R27 before the sitting's runbook is written in phase 9a; R24 belongs to the build retry's own pull request. R1 to R5 and R22 name no phase (step 72).
- Rows L1 to L3 are the lead's decisions of 2026-11-13 on the scope of the delete: one stated, one asked, one to confirm.
- Rows A1 to A41 that do not say "Answered" are decisions the architect made and the owner has not; the cell gives the recommendation taken and its consequence or alternative.
- "Withdrawn" and "Closed" rows keep their number, because other records cite it, and say in one line what they were.
- Rows A28 to A30 follow A41: the numbers, not the order, identify them.

|---|---|---|
| L1 | **Lead's decision, object if wrong: the delete does what the owner asked and nothing more.** The owner asked for a manual deletion option, a dry-run summary, a confirmation and an audit record (who, when, how many). Built: those, the deleted ids in the audit row, and a read-only "Deletion history" with "Download ids". Not built: a scope that takes a list of ids, an upload of ids, a re-apply of a logged deletion, and a typed confirmation of a wide delete (section "Alternatives considered") | After a restore an admin repeats a deletion by hand, with the downloaded ids to show what had been removed (step 97). Re-applying a logged deletion automatically is a possible later feature and is not planned. The figures of phases 1 and P in step 72 are priced without the parts not built, as the architect's estimates. Alternative: build them; they return as a separately priced step |
| L2 | **Answered by the owner's acceptance of 2026-11-13 ("accept"): no, the built default.** The question was: "Allow admins to delete by-role rows that nothing official depends on (no result, no used grant or request, no points, no day credit, no completion record, no webhook)?"** By-role rows are the earlier staff attempts that were classified as Preview by the user's role (`legacy_role`, step 82): every launch by an admin, a super owner or an analyst until the Start and Preview buttons serve. **What "no" means for deleting content, stated plainly:** any course or language that a staff member opened in that time, and any Learning Path or journey program that such a row names, can never be deleted again by an admin. It can only be archived or deactivated. Until now a course whose only attempts were staff attempts could be deleted (ADR 0003 decision 3) | **Recommended, and what is built: no.** No by-role row can be deleted by anyone: the delete function never selects one, Preview Reports show them in the read-only list "Kept earlier staff attempts", and a course, path, journey program or language that holds one is not deleted (409; archive or deactivate instead). The database enforces it, also against a delete made straight through the Data API: 0107 recreates the two delete guards so that they count by-role rows, and a path or a program is held by the row's own key (steps 6, 7). **Where it is felt.** On production: expected nowhere. Production has no courses, so no by-role rows are expected (Q10 is expected to read 0; no database was read; a count above 0 goes to the owner before the paste), and 0107, the tag and 0108 are one sitting, so none can be written afterwards. On staging: test courses, namely every course a staff member has opened and every one opened until phase 2 serves there. The test purges still work, because they delete whole test organisations with the service role, which the guards let through as before. After that, on both: a staff member's Start is a real attempt and blocks a delete like any learner's (R6); a Preview or a test run does not block the database guard, and a product delete removes it with an audit row. Two refusals remain for preview data, both in the routes (step 85): a preview set aside for review stops a course, path or program delete (409) until the owner decides on it, and any preview on a language stops the language delete (409) until an admin deletes that preview data by hand in Preview Reports. This changes ADR 0003 decision 3 for by-role rows (row 15 of "Conflicts with existing records"; spec 50's case changes, step 62). Why "no": the owner wrote "Please preserve the old staff records", A36 limits deletion to "Preview/test data", and some by-role rows are real learner work with no result yet, which nothing on the row tells from an abandoned test. **If the answer is yes:** a by-role scope and a test of what protects a row are added to 0107's function, with a list of deletable rows in Preview Reports; the guards then count only the by-role rows that test protects, reading the same test, so a course whose by-role rows are all unprotected could be deleted again, after the route removed those rows with an audit row. It is a separate step of about three quarters of a day (an estimate, step 72), and the answer must then come before phase 1. On production the list is expected to be empty either way |

ADR 0004 step 9 and ADR 0014 step 18 are read per context. | A27 | Journey republish and a learner's existing progress. **Answered by the owner on 2026-11-13, against the recommendation then made** (credit per day number, under which a moved day might have to be retaken; it is not built) | The owner's words: "I would prefer the alternative approach that protects learners' existing progress when an Admin republishes a Journey", with four expected behaviours (quoted in the Context) and the priority "that an Admin's Journey updates must not unexpectedly reset or repeat work that learners have already completed or started". **The owner asked whether the architecture supports this. Yes, with a day identity added in 0107** (new columns on `journey_days`, `journey_versions`, `journey_day_progress`, `course_attempts` and `attempt_requests`, a relaxed `journey_days` key, three new functions (`journey_save_days`, `journey_repin_enrollments`, `journey_attempt_satisfies`) and a second form of `consume_grant`, an editor and API change), one limit (step 94's gate for learners enrolled before the new editor ships) and readings R13 to R17. In short: a journey day gets an identity that it keeps when the admin moves it; credits and attempt contexts name that identity; a started day stays resumable; no row is re-tagged (section U). The outcome in every edge is step 95. It is applied in steps 4, 5, 7, 8, 10, 13, 18, 19, 22, 45 to 47, 60, 61, the tests and rehearsal of steps 62 and 63, spec 69, criterion 19, section L, the risks and the Consequences. What remains for the owner about it: readings R15 and R17 (R13, R14 and R16 are answered), and the one limit, step 94's gate for learners enrolled before the new editor ships. Five points of the design, stated: a learner who completed a day whose course was later swapped is not offered the new course (the third sentence, taken as written; step 22); a started day survives a publish that completes the enrolment (R13); a stricter pass rule does not fail started work (R14); the access check admits a started or credited day (step 23); the re-pin's dry run reads the draft (step 94). Its cost is inside the days of step 72's table. The alternatives weighed are in "Alternatives considered" |
| A31 | A test run's header row, `preview_runs` | Preview progress is derived from preview attempts only. One preview-only table holds each test run's ids and dates: which path, or which journey program and version, whose run, and its simulated date. It holds no result and no progress, apart from the activity days marked done inside a journey run (decision A41, from phase G), is not an enrolment, is service role only, and is purged with the run (step 79). It is the one exception to "a test run writes attempt rows only"; without it two test runs of one path could not be told apart and a journey run would have no version or date to read. Alternative: carry the path or program on every preview attempt and have no run row; then a tester can hold only one test run per path or journey at a time and must delete it to start again |
| A32 | The launch mode of a preview. **Answered by the owner on 2026-11-13: yes.** "I agree that Preview should run in normal course mode and store scores and results. However, all Preview data must remain completely isolated from official learner data, reports, analytics, progress, rewards and completion records." | A Preview and a test run launch in Normal mode, so every package, KIVO 2.0 included, records a score and a completion on the preview row, which is what Preview Reports and a test run need; the row is kept unofficial by its context, which the database enforces. The condition, complete isolation, is step 80's table, the database's CHECK and trigger, and spec 75. This changes ADR 0006 step 17 for previews and closes that record's question O-1 with no hold. The Browse alternative is not built (step 78) |
| A33 | Rows flagged as previews by role before this record. **Answered by the owner on 2026-11-13, and closed:** "Please preserve the old staff records that have been classified as Preview based on the user's role." The second sentence of that answer, on automatic deletion, fell with the owner's third message of the same day | The rows are kept, labelled `legacy_role`, counted nowhere official and never deleted, because some are real results or real work in progress (an analyst's official attempt, work done before a promotion). How that is applied is reading R26, and its one open question is row L2. Counted before the paste (Q10); expected 0 on production; on staging the owner may reset them as test data. Two effects on the people who own such rows are stated (step 82; Q10 counts both): a person whose real earlier work was flagged holds no official result on that course after 0107, so their next Start is a first official attempt beside a result that is hidden; and an open by-role row is not resumed by Start and is closed by that person's next Preview. A day credit or points such a row earned stay with the person, so the day is not asked for again and the course pays no second time |
| A34 | Preview data against backups and other copies. **Closed for Preview data by the owner's third message of 2026-11-13:** "Preview data should not be automatically deleted after 30, 60 or 90 days." The earlier answer of that day, on a retention limit for live rows and backups, is in the Context | No retention figure, no lifecycle rule and no gate are asked. The owner confirmed that the bucket read was the database backup bucket: it keeps every version of the nightly encrypted dumps. A deleted Preview row therefore stays in older dumps and a restore brings it back (steps 83, 97; reading R27) |
| A35 | Previews are never forwarded to a tenant LRS, and never reach the CRM | The LMS cannot delete from a tenant's system, so a manual delete could not reach a forwarded preview, and the owner's "completely separate" and "CRM/Yoddha updates" require that none is sent. The forwarder, every sweeper source, "Resend all history" and the abandon path (`lib/lrs/abandoned.ts`, which every restart, close and language switch calls) exclude the preview context; the CRM feeds and the webhook already do through the official rule. The source scan asserts it for every reader under `lib/lrs/` (steps 62, 80). For the tenant LRS this is the architect's decision, not the owner's words. Alternative: forward with a preview marker and record the tenant LRS as an exception |
| A36 | Deleting preview data: the mechanism, and the departure from principle 3. **Answered by the owner on 2026-11-13: allowed.** "I agree to allow deletion of Preview data, with a dry run before deletion and an audit record for every deletion. This permission must apply only to Preview/test data. Official learner records and completion history must remain protected." | One SQL function, `purge_preview_data`, is the only code that deletes an attempt row directly, and only a person starts it: from the manual delete route or a product-delete route (steps 81, 85, 99). Every real call must carry the hash of a dry run and writes an audit row with that person's id, ids and counts only; that the person saw that dry run's summary is the work of the routes that call the function, and of their tests (step 86). It can select only preview rows of the three new kinds, never a by-role row; it never deletes an attempt that an official side effect points at, but sets it aside, reports it and deletes the rest; a rehearsal proves no other row is touched. Four cascades delete preview rows outside it and leave no audit row; whether the owner's two conditions are met with them is reading R22. This departs from `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3, principle 3, for preview data only |
| A37 | A record of a language switch, and a switch inside a path or journey | An audit row per switch naming the attempts it closed; the redirect and a "Change language" entry that keep the product context; honest confirm copy (step 27). Alternative: a `closed_reason` column on the attempt, a schema change for the same information |
| A38 | A completed journey on the learner dashboard | A "Journey completed" card for the latest completed enrolment, so a completion, an admin's publish included, is "reflected correctly in the learner dashboard" (the owner, A20). Today the dashboard only stops showing the journey. Alternative: record that the dashboard shows nothing and the journey page and certificate carry the completed state |
| A39 | The journey editor's operations and two new database functions | Move, insert and remove are operations on days with ids; the curriculum save goes through `journey_save_days` and the publish to all through `journey_repin_enrollments`, each one transaction (steps 92, 94). Both change how an existing route writes. A removed day is kept without a number, so nothing that names it loses its parent |
| A40 | Corrections from the schedule read | Where the timed refresh runs, and its price (step 71); the staging paste windows (step 73); one more precondition for 0108, every open branch up to date (step 20); the nightly's timeout before new specs join (step 73); the attribution of "never while another harness runs". The 0108 paste stays in phase 2's gate as agreed; moving it is proposal P6 |
| A41 | An activity day inside a journey test run (phase G) | D13's activity days are credited with no attempt, so a test run built on preview attempts alone could not pass one. From phase G the tester marks such a day done inside the run; the mark is a day id in `preview_runs.done_activity_day_ids`, a column 0107 creates empty, purged with the run, written to no official table (step 89). It is an exception to "a test run's progress is derived from preview attempts only", limited to activity days. Half a day in phase G. Alternative: a test run skips activity days and says so, and they are tested on staging with a learner account; the column is then dropped from 0107, so an objection must come before phase 1 |
| A28 | New CRM keys are version 2 | In 0107's run-once block the column is added with default 1, labelling existing keys, and the default is then set to 2; the key route is unchanged and writes with the user's session, which cannot name the column (steps 8, 53, 74a). Chosen over creating keys with the service client: no route change, no new service-role write, and the label cannot be chosen by the caller. Consequence: every key created after the paste is version 2, on staging from the phase 1 paste, where it takes effect when 9a deploys; a consumer that must stay on version 1 keeps its existing key |
| A29 | Who the CRM feeds list, and unreleased path steps | Both versions keep today's contract (ADR 0020 decision 2, `docs/CRM_INTEGRATION.md`): the feeds expand the three enrolment tables themselves and list what is assigned, released or not; `steps[]` lists every step; each step, and each course row in version 2, gains `released` and `release_at` (step 55). Alternative: version 2 lists only what the learner can open, as the reports count; it would make version 2 drop rows version 1 returns and needs its own paragraph in the notice |
| A30 | No start-date floor or calendar ceiling when a journey attempt is credited | The tagged branch has neither today. The day is checked when the attempt is launched, and the attempt's context cannot change, so the row itself is the proof; a second check at completion would refuse an earned credit after a late save, a late `passed` or a sweep (step 13). The day's identity, the active enrolment, the owner and the pass rule are checked on every call, the sweep's included; the day's course is not checked at credit time (step 13). Alternative: re-check the floor at completion and leave refused rows for an admin |

Whether PostgREST resolves the two `consume_grant` forms as it does the two `record_revision_visit` forms is a case of spec 68 on staging. Nothing was committed by the architect.

## Review

The independent review of 2026-11-12 read this record, the filed ADR 0023 draft and the dated notes against the owner's answers, the plan, the technical detail and `main` 1aeb339. It made 50 findings in three passes: fidelity to the owner's answers (10), accuracy against the code and migrations (17), and whether an executor can build from the text (23). Nine matters were found more than once (eight by two findings, one by three); each is folded once and every id is given, so the table has 40 rows for 50 ids. The fold was made on 2026-11-13.

How the evidence was checked. The architect's session has no shell, so nothing was run: no `git` command, no test, no rehearsal, no database. Each finding's evidence was re-read in the checkout at `main` 1aeb339 or in the named source. Re-read and found as described: the owner's answers on D7, D12, D14, D15, D16 and the CRM ID requirement; the plan's D3, D7 and D11 rows, its timing paragraph and its production order; `0071:15-36`; the API keys route's insert and update; `0103:424-610`; `0105:688-697`; the partial unique indexes in 0005, 0008 and 0058; `journey_auto_enroll` in 0058, 0063 and 0096 and the template check in 0014 to 0065; `0053:227`; the publish route's loop; the profile page's badge line; the launch page's continue link and its `evaluate` rerun; the continue page's `isStaff` branch; every `course_attempts` insert under `tests/`; the nightly's default list; the e2e job's target; the language-preference route's body and its two callers; the reminders cron's header; ADR 0004 step 9; ADR 0014 step 18; ADR 0017's two sentences; ADR 0008 step 4; ADR 0019 decision 3; ADR 0020 step 9; ADR 0006 note (r); `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3 and section 7; the rehearsal runner and its folder; the readers of `mv_course_performance`; the cited lines of `continue.test.ts`, `version-restart.test.ts`, `webhook-hold.test.ts`, `enrolment-definition.test.ts` and spec 34. Not re-read, and taken from the review as stated: the lines of `alds-launch.test.ts`, `feedback-summary.test.ts`, `crm-guide-texts.test.ts` and `launch-bar-copy.test.ts`; the per-email subrequest lines of `lib/notifications/send.ts`; `lib/integrations/auth.ts`; the branch-by-branch read count of the launch page (accuracy-5, taken in part for that reason).

Outcome: 47 accepted, 3 accepted in part, none rejected. Where the fold differs from the proposed fix, the difference is stated.

### Final review, 2026-11-13

A second, independent review checked the late additions of the first fold against `main` 1aeb339. It made 11 findings (4 major, 7 minor) and listed 7 findings of the verification reads that had no home in this record (U1 to U7). The fold was made the same day.

How the evidence was checked. As before, the architect's session has no shell: no `git` command (so the list of files changed on this branch was taken from the notes this record names in step 77, not from a diff), no test, no rehearsal, no database. Re-read in the checkout and found as described: `computeJourneyState` (`lib/journey/journey.ts:212-283`); the whole of 0089; the publish route's loop and its comment (`app/api/journey/program/route.ts:127-172`); the unique key of `journey_day_progress` (`0058:173`); the key route's insert and update (`app/api/org/api-keys/route.ts:68-104`); `0008:23` (the review said `:22`) and `0034:34`; the rehearsal shim's header and `asrole-grants.sql`; `scripts/check-migration-grants.mjs` (its grant pattern, which checks relations and not functions); `webhook-hold.ts:159-314`; `0103:309-339`; the readers of `revision_visits` and of `journey_day_progress` under `app/` and `lib/` (by search); the nudges cron's call (`journey-nudges/route.ts:178-203`); `lib/integrations/progress.ts:415-484`; `docs/CRM_INTEGRATION.md:525-540`; the two verification reads cited for U1, U3 and U7; the plan's two sentences on re-pinning; the dated notes this branch wrote on ADR 0004, 0005, 0006, 0008, 0009, 0014, 0015, 0017, 0019, 0020 and 0021 and the note at the head of the filed ADR 0023. Of these only the ADR 0015 note changed (one sentence: the sweep's date floor stays); none of the others names a phase, an effort figure, the number of items for the owner, the journey completion rule or a key's version, and none states R6's reading of `is_preview` as a fact of 0107. Taken from the review as stated: the `learner-summary` expansion lines of U6.

Outcome: 16 accepted, 2 accepted in part (7 and 10), none rejected.

### Round 7, 2026-11-13 (consolidation)

Rounds 4 to 6 left overlay notes in the body ("round 6: ...", "the text that follows is round 5's") above the text they superseded. Round 7 rewrote the body, from the Decision to the References, so that it states only the current design. It deleted and folded; it added no requirement.
