No step, section letter, decision id, check, spec or rehearsal case was renumbered; a withdrawn item keeps a one-line stub with its number. The status stays Proposed. The work was done in three passes (sections A to H; I to Q; S to the References), each by the architect, each reading its sections in full.

**The lead's four decisions of this round.**
- L1, scope trim of the delete. The owner asked for a dry-run summary, a confirmation and an audit record. Removed from the design: the scope that takes a list of ids (with its cap of 5,000 ids and the repeat in parts), "Delete these again", "Upload ids", the typed total as the confirmation of a wide delete, and the answer field `not_found`. Kept: the audit row with who, when, the counts and the deleted ids, and a read-only "Deletion history" with "Download ids". Re-applying a logged deletion automatically is a possible later feature, not planned (steps 79, 81, 97, 99; row L1).
- L2, by-role rows are never deletable. Removed: the scope `{"legacy_role": true}`, the kept test with its six parts, `legacy_deleted` and `legacy_kept` in the answer and as a third list of the `selection_hash`, the list "Earlier staff attempts that can be deleted", the second language-delete message for deletable by-role rows, the course delete's purge of unprotected by-role rows and its second reading of the real call. The kept test is dropped entirely, not kept for product deletes: a product delete refuses on any by-role row, which a plain count decides, so no test of what protects a row is needed. What holds: no scope or reason selects a `legacy_role` row; Preview Reports list them read only under "Kept earlier staff attempts"; the course, path, program and language deletes answer 409 while one exists (steps 81, 82, 85, 99; reading R26). The owner is asked one yes or no (row L2), default no; on a yes the scope and the test return as a separate step of about three quarters of a day.
- L3, stated plainly: Preview data stays on production with no time limit until an admin deletes it, a wider exception to `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` principle 5 than R21's "deleted within the 90-day maximum retention period", which the owner's third message superseded. It is in step 76, the Consequences, Conflicts row 14 and row L3, as a confirmation asked at acceptance.
- L4, code moved since 1aeb339. `main` now holds pull request 245 (the language delete on the caller's session) and 246 (the nightly's build retry). The language delete route was opened at the branch's current `main` and step 85 and "Verification" cite it there (`:292-312`, `:337-341`, `:343-351`); the earlier text, which said pull request 245 could not be opened, is removed. Other citations stay at 1aeb339.

### Round 8, 2026-11-13

A check of decision L2 found that "by-role rows are never deleted" was not enforced in the database, and that the record did not tell the owner what it means for deleting content. Both are fixed. The status stays Proposed. No step, section letter, decision id, check, spec or rehearsal case was renumbered; no figure changed; Conflicts gains row 15.

- The facts, read in the migrations and the code. The two delete guards (`0100:511-576`; triggers `0098:249-257`) count only attempts that are not previews and return at once for the service role. A by-role row has been an `is_preview` row since 0100 (`0100:456-467`); 0107 only labels it `legacy_role`. So a delete of a course, a version or a language on an admin's session would have gone on cascading by-role rows away, as ADR 0003 decision 3 intended and against this record's never-delete rule; the routes' counts were the only protection, with a window, and a delete through the Data API had none. Round 7 had written "That guard is not changed, so these refusals are the routes'".
- The fix. 0107 recreates both guards from their 0100 bodies with one clause changed, so that they count every attempt that is not a preview of a new kind (step 7). Placed in the creation order (item 6), the never-paste list (0098 and 0100 added), after-check A23 (no new check number), the reverse script (part (2)) and the rehearsal's guard cases (step 63). The service-role return is kept, so the e2e purge of `qa-*` organisations and the lifecycle 48-hour cleanup are unchanged. Priced inside phase 1's days, as an estimate.
- The consequence, now stated. A course, version or language that holds only by-role rows could be deleted (ADR 0003 decision 3) and no longer can. Row L2 is rewritten to say so, with where it is felt and what "yes" would do to the guards. Conflicts row 15, a Risks row, the Consequences, steps 60, 82 and 85, row R26 and the Verification follow. Spec 50's case "staff-only attempts do not block" is listed as changed, in phase 1 and again in phase 2 (steps 62, 64). A dated note is written on ADR 0003, inline under its decision 3.
- Two points where this round differs from the task as first stated. (1) By-role rows are not only rows "made before 0107": on staging the old launch code keeps writing them until phase 2 serves (steps 7, 60), so the body says "until the Start and Preview buttons serve". (2) A staff launch after that is not always a preview: a Start is a real attempt and blocks a delete like any learner's (R6); only a Preview or a test run does not block.
- The recommendation on L2 stays "no". The facts do not make it unsound: production is expected to hold no by-role row, staging holds test courses, and the test purges are not affected.
- Removed with the fix: the argument in step 85 that no by-role row can appear after a route's count once 0108 is pasted. The database now refuses such a delete, so the argument is not needed.
- Left for a later pass, outside this round's files: the dated note on ADR 0009 says attempt rows leave by cascade with a course or a language "whose only attempts are previews"; under step 7 that holds for previews of the new kinds only. The dated note on ADR 0005 does not cover its step 10, which wrote the guards' `not a.is_preview` clause; Conflicts row 15 names it. Step 77 lists both.
- Not run: anything. The claim that spec 50's case fails on a database with 0107 is reasoned from the route and the guard.

An independent check of this round's edit raised six minor findings. One line each:

- R8-1, spec 50's phase 2 form lacked the seed change: fixed. Step 62's row says the seed assigns course C to the admin, because phase 2 refuses an unassigned launch on an "Assigned only" course and self-enrols on an open one; the Start then blocks and the delete answers 409.
- R8-2, row L2 gave Q10's count as read: fixed. It says "is expected to read 0; no database was read".
- R8-3, `assert-0100.sql` after 0107: fixed. Step 63 says it is not run after 0107 in the four new scenarios, and why (its body check (0) and its case (8) describe the 0100 guards). The other earlier assert files were not read; the executor checks each against what 0107 recreates before running it again.
- R8-4, "after 0107 they are previews": fixed in step 7, Conflicts row 15 and the first bullet of this round. By-role rows have been `is_preview` rows since 0100; 0107 only labels them; the guards change because of the never-delete rule.
- R8-5, "never blocks" too wide in row L2: fixed. It names the guard, and the two route refusals of step 85 (a preview set aside for review; any preview on a language). The note on ADR 0003 is left as it is, because it speaks of the guard.
- R8-6, the dated notes on ADR 0009 and ADR 0005: made by the lead after this pass, in phase 0 (2026-11-13). The ADR 0009 note now says a course or language leaves by cascade only when its previews are of the three new kinds, and that a by-role row blocks the delete (step 7, L2); the ADR 0005 note now says step 10's guards are recreated to count by-role rows and that its preview-only delete case holds for the 0100 scenario only.

## Notes

- **Note 2026-11-13 (phase 1 build notes: corrections and gap-fills found before 0107 was written).** This is the start of the phase 1 deviations list; later corrections are appended below it with their own date. The body of the record above is not edited: where an item corrects a sentence, this note is the current text. Sources, all read against `main` 769e778 and none of them in this repository: the twins contract (the answer shapes both sides of each SQL and TypeScript pair must fill; its items are cited as "contract B1" and so on), eight PGlite spikes ("spike 1" to "spike 8"; `@electric-sql/pglite` 0.5.10, which is Postgres 18.6, synthetic rows only), the paste-window read of today's code on the 0107 schema ("window H1" and so on), the step 4 before-checks read on staging (read-only, ids and counts), and the report of the fixtures pull request. Class **(a)** = a mechanical correction or a gap-fill consistent with the record, decided here by the architect. Class **(b)** = it touches an owner answer; it is not decided here and is listed under "To the owner". No item of class (a) changes an owner answer. **Revised on 2026-11-13, after an independent review and before 0107 was written** (no 0107 migration and no parity fixture file on this branch): N1, N9 to N13, N15, N19, N22 and O1 are corrected in place, so that the executor reads one text, and O2 is folded into N22. What changed: O1 quotes the owner's words in full and no longer recommends the re-lock; N11's `status` order puts a live granted retake first; N9 and N10 define the live-retake test on the retake row's own context; the rest are citations, plumbing and wording. **Revised again on 2026-11-13, after the owner's answer to O1 and the owner's two staging reads** (by now `supabase/migrations/0107_learning_contexts.sql` is begun on this branch; it holds none of the path views, the completion tables or the writer yet, so nothing built to option (i) has to be amended). What changed: O1 is answered, option (ii), and is designed and priced under "To the owner"; N10 and N12 gain the stored half of `passed_ever`; N3 and N24 record staging's `server_version`, 17.6, and the Q5 reading; N20 records that the fixtures pull request, 248, is merged. **Revised a third time on 2026-11-13, after an independent check of the O1 design** (0107 still holds none of the path objects). What changed, all of it under "To the owner": two questions the design raises are now put to the owner as items of their own, **O3** (a second writer of the stored fact, at rule changes; until it is answered 0107 falls short of the owner's O1 sentence in the three cases of O1-h) and **O4** (the words shown for a step that is done without a pass); O1-k is new and names where every reader takes a path step's done, with step 27's Continue among them; O1-a names every place the index count is given; O1-c and O1-e put the two new columns into the writer's answer, the CRM `steps[]` and the webhook; O1-g gains three rehearsal cases; O1-h is corrected on what O3 closes; O1-i names the loader of the fact rows and its cost; O1-j is priced again, 2.25 days in place of 1.75, a quarter of it in phase 2; the label on the O1 question says how its text differs from the text the owner read. Nothing else is changed.

  **Schema (0107)**

  - **N1. Step 91, `journey_day_progress.day_id`: the foreign key is deferred. (a)** The record says `references journey_days(id) on delete no action`. It becomes `on delete no action deferrable initially deferred`. This is the foreign key, not the unique key on `journey_days (program_id, day_number)`, which stays non-deferrable as "Alternatives considered" says (spike 5 confirmed that choice: a deferrable unique key breaks the old editor's upsert with 55000). Why: a program delete reaches a credit in two hops (program, enrolment, credit) and a day in one, so when the days' cascade fires first the `no action` check runs before the credit is gone and raises 23503. Spike 3 showed it on a program with one credit row, no learner attempt and no test run; the same delete passed when the cascade order was the other way round. The order follows the internal trigger names, which are made from object ids. On staging and production the failing order is expected from the creation order in 0058; that is an inference, not a reading (N24). Such programs exist: `legacy_cross_context` credits (step 8) and, from phase G, `self` and `admin` credits. Step 40 means a program without learner attempts to be deletable. With the deferred key the program delete passes, and a lone delete of a day that holds a credit is still refused, with 23503, at commit. So a program delete removes the credit rows no attempt backs, with their enrolments, as it does today (LIVE: credits cascade from the enrolment, `0058:164-166`). That is said plainly because they are learner credit rows (principle 5), and because under the record's literal key the expected firing order would have refused that delete. It stays class (a): it keeps the LIVE behaviour and step 40's intent, and the owner's A36 sentence on protected records was given for the deletion of Preview data. A credit backed by a learner's journey attempt still blocks the delete, through the attempt's own key (step 7). Whether phase G's `self` and `admin` credits must block a program delete is decided in phase G, by step 40's count, not by this key. `on delete cascade` was tested and rejected: a lone day delete then silently deletes the credit (principle 5). Step 40's route maps 23503 by SQLSTATE and needs no change. The `no action` keys of `attempt_requests` on the journey columns stay as written. Deferring them would change nothing: no part of a program delete removes a request (its cascades come from organizations, courses and users, `0083:20-22`), so a journey request refuses the delete in any firing order, as an attempt does. Rehearsal case added to step 63: "a program with a credit row and no learner attempt can be deleted", with the control "a lone delete of a credited day fails at commit".
  - **N2. Steps 5 and 79, creation order item 2: the rule tying `preview_kind` to `context_kind`. (a)** The rule `(preview_kind is not null) = (context_kind = 'preview')` is never written inline on the `preview_kind` column. It is part of the shape CHECK (or a constraint of its own) and is added after the `preview_kind` backfill. Why: added with the column, it fails at once with 23514 on any database that holds an `is_preview` row (spike 2; staging holds 51). The arms keep the record's null-safe form, where every term is `is [not] null` or a non-null boolean: a form such as `context_kind <> 'preview' or preview_kind in (...)` admits a preview with a null `preview_kind`. Spike 2 also showed that the record's backfill order followed by the CHECK refuses exactly the row classes Q2 and Q8 refuse and no other, so no paste reaches the CHECK and fails on a row the before-checks did not name.
  - **N3. Steps 5, 7 and 8: generated columns are written `stored`, with explicit `::text` casts. (a)** The record already writes both; this makes them a rule of the file, with a static check in the build that every `generated always as` of 0107 ends in `stored`. Why: the rehearsal engine is Postgres 18, production is 17.x (`.github/workflows/backup.yml:90`), and staging reads 17.6 (the owner's read of 2026-11-13, N24). On 18 a generated column without `stored` is virtual and passes unless it is indexed; on 15 to 17 it is a syntax error. On 18.6 `'path:' || uuid_col` without `::text` was accepted; an older version may refuse it as not immutable (not verified on 15 or 17). So a pass on PGlite proves neither. The record's SQL uses nothing newer than Postgres 15; `security_invoker` already sets that floor. Two more cautions of the same kind, for the after-checks and the privilege matrix of step 19: read not-null from `pg_attribute.attnotnull` and privileges through `has_*_privilege(...)` per privilege, never from `pg_constraint` or raw ACL text, and never pass `'MAINTAIN'` (it raises on 15 and 16). `selection_hash` (step 81) uses core `sha256(convert_to(..., 'UTF8'))` or `md5()`, never pgcrypto's `digest()`, which does not resolve under the pinned `search_path`. Permitted, not required: `context_key` and `settle_sig` may be added in one `alter table` (one table rewrite in place of two; spike 7 measured 20.1 s against 36.5 s on 100,000 synthetic rows). The shape CHECK does not read `settle_sig`, so moving it ahead of the CHECK changes nothing else in the creation order. `context_key` is not declared `not null`: the shape CHECK already keeps it non-null.
  - **N4. Step 78: a launch closes an open `legacy_role` row before it inserts a course preview. (a)** Spike 4 shows this order is required, so it is a rule of the launch and not a preference; where step 78's wording leaves the order open, this note fixes it. An open `legacy_role` row and a new open `course` preview of the same version share the key `preview`, so the insert fails with 23505 under the open-row key. The close and the insert are in one transaction, or the launch retries once on 23505.
  - **N5. Steps 7 and 13: replaced functions. (a)** As the record says, and now proven (spikes 4 and 6): the new `journey_record_completion` is in the same paste as the drop of `unique (enrollment_id, day_number)` and no statement between the two calls the old body, whose conflict target raises 42P10 once the key is gone; every `create or replace` restates `security definer` and `set search_path`, because leaving them out resets both while the trigger stays bound. Added: the privilege matrix asserts `prosecdef` as well as `proconfig` for the two delete guards.
  - **N6. Step 91, the paste-window trigger on `journey_versions`. (a)** Two rules the record leaves open (contract B8; window note 3). (i) It fills `day_ids` only when the insert gives none; a given map is left as it is, so the phase 2 publish and editor-sourced fixtures are not overwritten. (ii) When an entry of the new row's `days` has no `journey_days` row for (program, day number), it raises with SQLSTATE P0001 and writes nothing. Why raise: it is Q8's rule at run time (a day identity is never invented), and leaving the day out would move the failure to the learner's launch, where the derive trigger of step 7 raises and the page shows "Failed to create attempt." The old publish never reaches the raise, because every day it freezes comes from a `journey_days` row. A version with no days gets an empty map.
  - **N7. Steps 7 and 91: errors of the new triggers. (a)** The derive trigger, the immutability trigger and the `day_ids` trigger raise with SQLSTATE P0001 and a fixed short message. The message never contains "does not exist", "schema cache" or "could not find the", and the code is never 42703, 42P01, 42883 or a PGRST20x code. Why: the launch's schema probes, `isAldsSchemaMissing` (`lib/alds/flag.ts:43-50`) and `isSchemaMissing` (`lib/scoring/attempt-kind.ts:52-66`), match those codes and phrases. A match would make the old launch retry its insert without the ALDS_P4 columns, or log a skip as schema missing, and hide the refusal (window note 4). The delete guards keep `learner_history_exists` (step 7). Proof: the rehearsal asserts the SQLSTATE of each refusal, and a unit test feeds each message to both probes and expects false.

  **The official rule and the path views (0107)**

  - **N8. Step 9 (b): the abandoned filter is in the scoring view's `completed` CTE. (a)** No change to the record; the first parity case pins it. An abandoned row that is completed is official in the 0103 view and not in `computeScoring` today; under 0107 it is official on neither side (contract 1.2). The preview term of the rule is implied by key equality for every key that is not a preview key. For a preview key, which only Preview Reports ask for, the rule is "not abandoned and not a revision row", in TypeScript only (contract A1).
  - **N9. Steps 9, 10 and 41: a granted retake counts by `used_attempt_id`, whatever the grant's own context; the live-retake test is on the retake row's context. (a)** A retake belongs to the scored window when an approved `attempt_requests` row names it in `used_attempt_id` (contract B6, A2). That is the view's rule today (`0103:439-442`), and TypeScript matches it. The grant's `context_key` filters only the unused grant a context may consume. The live-retake test (in `consume_grant`, step 10; in `contextResult`; and in `v_path_step_status.retake_live`, N10) is on the retake row's own context: a row of (course, learner, `context_key`) that is in progress, neither completed nor passed, not a revision row, and named in `used_attempt_id` by an approved grant of any context (in `consume_grant`, a grant other than the one being consumed). That is today's test (`0103:780-797`) with the row's `context_key` added. It also settles whose key step 10 means: `retake_live`, the chain, `stands` and the abandon all read attempt rows whose `context_key` is the launch's context, and the grant's own `context_key` is matched only at the lock, against the context computed from the three tags. Where the twins contract's section 1.3 puts the live-retake test under the grant's context, this note corrects it by the contract's own rules 2.3.4 and 2.3.11. Why: step 8 labels every existing grant `individual`, but today's `consume_grant` writes the path or journey tags on the retake it inserts (`0103:889-894`). Filtered by the grant's context, those retakes would leave the window and become practice rows, which would rewrite official history (principle 5). The same holds for the live test: a legacy retake still open at the paste has a grant that reads `individual` and a row tagged with the path, so a test on the grant's context would call it live in the Individual context and not in `path:P`, and a new path grant would abandon it instead of answering `retake_live`. Only retakes open at the paste are affected, so staging only, but the rule is fixed in 0107. Parity case added (official and path pairs): an open legacy path retake whose grant reads `individual` is live in `path:P` and not in the Individual context, and the step reads `retake_live` true and `failed` false. Rehearsal case added to step 63 on the same rows: `consume_grant` for a second path grant answers `retake_live` with nothing abandoned. It is a grading rule; it keeps today's behaviour and changes no owner answer.
  - **N10. Step 9, `v_path_step_status`: added columns. (a)** `step_number int` and `release_at timestamptz` (contract B1: step 18 reads them "from `v_path_step_status`"). `official_attempt_id uuid` (the attempt behind `latest_result`), `retake_live boolean` = some row of (course, learner, `path:P`) is in progress, neither completed nor passed, not a revision row, and named in `used_attempt_id` by an approved grant of any context (N9's test, in the same words; the grant's own context is not read), `failed boolean` = `not passed_ever and latest_result = 'failed' and not retake_live` (step 44's sentence, as a column), and `open_attempt boolean` (an official row of the context is in progress) (contract B2). By the owner's O1 answer, two more: `passed_by_rule boolean` (N12's computed expression, alone) and `done_recorded_at timestamptz` (the `done_at` of the step's stored fact, null when there is none); `passed_ever` is true when either holds (O1 below). `failed` keeps its formula and reads that `passed_ever`. Why now: 0109 does not recreate the path views (step 21) and 0107 is not pasted again after 0109, so a column missing here would give step 44's failed rule a second home.
  - **N11. Step 9, `v_path_progress`: types, values and one added column. (a)** `next_step int` = the lowest `step_number` not done, null when every step is done; it ignores release dates and `sequence_mode`, which the launch and the page apply. Added: `next_course_id uuid`, the course of `next_step`, because Continue and the dashboard tile need the course (contract B3). `status` is one of four values, tested in this order: `in_progress` when some current step has `retake_live` (N10), whether or not a completion row exists (acceptance criterion 8, "the Overview shows P in progress while the retake is live"; step 52, "A live granted retake counts as in progress"; the Individual rule does the same); then `completed` (a completion row exists); then `failed` (some step is `failed`, N10); then `in_progress` (some current step has a live row in the path's context, `live_attempt_count > 0`, the 0099 meaning, so a reset-only learner reads not started); then `not_started`. That is step 52's "Path: a step is failed (step 44) and the path is not completed", and it agrees with reading R1: in the D14 case the step is `passed_ever`, so it is not `failed`, and once the retake ends, passed or failed, a completed path reads `completed` again. `completed`, `completed_at` and `overdue` do not follow `status`: `completed` stays true throughout a live retake, and `overdue` reads `completed`. So the two can differ while a retake is live, and each reader takes one of them: a reader that splits every enrolment once (the Overview's states of step 52, built in 0109) reads `status`, so while the retake is live P counts under In progress and not under Completed. This is a reading of step 52's Completed row ("Path: the `learning_path_completions` row exists") by criterion 8, made so that the split never counts P twice: in the split, Completed is `status = 'completed'`, and step 52's "the row exists" is the `completed` column. Criterion 6's 3 completions are unaffected, because no retake is live there. A reader that asks "has this learner completed the path" (the path page, the certificate, the email latch, the CRM completion) reads `completed`. No reader adds the two. Parity cases added: a completed path with a live granted retake reads `status` `in_progress` with `completed` true and `overdue` false, and reads `completed` again after the retake fails; a path with one failed step and a live retake on another step reads `in_progress`. `started_at` = the earliest `started_at` of the path context's rows that are not revision rows and not abandoned, on courses that are current steps. `path_score` is unrounded in SQL and compared at 4 decimals (contract B4). A completed path with a step added later reads `completed` with `steps_done < steps_total`: the record already says so (section L; contract B10) and a parity case pins it.
  - **N12. Steps 9 and 44, `passed_ever`: read over the scored window. (a)** `passed_ever` is true when some row of the path context's scored window (the base window plus completed granted retakes) satisfies the step's resolved rule: under `pass_required`, a window row with `success_status = 'passed'`; without it, the window is not empty. Practice rows, abandoned rows, revision rows and previews never count. `v_context_attempt_scoring` appends `passed_in_window boolean` (`bool_or` of in the window and passed), and the path view reads `case when pass_required then coalesce(passed_in_window, false) else coalesce(scored_attempts, 0) > 0 end`, so the window logic exists once (contract B5, A5, A6). `v_context_attempt_summary` carries `passed_in_window` through, beside `scored_attempts`, and the path view reads both from the summary it joins (step 9); it does not join the scoring view a second time. Step 9 (d)'s list of appended columns gains `passed_in_window`. The column is added to the `v_context_*` pair only: `v_course_attempt_scoring` and `v_course_attempt_summary` keep their columns and column order (step 9). It is not the summary's `ever_passed` (`0103:559`), which also counts practice and abandoned rows; a parity case separates the two. By the owner's O1 answer of 2026-11-13 the expression of this item is the computed half and the view names it `passed_by_rule`; `passed_ever` is `passed_by_rule or` a stored fact exists for the step (O1 below). The window logic is unchanged and still exists once.
  - **N13. Step 12.4, `attempt_number`. (a)** `attempt_number` is the context's completion order under the official rule: the scoring view's `attempt_no` (`0103:443-446`, partitioned by `context_key` in 0107), practice rows included. It is not a count of the rows that were official. That equals `computeScoring`'s `attemptNumber` (`lib/scoring/policy.ts:336-337`) on every row, a granted retake completed after a pre-0103 practice row included (it is 3 on both sides); the writer's comment says so; no fixture field (contract A15). Step 12.4's "counts official attempts within the context" is read this way.
  - **N14. Step 13, `journey_credit_state`: order and edges. (a)** `credited` ascending; `credited_day_ids` parallel to it; `resumable_day_ids` in ascending uuid order (a removed day has no number). A required day is an entry of the pinned version's `days` with a course and `day <= days_total`, whose id is its `day_ids` entry. A credit counts only when its `day_id` is the id of a required day; every credit kind counts. Zero required days: `required_days = 0`, `next_day` null, never complete. Resumable: a journey row of the enrolment with `status = 'in_progress'` and `is_revision = false`, whatever its day's place in the pinned version now (contract 4.1, B8).
  - **N15. Step 18, `launch_context`: the key tree and the added facts. (a)** The record lists the facts and not their keys. The tree is frozen as: `v` (1); `member.active`; `course` with `found`, `is_active`, `visibility` and `individual` (`enrolled`, `upcoming_at`); `path` (null without `p_path`) with `found`, `is_active`, `visibility`, `sequence_mode`, `enrolled`, `self_row`, `course_step` and `steps[]` (`course_id`, `step_number`, `release_at`, `done`; by `step_number`); `journey` (null without `p_enrollment`) with `found`, `status`, `program_id`, `program_is_active`, `start_date`, `version_id`, `unlock_mode`, `count_sundays`, `days_total`, `timezone`, `day` (`id`, `number`, `in_version`, `removed`, `course_id`), `day_credit`, `day_open_attempt`, `required_days`, `credited_days`, `next_day`, `next_day_id` and `resumable_day_ids`; `holders` with `paths[]` (`path_id`, `step_number`; active paths the learner is enrolled on that hold the course) and `journey_days[]` (`enrollment_id`, `day_id`, `day_number`, `credited`; required days of the learner's active enrolments that hold the course); and `run`, null, reserved for phase P. Facts step 18 does not list, each needed by a decision step 22 makes: `course.found` and `course.is_active` (the launch refuses an inactive course today); `individual.upcoming_at` ("not yet released" told from "not assigned"); `journey.program_is_active` (step 22 checks it); `day.removed`; `day_open_attempt` (resume needs an open official attempt of this course, and `resumable_day_ids` does not name the course). The inner keys and the array orders are part of the frozen tree, because the rehearsal compares the answer as jsonb, which ignores key order and not array order: `day_credit` (`course_id`, `attempt_id`, `credit_kind`, `day_number`; null when none); `day_open_attempt` (`attempt_id`, `course_id`; null when none); `path.steps` ordered by `step_number`; `holders.paths` ordered by `path_id`; `holders.journey_days` ordered by `enrollment_id`, then `day_number`; `resumable_day_ids` in ascending uuid order (N14). `found: false` carries no other key. Arrays are always present. The function still decides nothing (contract B7, A10).

Why there: the rehearsal workflow runs only when `supabase/migrations/**`, `scripts/migration-rehearsal/**` or the release SQL changes, so a fixture under `tests/unit/` could change without the SQL side running again; the folder exists already. Rows use database column names; ids are symbols resolved to lower-case uuids in a range of their own; every timestamp compared with `now()` is null, at or before 2001-01-01 or at or after 2999-01-01; `expect` is complete for its pair, so nothing passes by omission. `context-parity.test.ts` runs the official and path pairs; `launch-context-facts.test.ts` runs the launch pair as the record names it: SQL asserts `launch_context(given) = expect.facts`, TypeScript asserts the resolver fed `expect.facts` gives `expect.decision`.   **The paste window (step 60)**

  - **N21. Step 60, correction: a refused resume re-tag is not repaired by the next journey launch. (a)** The record says "the next launch from the journey page opens a journey attempt and repairs it". Under the old launch code that is wrong: once the untagged row completes, the old rule sees an official attempt for the course (`lib/attempts/launch-kind.ts:35-37`, `:71-79`), so the next journey launch is a revision visit, and neither write route credits a revision visit (`commit:364-374`, `statements:322-335`). Corrected text: the day stays uncredited until phase 2 serves; after that the learner's launch from the journey page opens a journey attempt in the day's own context and the day can be credited. Staging learners only (window H7); staging is test data, and production gets 0107 and the new code in one sitting (step 74). Also disclosed for the same window, none of them a change: a `data_analyst`'s granted retake loses `served_language` and `launch_mode`, because the whole update is refused (the retake stays a real attempt, as step 7 says); an Individual retake no longer abandons an open path or journey row of the same course; the old publish route still completes enrolments by count beside the database's rule and inserts the lifetime badge, so a learner can hold both badges; the `enr:` period is shown raw on the profile until step 35; every insert by old code updates the one-row `context_derive_log`, which briefly serialises concurrent launch inserts.

  **Records and counts**

  - **N22. Steps 9, 14 and 58: `learning_path_completions` has no backfill. Accepted and disclosed. (a)** A staging learner who finished a path before 0107 has every step done and no completion row, so `v_path_progress` reads `completed` false, `status` `in_progress` and, past a due date, `overdue` true. (A learner counted complete by today's cross-context rule whose steps are not all done in `path:P` reads by the path rule, in progress, failed or not started; that is the record's intended change, not this gap.) Nothing repairs it: the sweep never reaches history (`side_effects_sig` is backfilled, step 7) and a revision visit never calls the writer. Size: at most 39 path enrolments, the completed count of the path matview on staging by today's cross-context rule. Accepted because staging is test data and production has no learners. A run-once backfill into the new table is outside step 58's list and is not built; it is built only if the owner asks for it, that is, only if the owner wants staging's earlier path completions to read completed. It touches no owner answer, so it is not a question to the owner and the default stands without an answer (contract B9).

Q2 refusals 0, 0, 0; preview rows carrying a tag 6; abandoned rows that are completed 0. Q3: 259 open rows, at most 1 per (learner, version).
