  **To the owner (class (b)): O1, answered by the owner on 2026-11-13; O3 and O4, open (they follow O1-k)**

  - **O1. `passed_ever` when an admin tightens a step's rule after a learner finished it.** *The question follows; it is superseded by the answer and the design below it. It is not word for word the text the owner read. Three edits were made when the answer was recorded, as the independent check of the same day found them: option (ii) and the closing paragraph were put into the past tense ("when the question was put it was not designed in detail and not priced; both are below", "The answer was wanted"); the sentence "0107 is built to the record's wording, (i), until the owner answers." was removed from the closing paragraph; and the last sentence of the closing paragraph, "It came before the path views…", was added. The two options and the recommendation are otherwise as the owner read them.* N12 judges by the step's rule as it stands today, which is the record's wording ("satisfied the step's resolved rule"). So if a learner completed a step without passing while no pass was required, and an admin later turns `pass_required` on for that step, the step reads not done and, in a strict path, later steps lock again until the learner passes on a granted retake. A stored path completion is not undone. This touches the owner's words of 2026-11-11 ("Once a learner completes the official attempt launched from the Learning Path, the next chapter should unlock permanently"), which speak of completion and, read literally, keep the step unlocked; D14, which speaks of a pass; and the owner's R13/R14 answer for journeys ("They should continue under the version/rules that applied when they started that day"), which step 13 applies so that a stricter day rule never fails earlier work. None of the three was given for a path step whose rule an admin changes afterwards, so the choice is the owner's. The two options:
    - **(i) Re-lock, as the record's wording gives it.** The step is judged by its rule as it stands today. No new schema. A learner who completed the step under the old rule sees it not done, and in a strict path sees later steps locked, until a pass on a granted retake.
    - **(ii) A step once done stays done.** A stored per-step "done" fact, written by the writer of step 12 when the step first satisfies the rule in force at that time, and read by `passed_ever` beside the computed rule (so a rule relaxed later still helps at once). New schema in 0107, with its RLS, grants, privilege matrix and reverse-script entries; when the question was put it was not designed in detail and not priced; both are below. Steps finished before 0107 have no stored fact and are judged by the rule as it stands (staging test data; production has no learners).

    Which option the owner's words point to: (ii). "Unlock permanently" read literally, the journey rule the owner gave in R13/R14 and the owner's A27 choice to protect work already done all point the same way, and no owner sentence points to (i). The architect's earlier recommendation of (i) rested on cost (nothing stored today says what the rule was) and on tightening being an admin's deliberate act; neither is a reading of the owner's words, and it is withdrawn. Recommended answer: (ii), with its price given to the owner together with this question; (i) if the owner prefers no added schema and accepts the re-lock. The answer was wanted before the staging paste, because a stored fact is an object of 0107 and 0109 does not recreate the path views (step 21). It came before the path views, the completion tables and the writer of 0107 were written.

    **The owner's answer, 2026-11-13: option (ii).** In the owner's words: "I have selected Option (ii): Once a learner completes a Learning Path step, it should remain completed even if the Admin later changes the step to "Pass Required". The learner's existing completion should be preserved, and subsequent steps should remain unlocked. The learner should not be forced to repeat an already completed step because of a later configuration change. Please proceed with this behaviour in migration 0107." Option (i) is not built. The design below is the current text for 0107; it adds to steps 7 (the index count), 9, 12, 14, 19, 27 (Continue, O1-k), 31 and 55 (the two added fields, O1-e), 43, 44, 61, 62, 63 and 70 (the loader's reads, O1-i) and to the creation order, and changes no other owner answer. It leaves two questions open, O3 and O4, put to the owner after O1-k.

    - **O1-a. The stored fact: table `learning_path_step_completions`.** One row per (path, learner, course) says "this step was done". Columns: `id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`; `organization_id uuid not null references organizations(id) on delete cascade`; `path_id uuid not null references learning_paths(id) on delete no action` (as `learning_path_completions.path_id`, step 14); `course_id uuid not null references courses(id) on delete cascade`; `user_id uuid not null references auth.users(id) on delete cascade`; `attempt_id uuid` (an id only, no foreign key, like the ids in `learning_path_completions.steps`: the step's `official_attempt_id` at the write; a `set null` key would be an update of an append-only row); `rule_pass_required boolean not null` (the step's resolved rule at the write, so the row says under which rule it was earned); `done_at timestamptz not null default now()`; `unique (path_id, user_id, course_id)`. The key is the view's grain. There is no foreign key to `learning_path_courses`, on purpose: that table's keys cascade, and a step that is removed and added again must find its fact. The course key cascades because a course that holds learner attempts can be deleted only where the delete guards of step 7 let it through (the service role, and the organisation's cascade), and there its attempts cascade with it, so the fact leaves with the evidence behind it and adds no new refusal to a course delete; the path key refuses, as the attempts' own path key does. Append-only, in the form of step 14: trigger function `learning_path_step_completions_append_only()` (plpgsql; execute revoked from `public, anon, authenticated`) and trigger `learning_path_step_completions_no_mutation`, `before update or delete`, which lets the organisation, user and course cascades through by `pg_trigger_depth() > 1` as `0053:134-146` does. Foreign-key indexes by step 7's rule: `(organization_id)`, `(user_id)` and `(course_id)`; `path_id` is led by the unique key. Step 7's count of seventeen foreign-key indexes therefore reads twenty, and the new-table share thirteen, wherever the body gives it as a rule. The places, by name: step 7 ("That gives seventeen"); the creation order's item 2 ("Of step 7's seventeen", "the other ten", which reads thirteen, and "the three tables of item 3", which reads four); step 19's after-check A23 ("the seventeen foreign-key indexes of step 7 are present"); and step 63's rehearsal case ("Indexes: the seventeen of step 7 exist"), which asserts twenty. The body is not edited, so the executor reads twenty at each of them. "Seventeen" in the Review section, the round summaries and the phase tables is the history of earlier rounds and stays as written. It holds ids, a boolean and a time; nothing else about a learner.
    - **O1-b. RLS and grants, written out in the file.** `alter table public.learning_path_step_completions enable row level security;` one select policy, the form of `learning_path_completions`: the learner's own rows (`user_id = auth.uid()`) or `is_org_admin(organization_id)`; no insert, update or delete policy. `revoke all on public.learning_path_step_completions from public, anon, authenticated;` and `grant select on public.learning_path_step_completions to authenticated, service_role;`. `authenticated` needs the select because `v_path_step_status` is `security_invoker` and reads the table; without it a learner's read of the path view raises 42501. No API role is granted insert: the only writer is the `security definer` function of step 12, as the table's owner. `node scripts/check-migration-grants.mjs` must pass with the table in the file.
    - **O1-c. The writer: step 12 only, in the completion branch.** In step 12.3, for a path row that is official, before the path completion check of step 14 and in the same transaction: `insert into public.learning_path_step_completions (organization_id, path_id, course_id, user_id, attempt_id, rule_pass_required) select ... from public.v_path_step_status where path_id = P and user_id = U and passed_by_rule and done_recorded_at is null on conflict (path_id, user_id, course_id) do nothing`. So a fact is written the first time the writer runs while the step satisfies the rule in force at that moment, and never again: the conflict target makes a second call a no-op, and the trigger refuses any rewrite. The select covers every current step of this learner on this path, not only the row's own step, so a step that came to satisfy its rule without a completion of its own (a rule relaxed earlier, or a step finished before 0107) is recorded by the learner's next official completion anywhere in the path. The writer reads the rule from the view's `passed_by_rule` and holds no second copy of it (step 14's principle). The path completion check then reads `done` in a statement that starts after the insert, so it sees the new facts. The writer's answer (step 12.4) keeps its keys and gains two, `passed_by_rule` and `done_recorded_at`, set for a path row only and null otherwise, as `passed_ever` is (O1-e): all four, `done`, `passed_ever` and the two new ones, are read from the view after the insert. The sweep of step 16 re-runs the branch and is covered by the same conflict target. Nothing else writes the table: no route, no backfill, and no trigger on the step list or on `courses` unless the owner answers O3 with yes.
    - **O1-d. How the views read it.** `v_path_step_status` left-joins the table on (`path_id`, `user_id`, `course_id`) and has: `passed_by_rule` = N12's expression, unchanged; `done_recorded_at` = the fact's `done_at`, null when none; `passed_ever` = `passed_by_rule or done_recorded_at is not null`; `done = passed_ever`, as step 44 says. The computed half is always read, so a rule relaxed later helps at once, with no writer call. The stored half makes a rule tightened later harmless. `latest_result`, `latest_score`, `official_attempt_id`, `retake_live` and `open_attempt` do not read the fact: they show the standing official result under the rule as it stands, so nothing is hidden. `failed` keeps N10's formula on the new `passed_ever`, so a step with a fact is never failed. Everything that reads `done` or `passed_ever` from SQL follows without a change of its own (the readers that compute it in TypeScript, Continue among them, need the fact rows as an input; O1-k lists every reader and its source): the strict lock, `steps_done`, `next_step` and `next_course_id`, `v_path_progress.status` (N11's order is unchanged), `launch_context`'s `path.steps[].done` (N15's tree is unchanged), the writer's answer, the CRM `steps[]` and the webhook, and the path matviews of 0109. `learning_path_completions` is unchanged: its row is inserted when every step reads `done`, which now includes the facts, and it is never undone. `path_score` is unchanged (the latest official scores).
    - **O1-e. What `passed_ever` now means.** Step 44's sentence is read as: some official attempt or granted retake satisfied the step's resolved rule as it stood when the writer recorded it, or satisfies it as it stands now. A step completed without a pass, and tightened afterwards, therefore reads `passed_ever` true with `passed_by_rule` false and `done_recorded_at` set, and its `latest_result` reads as the standing result does under today's rule. So `passed_ever` can be true for a learner who never passed, which it could not be when A1 named it and gave it the words "passed earlier". Decided here, class (a), because it is additive, renames nothing and follows step 44's "so nothing is hidden": wherever `passed_ever` leaves the database as data, `passed_by_rule` (boolean) and `done_recorded_at` (a time or null) go beside it, under those names. That is the writer's answer (O1-c), the webhook's path-context messages (step 31), the CRM `steps[]` (step 55, in every contract version that carries `passed_ever`), and the path CSV. A reader of the API tells the case by them: `passed_ever` true with `passed_by_rule` false is a step that counts as done by its stored fact and does not satisfy today's rule. The addendum to the tech team (step 57) and `docs/CRM_INTEGRATION.md` explain the three fields together in phase 9a; `webhook-context.test.ts` and the CRM harness assert the two fields wherever they assert `passed_ever`. Not decided here: the words shown to people for that case, because "passed earlier" is the owner's A1. That is question O4 below. The rule holds inside one path; the Individual product and journey days are untouched (step 44's last sentence; journeys keep step 13's rule).
    - **O1-f. Place in the creation order, the checks and the reverse script.** Creation order, item 3: after `learning_path_completions` and before `completion_notices`, with its trigger function before its trigger. Item 4's last sentence reads: the two path views read the terms view, `learning_path_completions` and `learning_path_step_completions`. The writer is plpgsql (item 6), so it needs no place of its own. The file uses nothing newer than Postgres 17 for it (`gen_random_uuid()` is core). Step 19: the table and its trigger function join the privilege matrix (a real 42501 as `anon`; as `authenticated`, another organisation's rows read 0; an insert, update or delete as `authenticated` raises 42501); A23's list gains "RLS on with a policy, the append-only trigger present, the three foreign-key indexes present"; the after-check on `v_path_step_status`'s column list names the two new columns. Reverse script (step 61), part (3): `learning_path_step_completions` is dropped after `v_path_step_status` (the view reads it) and beside `learning_path_completions`, then its trigger function, each with `if exists`. The facts go with the table; the reverse is for staging only, which is test data.
    - **O1-g. Rehearsal cases added to step 63**, each on synthetic rows, each asserting the view row, `v_path_progress` and `launch_context`'s `steps[].done`: (1) *tighten after done keeps done.* Step 1 without a required pass; the learner completes it without passing; the writer writes the fact; an update sets the step's `pass_required` true. Step 1 reads `passed_by_rule` false, `done_recorded_at` set, `passed_ever` and `done` true, `failed` false, `retake_live` false, and `latest_result` and `latest_score` exactly as the same rows read without the fact row (the fixture pins those literals from the standing-result rule; the fact must not change them); `next_step` is 2 and step 2 is unlocked; completing the remaining steps inserts the path completion. (2) The same with the step's `pass_required` null and `courses.pass_required` set true: a rule tightened at the course protects the same way. (3) *Tighten before done applies.* The rule is set first; the learner completes without passing; no fact is written, the step is not done and step 2 stays locked in a strict path. (4) *Relax helps at once.* From (3), the rule is set false: the step reads done with no fact row and no writer call; the learner's next official completion in the path writes the fact for it; a second tightening then keeps it done. (5) *Idempotent.* A second writer call for the same row (cmi5's `completed`, then `passed`; the sweep) leaves one row with the same `done_at` and `rule_pass_required`; a direct update or delete as the service role raises; the organisation and user deletes of N19 pass over a fact row; a second paste of 0107 leaves the rows as they are. (6) A completed path whose step is tightened stays completed, and `steps_done` does not fall. (7) A step removed from the path and added again reads its fact. (8) The limit of O1-h (2), pinned so that a later change to it is deliberate: relax, no completion, tighten again reads not done. (9) *The course cascade passes.* With the service-role claim, a delete of a course that holds a path attempt and its fact row succeeds, and the attempt and the fact are both gone (the fact through the course key, let through by the trigger's depth test); the control: with a session claim that is not the service role the same delete raises `learner_history_exists` (step 7) and the fact row is still in place. (10) *A lone path delete is refused.* A fact row seeded alone, with no attempt of the path, so that the fact's own key is what refuses: a delete of the `learning_paths` row raises 23503 and the path and the fact stay. This adds no refusal a real path does not already have, because a fact is written only for a step with an official path attempt and that attempt's own path key refuses the same delete; when the course is deleted first, the attempt and the fact leave together (case (9)). The control: a path that holds only a path test run's preview rows holds no fact row (the writer writes none for a preview row), and after the product-delete purge of step 63's case (12) it deletes. (11) *A failed granted retake on a step done by its fact.* From (1): a path grant opens a retake on step 1 and the retake fails. While it is live the step reads `retake_live` true and the path `in_progress` (N11); after it fails the step reads `passed_by_rule` false, `passed_ever` and `done` true, `failed` false and `latest_result` failed, step 2 stays unlocked, and a stored path completion is unchanged. This is the SQL half of O1-k's Continue case.
    - **O1-h. Limits, disclosed.** (1) A step finished before 0107 has no fact until the learner's next official completion in that path; until then it is judged by the rule as it stands. Staging is test data and production has no learners. (2) The writer runs on completions, not on rule changes. If a rule is relaxed, the learner completes nothing more in that path, and the rule is tightened again, the step has no fact and reads not done again. Closing it needs a second writer that records a fact just before a rule changes. That is a new structural object beyond option (ii) as it was put to the owner, which named the writer of step 12 only, so it is not decided here: it is question **O3**, put to the owner as an item of its own after O1-k, with its design, its recommended answer and its price. (3) If an admin tightens the rule between the learner's completion and the writer's call, the rule at the writer's call decides, and a learner who completed the step without a pass reads not done. From phase 3 the routes call the writer in the request and the window is a failed call's retry by the sweep, two minutes or more. Until phase 3 serves on staging only the sweep calls the writer, nightly, so there a rule tightened on the day of a completion locks the step again; that is a real breach of the owner's sentence on staging, on test data, and not a rare race. In each of (1), (2) and (3) the learner's step reads not done after a later tightening, which the owner's sentence ("should remain completed even if the Admin later changes the step") read literally does not allow. O3 closes all three, not only (1) and (2): its writer reads the attempts through the view at the moment of the change, so it does not depend on the writer of step 12 having run. Until the owner answers O3, 0107 is built to this item and falls short of the sentence in these three cases; that is said to the owner with O3. (4) A fact proves the step was done under some rule, not that the learner passed; `rule_pass_required` and the standing result say which.
    - **O1-i. The TypeScript twin and parity.** `pathProgress()` (`lib/paths/progress.ts`, step 43) takes the learner's fact rows for the path as an input and answers `passedByRule`, `doneRecordedAt` and `passedEver = passedByRule || doneRecordedAt !== null` per step; `done`, the lock and the totals follow as in SQL. No TypeScript code writes the table. The fact rows are a required argument with no default, as `courseDays` is in N18, so a caller cannot leave them out and read every step by today's rule without a type error. **Who loads them:** one server-only reader, `readPathStepFacts(client, userId, pathIds)` in a new file `lib/paths/facts-read.ts`, beside `progress.ts` in the folder step 3 already adds (recorded here as a new file, as `CLAUDE.md` requires). It makes one read of `learning_path_step_completions` (`path_id`, `course_id`, `done_at`, `rule_pass_required`) for the learner and the given paths, with the caller's own client (the select policy of O1-b gives a learner their own rows), and answers `{ok: false}` on any error, as `readContextResult` does. A failed read is never an empty list: an empty list would silently lock a step again, so the caller treats `{ok: false}` as it treats a failed attempts read. The callers and their added reads, each an estimate until it is measured: the path page, one read for its path; the dashboard, one read for all of the learner's paths together, never one per path; Continue, none in the common case and one at most (O1-k); the launch, none (`launch_context` reads the view inside its one call); reports, the CSV, the CRM, the webhook and the Overview, none (they read the views or the matviews). Against step 70: the launch's target of 40 is untouched; Continue's added read joins the measured count that is phase 2's gate, and the path page and the dashboard are counted on a local build in the same phase 2 pull request and recorded with it. A phase P test run passes an empty list, on purpose: the writer writes no fact for a preview row, and a tester's own real facts must not unlock steps inside a test run; `preview-run-state.test.ts` gains that case. Phase 1 parity, in `parity-path.cases.json` (N16; the fixture rows gain the table, in database column names): a static case "a fact row, the step now pass-required, the standing result completed and not passed" with the complete `expect` of O1-g (1), run on both sides; a static case "no fact, rule relaxed" (done on both sides); the limit case of O1-h (2); and one case marked `writer`, which calls `record_learning_activity` on a step that satisfies its rule and compares its `done` and `passed_ever` with the view and expects the fact row. A fourth static case, run on both sides: "a fact row, the step now pass-required, then a completed granted retake that failed", with the complete `expect` of O1-g (11). `path-progress.test.ts` (step 62) gains the same four readings.
    - **O1-j. Price (a build-note figure; step 72's table and totals are not edited).** About **2.25 executor days**, an estimate: 2 in phase 1 and a quarter in phase 2. (This item first said 1.75, all in phase 1. The independent check added three rehearsal cases, a parity case and the loader. The base is unchanged: phase 1 is 19.5 days after the round 7 change to the figures, and phase 2 is 18.75.) Phase 1: the table with its policy, grants, trigger and indexes, a quarter day; the two view columns, the writer's insert and the two keys of its answer, a quarter day; the privilege matrix, the after-checks and the reverse script, a quarter day; the eleven rehearsal cases of O1-g, three quarters of a day; the twin, the four parity cases and the unit test, half a day. Phase 2: `readPathStepFacts`, its three callers, Continue's rule with its unit cases (O1-k) and the two measured counts, a quarter day. Phase 1 therefore reads 19.5 + 2 = **21.5 days** as built (plan for about 32 by the record's factor of one and a half) and phase 2 reads 18.75 + 0.25 = **19 days** (plan for about 28.5). The round 7 totals grow by the same 2.25: phases 0 to 9b with P, 95.5 becomes 97.75 (plan for about 147); production's first release, 86 becomes 88.25 (plan for about 132); the longest chain, 1, 2, 7, 9a, 55.25 becomes 57.5 (plan for about 86). The two added fields of O1-e in the webhook, the CRM `steps[]`, the CSV and their documents are a few lines in each of the phases that build those readers and are not priced apart. Not built and not in these figures: the second writer, question O3, about one day more in phase 1 if the owner says yes (priced in O3; it was first given here as three quarters of a day, before the delete trigger and the cascade guard were added). O4 changes a label and has no price of its own.
    - **O1-k. Where every reader takes a path step's done and `passed_ever` (a rule; the path analogue of N18's A4).** A path step's `done`, `passed_ever` and `failed` come from exactly one of three sources: `v_path_step_status` (or a view or matview built on it); `launch_context`, which reads that view; or `pathProgress()` fed with the learner's fact rows (O1-i). They never come from `contextResult.done`, `officialDone` or `computeScoring`: those give the standing result of the context under today's rule, which is `latest_result`, and know neither an earlier pass (D14) nor a stored fact. The readers, each with its source:

      | Reader | Source |
      |---|---|
      | The launch's strict lock (step 22) | `launch_context`, `path.steps[].done` |
      | The path completion insert, the writer's answer and, from it, the webhook's path messages (steps 12, 14, 31) | the view, read after the fact insert (O1-c) |
      | `steps_done`, `next_step`, `v_path_progress.status`, the path matviews of 0109, the Overview | the view |
      | Path Reports, Learners, the CSV, the CRM `steps[]`, the tenant LRS path statements (steps 43, 49, 55) | the view or its matviews; where one of them computes in TypeScript it calls `pathProgress()` with the fact rows, loaded for its learners in one read |
      | The path page and the dashboard path tile (phase 2) | `pathProgress()` with `readPathStepFacts` |
      | **Continue (step 27)** | `pathProgress()` with `readPathStepFacts`, as below |
      | A phase P path test run | `pathProgress()` over the run's preview rows with an empty fact list (O1-i) |

      **Step 27, made exact.** Today the page computes `done = officialDone(scoring, passRequired)` (`continue/page.tsx:164`) and `continueTarget` sends `stored && !done` to `?failed=1` (`lib/learner/continue.ts:129`). Under step 27 the wait still reads through `readContextResult` in `path:P`, and that read decides only two things: whether the result is stored and whether its verdict is pending. The step's done is not `contextResult`'s `done`. It is the per-step answer of `pathProgress()` for the `from` step, fed with the in-context rows of that same read, the step's resolved rule and the step's fact row or null. The fact row is read once, through `readPathStepFacts`, after the wait has ended on a stored result, and only when the step is not `passedByRule` on those rows, because a fact can change the answer only then; so a learner who has just satisfied the rule costs no added read. If that read answers `{ok: false}` the page takes its read-error exit, never `?failed=1`. Step 27's sentence "whose step is `passed_ever` continues to the next step" is read with this source. The failure it prevents: a step done by a stored fact, then a granted retake on it that fails; judged from `contextResult` alone the learner would land on `?failed=1` with the next step unlocked on the path page beside it. Proof, in the phase 2 pull request: `tests/unit/continue.test.ts` gains (a) a step done by its fact whose granted retake failed goes to the next launch; (b) the same on the last step goes to `?end=1`; (c) the control, no fact and the rule never satisfied, goes to `?failed=1`; (d) a failed facts read does not go to `?failed=1`. The parity case of O1-i and rehearsal case (11) of O1-g prove the same rows on the SQL side, and the continue lifecycle spec (spec 63) gains case (a) on staging after phase 2 serves.

  - **O3. Open: should a rule change itself record the steps that are done at that moment? (class (b))** The owner's answer to O1 says a completed step "should remain completed even if the Admin later changes the step". As 0107 is designed above, the stored fact is written only when the learner next completes something in the path. So there are three cases where a completed step still reads not done after an admin makes its rule stricter (O1-h (1) to (3)): the step was finished before 0107; the rule was relaxed and tightened again with no completion in between; and the rule was tightened before the writer had processed the completion (on staging before phase 3 that is any tightening on the day of the completion). Production has no learners yet and staging is test data, so no real learner is affected today; from the first production learner the second and third cases can happen.
    - **(yes) Build the second writer in 0107.** One trigger function, `learning_path_step_facts_before_rule_change()` (plpgsql, `security definer` with a pinned `search_path`, because the admin who changes the rule is `authenticated` and holds no insert on the table; execute revoked from `public, anon, authenticated`), bound three times: `before update of pass_required on learning_path_courses`, for each row, when the old and new values differ; `before delete on learning_path_courses`, for each row; and `before update of pass_required on courses`, for each row, when the values differ. Each inserts a fact, `on conflict do nothing`, for every learner whose row of `v_path_step_status` for the affected step (for a course, every path step that holds the course and has no rule of its own) reads `passed_by_rule` with no fact yet. It fires before the change, so the view still reads the old rule and the old step list. It writes on any change of the rule, not only a tightening, so it holds no copy of the rule. It returns without writing when `pg_trigger_depth() > 1`: inside a path, course or organisation cascade it would insert facts for rows that are being removed, and a fact inserted during a path delete would refuse that delete by its own `no action` key. The delete trigger is there because a step that is removed and added again with no rule of its own can come back under a stricter course rule; the fact recorded at the removal is found again by the key (O1-a). An insert trigger is not built: before the insert there is no step and no view row, so there is nothing to record that the delete trigger or the writer of step 12 did not already have the chance to record; the one case it would serve, a step removed before 0107 and added again after it, is O1-h (1)'s history on staging. Today's step editor fits this: it removes one step with a direct delete and reorders with updates of `step_number` only (`app/api/learning-paths/[id]/courses/route.ts:248-306`), which the update trigger's column list and `when` clause skip. If the trigger fails, the admin's rule change fails with it and nothing is half done. Cost to the admin's request: one set-based insert, of at most one row per learner per affected step; not measured. Creation order: after the path views (item 4), because the function's first run needs them. Reverse script: the three triggers and the function are dropped before the views. Rehearsal cases: a tightening on the step, and on the course, records facts first and the step stays done; O1-g (8) is turned round and reads done; a completion the writer has not processed is recorded at the tightening; a step with no fact from before 0107 is recorded at the tightening; a step removed and added again under a stricter course rule reads done; a reorder writes nothing; a path delete, a course delete with the service-role claim and an organisation delete write nothing and behave as in O1-g (5), (9) and (10); as an `authenticated` admin the rule change succeeds and a direct insert into the table still raises 42501.
    - **(no) Keep the one writer.** No further object. The three cases stay as disclosed limits, and O1-g (8) pins the second.

    **Recommended answer: yes.** It is the only form in which the owner's sentence holds whatever the order of changes, and it closes all three cases. Price: about **1 executor day** more in phase 1, an estimate (the function and its three triggers, a quarter day; the privilege matrix, the creation order and the reverse script, a quarter day; the rehearsal cases, half a day), so phase 1 would read 22.5 days (plan for about 34). Until the owner answers, 0107 is built without it and falls short of the owner's sentence in the three cases above. The answer is wanted before the staging paste of 0107; if it comes later, the triggers can be added by a second paste of 0107 before 0109, since the file is written to be pasted twice.

  - **O4. Open: the words shown for a step that is done without a pass. (class (b))** A1 names the field `passed_ever` and shows it to people as "passed earlier". Under option (ii) a step can be done by its stored fact although the learner never passed it: completed while no pass was required, then the rule was tightened. For that learner "passed earlier" is untrue. The data already tells the two apart (`passed_by_rule`, `done_recorded_at`, O1-e); the question is only the label on the path page, the reports and the CSV header. Options: **(i)** keep "passed earlier" for every such step; **(ii)** show "passed earlier" only when an attempt of the step was in fact passed, and "completed under the earlier rule" when the step is done by its fact without a pass. **Recommended answer: (ii)**, because the label is then true in both cases and an admin reading a report is not told a learner passed who did not. The field name `passed_ever` stays either way. This does not hold up 0107 or phase 1; it is wanted before the path page of phase 2 is built, and until then no page shows the case.

- **Note 2026-11-14 (phase 1 as built).** How phase 1 was built on the branch `feat/adr-0024-p1-core`, what departs from the record, and what is still open. The body of the record and the note of 2026-11-13 are not edited; where an item below corrects a sentence, this note is the current text. Classes as in the note above: **(a)** decided here by the architect, **(b)** touches an owner answer and is put to the owner. The builders' deviation list holds 116 lines, every one class (a); it is summarised by area below, not copied. Nothing here has been pasted on staging or production: every database result is from the rehearsal on PGlite 0.5.10 (Postgres 18.6), and the Supabase CLI was not used.

  **What was built**

  - **SQL.** `supabase/migrations/0107_learning_contexts.sql` (about 7,600 lines; 35 functions; before-checks Q1 to Q10; after-checks A1 to A24), `supabase/migrations/0108_drop_context_derive.sql` and `ops/0107-reverse.sql` (staging only). O1 is built as option (ii), with one writer; O3's second writer is not built.
  - **TypeScript.** `lib/scoring/context.ts` (`contextResult`, `contextKeyOf`, `contextKindOfKey`, `passedInWindow`), `lib/scoring/context-read.ts` (`readContextResult`, `individualContextOnly`), `lib/paths/progress.ts` (`pathProgress`, `pathStepStatus`), `lib/attempts/launch-context.ts` (`resolveLaunchContext`, without its preview and test-run rows), `todayStrAt` in `lib/journey/journey.ts`, and the sweep's failure reporting in `app/api/cron/refresh-report-views/route.ts`. The resolver is not wired into the launch page; that is phase 2.
  - **Not built, as the record places them.** 0109, its reverse script and its scenario (phase 7); `lib/paths/facts-read.ts` (phase 2); the tier guard's check (ii-b) and `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` (phase 2); the TypeScript journey parity (N17, phase 2).

  **Measured results**

  - **Rehearsal:** PASS, 5,290 checks, 0 failed, 31 scenarios. Seven scenarios are new for 0107: the two paste orders (each pastes 0107 twice), three refusals (Q8, Q2, 0106 missing), `0107-then-0108`, and `0107-0108-reverse-then-0107`.
  - **Parity (N16), cases that agree on both sides:** official 17, path 27, journey 17, launch 25. The SQL side runs ten times in one rehearsal (after each paste, after 0108 and after the reverse). The journey pair is proven on the SQL side only (N17).
  - **Unit tests:** 79 of 79 files pass. `npx tsc --noEmit` is clean, eslint is clean on the changed files, and `scripts/check-migration-grants.mjs` passes (53 relations).
  - **An independent assertion file** (`assert-0107-independent.sql`, written from the record without opening the migration, as its header says) found three defects, all in itself and none in 0107. Five mutations of 0107 were each caught by a labelled check.
  - **The reverse script:** 24 mutations, 23 caught; the one not caught changed a comment that Postgres does not keep. After the reverse the catalog of `public` equals the catalog before 0107 object by object (2,623 objects), except three named privilege rows.
  - **Not proven by these figures:** behaviour on Postgres 17 (staging reads 17.6; N3's guards stay); anything that needs two connections (lock order is asserted on the stored function bodies; the purge's window between its read and its delete is disclosed, not shown); the rulings A1, A4 and A5, the user-delete guard and the hardening items H1 to H5 below, which are decided here and are not built yet.

  **Departures from the record, by area (all class (a))**

  - **Header, prerequisites and before-checks (step 4).** The prerequisite block names every object the file reads, which is more than the record lists. Q8's first-paste marker is a row, not a count, so step 74's "a count above 0 stops" is not tripped by a first paste. Q9's predicates are written out in the header. Two Q4 row counts are excepted from "0 on production": `mv_learner_metrics` holds one row per member and `mv_leaderboard` one per `user_gamification` row, whatever the activity.
  - **Columns, keys and triggers (steps 5 to 8, 79, 91).** The deferred key on `journey_day_progress.day_id` (N1) is added as a named constraint after the last write to the table, because written inline it fails with 55006 in the same transaction. Every revoke on a new table names `service_role` as well, so the role holds exactly the grants the record states. `credit_kind`'s "not a journey row" is read as "not a journey row of the credit's own enrolment" (Q7's reading). The `pre_0107` marker is set on exactly the SCORM 1.2 rows the `side_effects` backfill stamps. The derive trigger keeps a `journey_day_id` that the insert gives. The `day_ids` trigger is `security invoker`. Names the record leaves open are fixed in the file.
  - **New tables (steps 14, 15, 79, O1-a, O1-b).** Columns, policies, constraint and index names the record leaves open are fixed. The three append-only trigger functions take the `xp_events` form with the file's rules (pinned `search_path`, P0001, a fixed message).
  - **Views (steps 9, 18).** New columns are appended, so each 0103 column list stays a prefix. The summary groups by (course, learner, `context_key`) and reads `journey_day` from the newest row. The due rule: the learner's own row wins even when its date is null (decision A9's two readers do the same). The terms views write the enrolment arms out again with their dates, release gates kept. Disclosed and not changed: a learner's own session reads no terms or path progress row that only a Custom Group reaches (the 0105 behaviour; see "Phase 2 must take over").
  - **Functions (steps 9 to 17, 33, 35, 47, 81, 92 to 95).** "Satisfies" is the credit's rule since 0081 (under `pass_required` a pass; otherwise completed or passed). A day number listed twice in one version is one day. `consume_grant` computes the context from the tags and matches the grant at its lock, so a differing day id answers `unavailable` there. `journey_save_days` and `journey_repin_enrollments` have their payloads, refusals, gate and seven counts written out. The credit of a relaxed rule is written only where the pinned rule was not met, and `late` uses one expression in both writers of an attempt credit. The XP core gains a three-argument form and the writer a two-argument form, so the sweep re-runs the completion branch without moving a streak. The writer's `done` is read per context (a path step from the view after the fact insert, a journey day from its credit row, an Individual row from the official attempt). A path with no step never completes. The sweep answers ids and SQLSTATEs only. The purge raises 40001 when the selection changed and 22023 for every other refusal. `launch_context`'s edges beyond N15 are fixed. `gamification_close_month`'s `highest_scorer` averages one official result per Individual or path context completed in the month, where the 0073 body averaged every scored-window attempt; this was flagged for the architect and is confirmed here, by step 9's sentence that `official_attempt_in_context` replaces the ad-hoc chain in `gamification_close_month` (D8 and D9 speak of points and badges, not of the monthly recognitions).
  - **0108 and the reverse (steps 20, 61).** 0108 must be pasted again after any later paste of 0107. The reverse also drops the three-argument XP core and both forms of the writer, refuses on two more row classes that would stop it half way, restores comments and privileges so the catalog compares equal, and ends with its own after-check block. Its header says that dropping a foreign key locks the parent tables against reads until commit.
  - **Rehearsal shape (step 63).** The 0107 assertions are split by section into several files, with seed and repair files per refusal. The runner gains the step kinds `cases`, `script` and `fingerprint` and the static rules `generatedStored` (3 on 0107) and `functionList` (35). The second-paste proof runs before the refresh, because the refresh now runs the sweep.
  - **TypeScript (steps 22, 41, 43, 49, 72, 81).** `pathProgress()` answers the views' column names in snake_case. `contextResult` throws on a grant without a context key. The resolver's precedence, which step 22 leaves open, is: conflict; preview (refused until phases 2 and P build its rows); membership; course; then the path or journey row, with resume before review before open; then, with no product parameter, Individual, "Everyone", holders, upcoming. `readContextResult` falls back to today's undivided read on a database without 0107. From the paste, the course card, the Reports tab, the course learners table and its CSV read the Individual context only, through one helper. The refresh route answers 500 only on the sweep entry's own error. The allow-list guard of step 41 is built: `computeScoring` is called by `contextResult` only, and today's 20 other callers stand on the dated list `PENDING_0024`.
  - **Disclosed for the staging paste window (step 60), none of them a change:** until phases 2 and 3 serve, the old routes run the new function bodies; on staging a grant used before 0107 can name a retake in another context, which the Individual readers leave out without an error.

  **Rulings of 2026-11-14**

  - **A1. `launch_context`, `holders.journey_days`: only journeys of active programs are holders. (a)** As built, the arm reads the enrolment's status and not the program's `is_active`, so a launch with no product parameter can redirect into a journey that step 22's journey row then refuses. The paths arm already keeps active paths only, and today's access check drops an active enrolment of an inactive program. Change: the arm joins `journey_programs` and keeps `is_active`. N15's phrase "required days of the learner's active enrolments" reads "of the learner's active enrolments on active programs". Completed enrolments are not holders either way, so this does not depend on O5. If 0107 is already on staging when the change lands, 0107 is pasted again before 0109, and 0108 after it.
  - **A2. A completed enrolment's review when its program is inactive. (b); it is question O5 below.** Step 22 reads "the program is active" for every journey launch, and the resolver is built to that. Today's access check admits the review on purpose. The record does not list the loss of that review as an intended change, and no owner answer speaks of an inactive program. Not decided here.
  - **A3. An "Everyone" path learner with no enrolment row, launching a later step. (a), built in phase 2.** `launch_context` reads each step's `done` from `v_path_step_status`, which holds enrolled learners only, so for this learner every earlier step reads not done and the resolver answers the lock before the self-enrolment of step 24 can run. Ruling: for a path whose learner is not enrolled and whose visibility is "Everyone", the resolver answers "enrol first" before it reads the strict order; the page inserts the row (step 24), calls `launch_context` once more and resolves again. The view's grain is not widened. The second call is counted against step 70's target of 40.
  - **A4. The gamification refresh route and the sweep's error. (a)** `refresh_gamification_views()` also runs the sweep (step 16), and its route runs every 15 minutes, so it is the sweep's most frequent caller. Step 81 names the report route only, but its reason ("a failed sweep of the fail-closed writer would pass unseen") holds for both. `app/api/cron/gamification-refresh/route.ts` therefore follows the same rule: `ok` false and 500 with `error: "completion_sweep: <message>"` when the entry carries its own error; 200 for rows that failed inside the sweep, for a view entry's error and for an answer without the entry. The held-webhook sweep of ADR 0015 step 5 still runs in every case, stays in the body and never decides `ok`. The effect is that the 15-minute job reads red while the sweep itself is broken, which is the point.
  - **A5. Two functions named `pathProgress`. (a)** `lib/paths/progress.ts` is the path rule (step 43). The older roll-up in `lib/courses/progress-view.ts` still serves the admin Learning Paths page, the Analytics page and the path learners CSV; it is retired by the pull request that moves the last of those three to the views or to the new function with the fact rows (O1-k's "Path Reports, Learners, the CSV" row). Until then: the old function is marked deprecated with a pointer to step 43; a unit test pins its importers to exactly those three files on a dated list, which that pull request empties before deleting the function; and no file imports both. Nothing is renamed in phase 1.
  - **A user delete can be refused (23503). (a)** Step 7 says the user cascade still works under the `no action` key on `journey_enrollment_id`. That holds only when the attempt's enrolment belongs to the attempt's learner: if learner A's attempt names learner B's enrolment, deleting B removes the enrolment while A's attempt stays, and the delete is refused. Step 6 already states the half of this that concerns the enrolment (an enrolment named by a `legacy_role` row's old tag cannot be deleted); it does not say that the row can belong to another user. No application writer creates such a row (the launch reads the enrolment by the learner's id, and `launch_context` does the same), but the database does not forbid it, and `consume_grant` locks the enrolment by its id alone. Ruling, both in 0107: (1) a permanent `before insert` trigger on `course_attempts`, `course_attempts_context_owner`, refuses a row whose `journey_enrollment_id` names an enrolment that is not of the row's own `user_id` and `organization_id`, with P0001 and a fixed message in N7's form; the immutability trigger already keeps the tag from changing afterwards. (2) `consume_grant` reads the enrolment with the learner's id and answers `unavailable` when it finds none. Rows from before 0107 are not touched (principle 5): a before-check counts them without refusing, and a user delete that meets one is still refused. That residue is expected on staging's test data only; production has no learner rows.
  - **Hardening H1 to H5 (a).** H1: the parity loader refuses a case that only one side runs, except those on an allow-list, and the both-sided count of each pair is pinned in `context-parity.test.ts` and in the SQL step. The counts are pinned after A1 and H5 change the launch pair. H2: a launch case's SQL call parameters and its resolver parameters must agree. H3: the step 41 guard also refuses an aliased or namespace import of `lib/scoring/policy` or `lib/scoring/status` outside the allow-list, and asserts that `PENDING_0024` holds at most 20 files. H4: `readContextResult` answers a bad-key error for a key that is not in the generated-key form, where today it answers not started. H5: the two self-enrol launch cases join `parity-launch.cases.json`.

  **To the owner (class (b))**

  - **O3 and O4 stand as written in the note of 2026-11-13; neither is answered.** O3 (should a rule change itself record the steps that are done at that moment; recommended: yes, about one executor day): 0107 is built without the second writer and falls short of the owner's O1 sentence in the three cases of O1-h. The answer is wanted before the staging paste; if it comes later the triggers go in by a second paste of 0107 before 0109. O4 (the words for a step that is done without a pass; recommended: option (ii)) is wanted before the path page of phase 2.
  - **O5. Open: may a learner who completed a journey still review it after the program is made inactive? (class (b))** Today they can: the access check lets a completed enrolment open its journey modules for review while the program is inactive, and stops only learners who are still in progress. Step 22 as written refuses every journey launch on an inactive program, and the phase 1 resolver follows it. No learner is affected yet, because the resolver is not wired until phase 2. Options: **(i)** keep today's behaviour: a completed enrolment keeps the review of its credited days, and the resume of an attempt that was open when a publish completed it, on an inactive program; it never gets a new official attempt, active program or not. **(ii)** follow step 22's wording: an inactive program refuses every launch, the completed learner's review included. **Recommended answer: (i).** Making a program inactive is meant to stop new work, the record's other rules on completed enrolments keep what is finished (the enrolment stays completed with its badge and certificate), and the record nowhere says this review is meant to end. Cost of either answer: none in 0107, because `launch_context` already returns the enrolment's status and the program's activity; (i) is a one-line change of the resolver and one launch parity case. The answer is wanted before the launch page of phase 2 is built.

  **Phase 2 must take over**

  - A3's wiring, with a launch parity case and the measured call count.
  - The TypeScript journey parity: the 17 journey cases stand on `context-parity.test.ts`'s dated pending list, and the phase 2 pull request empties it (N17).
  - `PENDING_0024`: each pull request that moves callers onto `contextResult` removes its files; the list never grows, and the last pull request asserts it empty (phases 2, 4, 6, 7 and 9a).
  - O5's answer in the resolver, and the access check of step 23 kept in step with it.
  - An "Everyone" course whose learner also holds a direct assignment that is not released yet: the resolver self-enrols at once, as today's access check admits such a course, but step 24's insert then answers "already enrolled" while the released enrolment view still reads not enrolled. Which wins is not settled. It is settled before the launch is wired, and it goes to the owner if D15 does not answer it.
  - The learner pages that read the terms views or `v_path_progress` on the learner's own session read no row that only a Custom Group reaches. Each such page reads through a server read that sees group membership, or the limit is put to the owner before the page ships.
  - The tier guard's check (ii-b) and `preview-isolation-sites.test.ts` with step 81's source scan (it gates phase P).
  - The 0109 probe list in part 1 of the reverse script is checked again when 0109 is written (phase 7).

  **Built 2026-11-14, after the text above** (commits dcda659 to fee8556 on the branch): the rulings A1, A4 and A5, the user-delete guard and H1 to H5, which "Not proven by these figures" lists as not built; and, after an independent check, `consume_grant` reads the enrolment with the grant's organisation as well as the learner's id (both forms, the wrapper with the organisation it reads at its grant lock; the second column the owner trigger compares, so that case answers `unavailable` where it raised P0001) and the step 41 guard also refuses a named import or re-export of `computeScoring` outside the allow-list. The figures above, measured again at fee8556: 36 functions (`functionList`: 36 created, 36 listed); rehearsal PASS, 5,292 checks, 0 failed, 31 scenarios; unit tests 80 of 80 files; parity cases on both sides: official 17, path 27, journey 17, launch 28; `scripts/check-migration-grants.mjs` passes (53 relations).
